\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{N,l}^{1,\mathbf{r}}=\left(\left(X_{l}^{1}({\mathbf{s}})\right)_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R},\mathbf{s}\neq\mathbf{r}}\right)^{T}~\text{and}~\mathbf{U}_{N,l}^{2,\mathbf{r}}=\left(\left(X_{l}^{1}({\mathbf{s}})\right)_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}},\left(X_{l}^{2}({\mathbf{s}})\right)_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R},\mathbf{s}\neq\mathbf{r}}\right)^{T},~\text{for $l=1,2$.}

(E.26)

Next, we define the following random vectors on the same probability space,

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{N,l}^{1,(1,\mathbf{r})}=\Bigl(\bigl(X_{l}^{1,(1,\mathbf{r})}(\mathbf{s})\bigr)_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R},\ \mathbf{s}\neq\mathbf{r}}\Bigr)^{T}~\text{and}\hskip 8.50012pt\mathbf{U}_{N,l}^{2,(2,\mathbf{r})}=\Bigl(\bigl(X_{l}^{1,(2,\mathbf{r})}(\mathbf{s})\bigr)_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}},\,\bigl(X_{l}^{2,(2,\mathbf{r})}(\mathbf{s})\bigr)_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R},\ \mathbf{s}\neq\mathbf{r}}\Bigr)^{T},

(E.27)

such that the following relation holds,

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{N,l}^{1,(1,\mathbf{r})}\overset{d}{=}\left[\mathbf{U}_{N,l}^{1,\mathbf{r}}~|~X_{l}^{1}({\mathbf{r}})=1\right]~\text{and}~\mathbf{U}_{N,l}^{2,(2,\mathbf{r})}\overset{d}{=}\left[\mathbf{U}_{N,l}^{2,\mathbf{r}}\,|X_{l}^{2}(\mathbf{r})=1\right].

(E.28)

We choose an index
\mathbf{I}_{i}\in{[N]}_{R}
for
i=1,2
, with the following probability distribution,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(\mathbf{I}_{i}=\mathbf{r}\right)=\frac{\mathbf{E}(X_{l}^{i}({\mathbf{r}}))}{\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,i})},\hskip 8.50012pt\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}~\text{and}~\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{P}\left(\mathbf{I}_{i}=\mathbf{r}\right)=1,

(E.29)

where the choice of
\mathbf{I}_{i}
,
i=1,2
, is independent of
\mathbf{U}_{N,l}^{j,\mathbf{r}}
and
\mathbf{U}_{N,l}^{j,(j,\mathbf{r})}
for all
j=1,2
, see (
E.27
). The size biased coupling of
\mathbf{U}_{N,l}
,
l=1,2
, (cf. (
E.25
)) is denoted as
\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}_{N,l}={\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}_{N,l}^{1},\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}_{N,l}^{2}\right)}^{T}
, and it is defined as

\begin{aligned} &\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}_{N,l}^{1}=\left(\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R},\mathbf{s}\neq\mathbf{I}_{1}}X_{l}^{1,(1,\mathbf{I}_{1})}({\mathbf{s}})+1\right)=\widetilde{U}^{1}_{N,l,1},\\
&\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}_{N,l}^{2}={\left(\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}X_{l}^{1,(2,\mathbf{I}_{2})}({\mathbf{s}}),\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R},\mathbf{s}\neq\mathbf{I}_{2}}X_{l}^{2,(2,\mathbf{I}_{2})}({\mathbf{s}})+1\right)}^{T}={\left(\widetilde{U}^{2}_{N,l,1},\widetilde{U}^{2}_{N,l,2}\right)}^{T},\end{aligned}\hskip 8.50012pt\text{for $l=1,2$.}

Firstly we show that
\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}_{N,l}={\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}_{N,l}^{1},\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}_{N,l}^{2}\right)}^{T}
(cf. (
Proof of Theorem
(a): Dense sampling.
)) is indeed a size biased coupling of
\mathbf{U}_{N,l}
, for
l=1,2
. Using (
E.28
) and (
E.29
), we note that for any suitable value of
u
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,1})\cdot\mathbf{P}\left(\widetilde{U}^{1}_{N,l,1}=u\right)=\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,1})\cdot\mathbf{P}\left(\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R},\mathbf{s}\neq\mathbf{I}_{1}}X_{l}^{1,(1,\mathbf{I}_{1})}({\mathbf{s}})+1=u\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{I}_{1}=\mathbf{r})\cdot\mathbf{P}\left(\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R},\mathbf{s}\neq\mathbf{r}}X_{l}^{1,(1,\mathbf{r})}({\mathbf{s}})+1=u\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{P}(X^{1}_{l}(\mathbf{r})=1)\cdot\mathbf{P}\left(\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}X_{l}^{1}({\mathbf{s}})-X^{1}_{l}(\mathbf{r})+1=u~|~X^{1}_{l}(\mathbf{r})=1\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{P}\left(\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}X_{l}^{1}({\mathbf{s}})=u,~X^{1}_{l}(\mathbf{r})=1\right)=\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{P}\left(U_{N,l,1}=u,~X^{1}_{l}(\mathbf{r})=1\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{E}\left(X^{1}_{l}(\mathbf{r})\cdot\mathbf{1}(U_{N,l,1}=u)\right)=\mathbf{E}\left(\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}X^{1}_{l}(\mathbf{r})\cdot\mathbf{1}(U_{N,l,1}=u)\right)=u\cdot\mathbf{P}\left(U_{N,l,1}=u\right)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow~\mathbf{P}\left(\widetilde{U}^{1}_{N,l,1}=u\right)=\frac{u}{\mathbf{E}\left(U_{N,l,1}\right)}\cdot\mathbf{P}\left(U_{N,l,1}=u\right).

(E.30)

A similar argument can be used to show that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left((\widetilde{U}^{2}_{N,l,1},\widetilde{U}^{2}_{N,l,2})^{T}=(u_{1},u_{2})^{T}\right)=\frac{u_{2}}{\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,2})}\cdot\mathbf{P}\left((U_{N,l,1},U_{N,l,2})^{T}=(u_{1},u_{2})^{T}\right).

(E.31)

This ensures that
\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}_{N,l}
is a size biased version of
\mathbf{U}_{N,l}
, for
l=1,2
. Now, define the random vectors
\mathbf{Z}_{N,l}={(Z_{N,l,1},\,Z_{N,l,2})}^{T}
, for
l=1,2
, where
Z_{N,l,1}\sim\mathrm{Poisson}\bigl(\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,1})\bigr)
and
Z_{N,l,2}\sim\mathrm{Poisson}\bigl(\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,2})\bigr)
, for
l=1,2
, and
Z_{N,l,1}
and
Z_{N,l,2}
are independent. We apply Theorem 2.6
Nualart and Zhang (2025)
(see Theorem
C.4
) to obtain the following upper bound on the Wasserstein distance
d_{W}
between the random vectors
\mathbf{U}_{N,l}
and
\mathbf{Z}_{N,l}
, for
l=1,2
,

\displaystyle d_{{W}}\left(\mathbf{U}_{N,l},~\mathbf{Z}_{N,l}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{2}\min\{1,E(U_{N,l,i})\}\cdot\mathbf{E}\left|\widetilde{U}_{N,l,i}^{i}-1-U_{N,l,i}\right|+2\cdot\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,2})\cdot\mathbf{E}\left|\widetilde{U}_{N,l,1}^{2}-U_{N,l,1}\right|

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{2}\min\{1,E(U_{N,l,i})\}\cdot B_{N,l,i}+2\cdot\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,2})\cdot B_{N,l,3},~\text{(say)},

(E.32)

where,
\widetilde{U}^{1}_{N,l,1},\widetilde{U}^{2}_{N,l,1}
and
\widetilde{U}^{2}_{N,l,1}
,
l=1,2
, are defined in (
Proof of Theorem
(a): Dense sampling.
). Using (
E.25
), (
Proof of Theorem
(a): Dense sampling.
) and (
E.29
), for
i=1,2
, we can write

\displaystyle B_{N,l,i}=\mathbf{E}\left|\widetilde{U}^{i}_{N,l,i}-1-U_{N,l,i}\right|=\mathbf{E}\left|\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R},\mathbf{s}\neq\mathbf{I}_{i}}X_{l}^{i,(i,\mathbf{I}_{i})}(\mathbf{s})-\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}X^{i}_{l}(\mathbf{s})\right|

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{P}\left(\mathbf{I}_{i}=\mathbf{r}\right)\mathbf{E}\left|\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R},\mathbf{s}\neq\mathbf{r}}\left(X^{i,(i,\mathbf{r})}_{l}(\mathbf{s})-X^{i}_{l}(\mathbf{s})\right)-X^{i}_{l}(\mathbf{r})\right|

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{P}\left(\mathbf{I}_{i}=\mathbf{r}\right)\mathbf{E}\left|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R},\mathbf{s}\neq\mathbf{r}\\
A(\mathbf{s})\cap A(\mathbf{r})\neq\varnothing\end{subarray}}\left(X^{i,(i,\mathbf{r})}_{l}(\mathbf{s})-X^{i}_{l}(\mathbf{s})\right)-X^{i}_{l}(\mathbf{r})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{P}\left(\mathbf{I}_{i}=\mathbf{r}\right)\mathbf{E}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R},\mathbf{s}\neq\mathbf{r}\\
A(\mathbf{s})\cap A(\mathbf{r})\neq\varnothing\end{subarray}}\left(X^{i,(i,\mathbf{r})}_{l}(\mathbf{s})+X^{i}_{l}(\mathbf{s})\right)+X^{i}_{l}(\mathbf{r})\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{P}\left(\mathbf{I}_{i}=\mathbf{r}\right)\mathbf{E}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R},\mathbf{s}\neq\mathbf{r}\\
A(\mathbf{s})\cap A(\mathbf{r})\neq\varnothing\end{subarray}}\left(X^{i,(i,\mathbf{r})}_{l}(\mathbf{s})-X^{i}_{l}(\mathbf{s})\right)+2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R},\mathbf{s}\neq\mathbf{r}\\
A(\mathbf{s})\cap A(\mathbf{r})\neq\varnothing\end{subarray}}X^{i}_{l}(\mathbf{s})+X^{i}_{l}(\mathbf{r})\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{P}\left(\mathbf{I}_{i}=\mathbf{r}\right)\mathbf{E}\left(\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R},\mathbf{s}\neq\mathbf{r}}\left(X^{i,(i,\mathbf{r})}_{l}(\mathbf{s})-X^{i}_{l}(\mathbf{s})\right)+2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R},\mathbf{s}\neq\mathbf{r}\\
A(\mathbf{s})\cap A(\mathbf{r})\neq\varnothing\end{subarray}}X^{i}_{l}(\mathbf{s})+X^{i}_{l}(\mathbf{r})\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{P}\left(\mathbf{I}_{i}=\mathbf{r}\right)\mathbf{E}\left(\left\{\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}X^{i,(i,\mathbf{r})}_{l}(\mathbf{s})-1-\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}X^{i}_{l}(\mathbf{s})\right\}+2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}\\
A(\mathbf{s})\cap A(\mathbf{r})\neq\varnothing\end{subarray}}X^{i}_{l}(\mathbf{s})\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\left(\widetilde{U}_{N,l,i}^{i}-1-U_{N,l,i}\right)+2\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{P}\left(\mathbf{I}_{i}=\mathbf{r}\right)\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in N(\mathbf{r})}\mathbf{E}\left(X^{i}_{l}(\mathbf{s})\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{i}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}x_{i}\mathbf{P}(\widetilde{U}_{N,l,i}^{i}=x_{i})-1-\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,i})+\frac{2}{\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,i})}\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in N(\mathbf{r})}\mathbf{E}(X_{l}^{i}(\mathbf{s}))\mathbf{E}(X_{l}^{i}(\mathbf{r}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{i}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}x_{i}\cdot\mathbf{P}(\widetilde{U}^{i}_{N,l,1}=x_{1},\ldots,\widehat{U}^{i}_{N,l,i-1}=x_{i-1},\widetilde{U}_{N,l,i}^{i}=x_{i})

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}-1-\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,i})+\frac{2}{\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,i})}\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in N(\mathbf{r})}\mathbf{E}(X_{l}^{i}(\mathbf{s}))\mathbf{E}(X_{l}^{i}(\mathbf{r}))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,i})}\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{i}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}x_{i}^{2}\cdot\mathbf{P}({U}_{N,l,1}=x_{1},\ldots,{U}_{N,l,i-1}=x_{i-1},{U}_{N,l,i}=x_{i})

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}-1-\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,i})+\frac{2}{\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,i})}\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in N(\mathbf{r})}\mathbf{E}(X_{l}^{i}(\mathbf{s}))\mathbf{E}(X_{l}^{i}(\mathbf{r}))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,i})}\sum_{x_{i}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}x_{i}^{2}\cdot\mathbf{P}({U}_{N,l,i}=x_{i})-1-\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,i})

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\frac{2}{\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,i})}\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in N(\mathbf{r})}\mathbf{E}(X_{l}^{i}(\mathbf{s}))\mathbf{E}(X_{l}^{i}(\mathbf{r}))

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbf{E}([U_{N,l,i}]^{2})}{\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,i})}-1-\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,i})+\frac{2}{\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,i})}\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in N(\mathbf{r})}\mathbf{E}(X_{l}^{i}(\mathbf{s}))\mathbf{E}(X_{l}^{i}(\mathbf{r}))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,i})}\left(\mathbf{Var}\left(U_{N,l,i}\right)-\mathbf{E}\left(U_{N,l,i}\right)\right)+\frac{2}{\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,i})}\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in N(\mathbf{r})}\mathbf{E}(X_{l}^{i}(\mathbf{s}))\mathbf{E}(X_{l}^{i}(\mathbf{r})).

(E.33)

Next, we analyze the term
B_{N,l,3}
from the expression (
E.32
), that is,

\displaystyle B_{N,l,3}=\mathbf{E}\left|\widetilde{U}^{2}_{N,l,1}-U_{N,l,1}\right|=\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{I}_{2}=\mathbf{r})\cdot\mathbf{E}\left|\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}X_{l}^{1,(2,\mathbf{r})}(\mathbf{s})-\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}X_{l}^{1}(\mathbf{s})\right|

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{I}_{2}=\mathbf{r})\cdot\mathbf{E}\left|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}\\
|A(\mathbf{s})\cap A(\mathbf{r})|\neq\varnothing\end{subarray}}\left(X_{l}^{1,(2,\mathbf{r})}(\mathbf{s})-X_{l}^{1}(\mathbf{s})\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{I}_{2}=\mathbf{r})\cdot\mathbf{E}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}\\
|A(\mathbf{s})\cap A(\mathbf{r})|\neq\varnothing\end{subarray}}\left(X_{l}^{1,(2,\mathbf{r})}(\mathbf{s})-X_{l}^{1}(\mathbf{s})\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}+2\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{I}_{2}=\mathbf{r})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}\\
|A(\mathbf{s})\cap A(\mathbf{r})|\neq\varnothing\end{subarray}}\mathbf{E}(X_{l}^{1}(\mathbf{s}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{I}_{2}=\mathbf{r})\cdot\mathbf{E}\left(\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}X_{l}^{1,(2,\mathbf{r})}(\mathbf{s})-\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}X_{l}^{1}(\mathbf{s})\right)+2\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in N(\mathbf{r})}\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{I}_{2}=\mathbf{r})\mathbf{E}(X_{l}^{1}(\mathbf{s}))

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\left(\widetilde{U}^{2}_{N,l,1}-U_{N,l,1}\right)+\frac{2}{\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,2})}\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in N(\mathbf{r})}\mathbf{E}(X^{2}_{l}(\mathbf{r}))\mathbf{E}(X_{l}^{1}(\mathbf{s}))

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\left(\widetilde{U}^{2}_{N,l,1}\right)-\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,1})+\frac{2}{\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,2})}\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in N(\mathbf{r})}\mathbf{E}(X^{2}_{l}(\mathbf{r}))\mathbf{E}(X_{l}^{1}(\mathbf{s}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}x\mathbf{P}\left(\widetilde{U}^{2}_{N,l,1}=x\right)-\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,1})+\frac{2}{\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,2})}\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in N(\mathbf{r})}\mathbf{E}(X^{2}_{l}(\mathbf{r}))\mathbf{E}(X_{l}^{1}(\mathbf{s}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\sum_{y\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}x\mathbf{P}\left(\widetilde{U}^{2}_{N,l,1}=x,\widetilde{U}^{2}_{N,l,2}=y\right)-\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,1})+\frac{2}{\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,2})}\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in N(\mathbf{r})}\mathbf{E}(X^{2}_{l}(\mathbf{r}))\mathbf{E}(X_{l}^{1}(\mathbf{s}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\sum_{y\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\frac{xy\cdot\mathbf{P}\left(U_{N,l,1}=x,{U}_{N,l,2}=y\right)}{E(U_{N,l,2})}-\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,1})+\frac{2}{\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,2})}\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in N(\mathbf{r})}\mathbf{E}(X^{2}_{l}(\mathbf{r}))\mathbf{E}(X_{l}^{1}(\mathbf{s}))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,2})}\left(\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,1}\cdot U_{N,l,2})-\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,1})\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,2})\right)+\frac{2}{\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,2})}\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in N(\mathbf{r})}\mathbf{E}(X^{2}_{l}(\mathbf{r}))\mathbf{E}(X_{l}^{1}(\mathbf{s}))

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbf{cov}(U_{N,l,1},U_{N,l,2})}{\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,2})}+\frac{2}{\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,2})}\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in N(\mathbf{r})}\mathbf{E}(X^{2}_{l}(\mathbf{r}))\mathbf{E}(X_{l}^{1}(\mathbf{s})).

(E.34)

Therefore, combining (
E.33
) and (
E.34
), the upper bound in (
E.32
) reduces to

\displaystyle d_{{W}}(\mathbf{U}_{N,l},\mathbf{Z}_{N,l})\leq\sum_{i=1}^{2}\min\left\{1,\frac{1}{\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,i})}\right\}\left\{\mathbf{Var}\left(U_{N,l,i}\right)-\mathbf{E}\left(U_{N,l,i}\right)+2\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in N(\mathbf{r})}\mathbf{E}\left(X_{l}^{i}(\mathbf{s})\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}\left(X_{l}^{i}(\mathbf{r})\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}+2\cdot\mathbf{cov}(U_{N,l,1},U_{N,l,2})+4\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in N(\mathbf{r})}\mathbf{E}\left(X^{2}_{l}(\mathbf{r})\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}\left(X_{l}^{1}(\mathbf{s})\right),\ \text{for $l=1,2$,}

(E.35)

where,
N(\mathbf{r})=\{\mathbf{s}:|A(\mathbf{s})\cap A(\mathbf{r})|\geq 1\}
.
Note that, for
l=1,2
, using the definitions provided in (
E.25
) we can write

\displaystyle\left|\mathbf{Var}(U_{N,l,1})-\mathbf{E}\left(U_{N,l,1}\right)\right|=\left|\mathbf{Var}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,l}(H)\right)-\mathbf{E}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,l}(H)\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{t=1}^{R}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)-\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{E}(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H))\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.{}+\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{Var}\left(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H)\right)-\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{E}(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H))\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)+\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\left(\mathbf{E}\left(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H)\right)-\left[\mathbf{E}\left(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H)\right)\right]^{2}\right)-\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{E}(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H))\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt\left.{}-\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\left[\mathbf{E}\left(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H)\right)\right]^{2}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|D^{(1)}_{N,l,1}-D^{(1)}_{N,l,2}\right|,\hskip 8.50012pt\text{(say),}

\displaystyle\leq|D^{(1)}_{N,l,1}|+|D^{(1)}_{N,l,2}|.

(E.36)

From the expression in (
E.36
), using the fact that
H
is strictly balanced,
(\alpha,\beta)=(0,R/T)
and using Lemma
D.5
, there exists a positive integer
M_{4}
, dependent on the graph
H
and the model parameters, such that for
N\geq M_{4}
we can claim,

\displaystyle|D^{(1)}_{N,l,1}|=\left|\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{E}(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}-\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{E}(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H))\mathbf{E}(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H))\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))-\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}f_{l}(c:H)^{2}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}\pi_{N,\alpha_{s_{1,i}},\alpha_{s_{1,j}}}\pi_{N,\alpha_{s_{2,i}},\alpha_{s_{2,j}}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}N^{2R-2T\beta-t+\beta m(t;H)}\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{2T-m(t:H)}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\left|\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}N^{2R-t-2T\beta}\sum_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\in[K]^{R}}f_{l}(c:H)^{2}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}N^{2\beta}\pi_{N,u_{i},u_{j}}\pi_{N,v_{i},v_{j}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{R}\lambda_{N,u_{i}}\lambda_{N,v_{i}}+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{2T}\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}N^{-t+\frac{R}{T}m(t;H)}

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}N^{-t}\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{R}}f_{l}(c:H)^{2}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}N^{2\beta}\pi_{N,u_{i},u_{j}}\pi_{N,v_{i},v_{j}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{R}\lambda_{N,u_{i}}\lambda_{N,v_{i}}+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right).

(E.37)

In the expression (
E.37
), the exponent in the first term is negative since H is strictly balanced. This implies
\frac{t}{m(t:H)}>\frac{R}{T}
for all
t\in[R-1]
. Hence, using Assumptions
(A.2)
and
(A.3)
, it follows that
D^{(1)}_{N,l,1}\to 0
, as
N\to\infty
. Next, consider the term
|D^{(1)}_{N,l,2}|
in (
E.36
). Write

\displaystyle|D^{(1)}_{N,l,2}|=\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\left[\mathbf{E}\left(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H)\right)\right]^{2}=\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}f_{l}^{2}(c:H)\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}\pi_{N,\alpha_{s_{i}},\alpha_{s_{j}}}

\displaystyle=N^{R-2T\beta}f_{l}^{2}(c:H)\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{R}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}N^{2\beta}\pi^{2}_{N,u_{i},u_{j}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{R}\lambda_{N,s_{i}}+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=N^{-T\beta}f_{l}^{2}(c:H)\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{R}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}N^{2\beta}\pi^{2}_{N,u_{i},u_{j}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{R}\lambda_{N,s_{i}}+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right).

(E.38)

Therefore, using Assumptions
(A.2)
and
(A.3)
,
D_{2,N}\to 0
as
N\to\infty
. Hence, combining (
E.37
) and (
E.38
), we can say that
\left\{\mathbf{Var}(U_{N,l,1})-\mathbf{E}\left(U_{N,l,1}\right)\right\}\to 0
as
N\to\infty
. Using a similar argument as the one used in (
E.36
) above, one can show that
|\mathbf{Var}(U_{N,l,2})-\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,2})|\leq|D^{(2)}_{N,l,1}|+|D^{(2)}_{N,l,2}|
, where

\displaystyle\left|D^{(2)}_{N,l,1}\right|\leq\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{2T}\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}N^{-t+\frac{R}{T}m(t;H)}

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}N^{-t}\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{R}}(1-f_{l}(c:H))^{2}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}N^{2\beta}\pi^{2}_{N,u_{i},u_{j}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{R}\lambda_{N,u_{i}}\lambda_{N,v_{i}}+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right),\hskip 8.50012pt\text{and}

\displaystyle|D^{(2)}_{N,l,2}|=N^{-T\beta}(1-f_{l}(c:H))^{2}\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{R}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}N^{2\beta}\pi^{2}_{N,u_{i},u_{j}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{R}\lambda_{N,s_{i}}+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right),

using arguments similar to those used in (
E.37
) and (
E.38
). Thus, using Assumption
(A.2)
,
(A.3)
and the fact that
\beta=R/T
, we can say that
\left|D^{(2)}_{N,l,1}\right|+\left|D^{(2)}_{N,l,2}\right|\to 0
as
N\to\infty
. Hence
\{\mathbf{Var}(U_{N,l,2})-\mathbf{E}(U_{N,l,2})\}\to 0
as
N\to\infty
. Next, consider the term
\mathbf{cov}(U_{N,l,1},U_{N,l,2})
in the r.h.s. of (
E.35
). Using similar arguments as (
E.37
) we write,

\displaystyle\left|\mathbf{cov}\left(U_{N,l,1},U_{N,l,2}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{t=1}^{R}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H),(1-W_{N,l})Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{cov}(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),(1-W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\left|\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{R}}\mathbf{cov}(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),(1-W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\left(\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))-[((1-f_{l}(c,H))f_{l}(c:H))\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H))]^{2}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\left|\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{R}}\mathbf{cov}(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),(1-W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{2T}\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}N^{2R-2T\beta-t+\beta m(t;H)}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\left|\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}N^{2R-t-2T\beta}\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{R}}f_{l}(c:H)(1-f_{l}(c:H))\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}N^{2\beta}\pi^{2}_{N,u_{i},u_{j}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{R}\lambda_{N,u_{i}}+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\left|f_{l}(c:H)(1-f_{l}(c:H))\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}\pi^{2}_{N,\alpha_{s_{i}},\alpha_{s_{j}}}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{2T}\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}N^{2R-2T\beta-t+\beta m(t;H)}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\left|\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}N^{2R-t-2T\beta}\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{R}}f_{l}(c:H)(1-f_{l}(c:H))\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}N^{2\beta}\pi^{2}_{N,u_{i},u_{j}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{R}\lambda_{N,u_{i}}+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\left|N^{R-2T\beta}f_{l}(c:H)(1-f_{l}(c:H))\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{R}}N^{2\beta}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}\pi^{2}_{N,u_{i},u_{j}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{R}\lambda_{N,u_{i}}+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{2T}\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}N^{-t+(R/T)m(t;H)}\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}N^{-t}\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{R}}f_{l}(c:H)(1-f_{l}(c:H))\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}N^{2\beta}\pi^{2}_{N,u_{i},u_{j}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{R}\lambda_{N,u_{i}}+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)\right|

\displaystyle+\left|N^{T\beta}f_{l}(c:H)(1-f_{l}(c:H))\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{R}}N^{2\beta}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}\pi^{2}_{N,u_{i},u_{j}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{R}\lambda_{N,u_{i}}+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)\right|.

(E.39)

Therefore, using the fact that
H
is strictly balanced, that is,
t/m(t:H)<R/T
, for all
t\in[R-1]
,
\beta=R/T
and using Assumptions
(A.2)
and
(A.3)
, we can say that
\mathbf{cov}\left(U_{N,l,1},U_{N,l,2}\right)\to 0
, as
N\rightarrow\infty
.
We show that the remaining terms on the r.h.s. of (
E.35
) also converge to zero as
N\rightarrow\infty
. Using Assumption
(A.3)
and the fact that
\beta=R/T
, we obtain the following

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in N(\mathbf{r})}\mathbf{E}\left(X_{l}^{i}(\mathbf{r})\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}\left(X_{l}^{i}(\mathbf{s})\right)=\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in N(\mathbf{r})}\left[f_{l}(c:H)\right]^{2}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}\pi_{N,\alpha_{s_{i}},\alpha_{s_{j}}}\cdot\pi_{N,\alpha_{r_{i}},\alpha_{r_{j}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in N(\mathbf{r})}N^{-2R}\left(N^{2(R/T)}\max_{1\leq i,j\leq K}\pi^{2}_{N,i,j}\right)^{T}\leq R\cdot\frac{1}{N}\left(N^{2(R/T)}\max_{1\leq i,j\leq K}\pi^{2}_{N,i,j}\right)^{T}\to 0,~\text{and}

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{[N]}_{R}}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in N(\mathbf{r})}\mathbf{E}\left(X_{l}^{2}(\mathbf{r})\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}\left(X_{l}^{1}(\mathbf{s})\right)\leq R\cdot\frac{1}{N}\left(N^{2(R/T)}\max_{1\leq i,j\leq K}\pi^{2}_{N,i,j}\right)^{T}\to 0,~\text{as}~N\to\infty.

(E.40)

Combining all the above steps, it follows that
d_{{W}}(\mathbf{U}_{N,l},\mathbf{Z}_{N,l})\rightarrow 0
(see (
E.35
)), for both
l=1,2
, as
N\rightarrow\infty
. Convergence in
d_{{W}}
implies convergence in
d_{{BL}}
from the definition (see. (
E.5
)). Hence, we can write

d_{{BL}}(\mathbf{U}_{N,l},\mathbf{Z}_{N,l})\rightarrow 0,\hskip 8.50012pt\text{as $N\rightarrow\infty$, for $l=1,2$.}

(E.41)

Next, using Assumptions
(A.2)
and
(A.3)
and
\beta=R/T
, we can write

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left(U_{N,l,1}\right)=f_{l}(c:H)\cdot\kappa_{N}(\bm{\Pi}_{N},\bm{\lambda}_{N}:H),~\text{and}~\mathbf{E}\left(U_{N,l,2}\right)=(1-f_{l}(c:H))\cdot\kappa_{N}(\bm{\Pi}_{N},\bm{\lambda}_{N}:H),~\text{where},

\displaystyle\kappa_{N}(\bm{\Pi}_{N},\bm{\lambda}_{N}:H)=\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H)\right)=\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}\pi_{N,\alpha_{s_{i}},\alpha_{s_{j}}}

\displaystyle=N^{R-T\beta}\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{R}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}N^{\beta}\pi_{N,u_{i},u_{j}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{R}\lambda_{N,u_{i}}+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\to\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:H),\hskip 8.50012pt\text{(cf. \eqref{kap-def}).}

Define the random vector
\mathbf{Z}_{l}={(Z_{l,1},Z_{l,2})}^{T}
, for
l=1,2
, where
Z_{l,1}
and
Z_{l,2}
are independent,
Z_{l,1}\sim\text{Poisson}\bigg(f_{l}(c:H)\cdot\kappa\left(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:H\right)\bigg)
,
Z_{l,2}\sim\text{Poisson}\bigg((1-f_{l}(c:H))\cdot\kappa\left(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}\right)\bigg)
, and
\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:H)
is defined in (
3.18
). From the definition of
\mathbf{Z}_{N,l}
and
\mathbf{Z}_{l}
,
l=1,2
, and using the fact that
\kappa_{N}(\bm{\Pi}_{N},\bm{\lambda}_{N}:H)\rightarrow\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:H)
(see (
3.18
)), it implies that the characteristic function of
\mathbf{Z}_{N,l}
converges to the characteristic function of
\mathbf{Z}_{l}
, for
l=1,2
. Thus,

\mathbf{Z}_{N,l}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbf{Z}_{l}
, for
l=1,2
. Using Theorem 11.3.3 of
Dudley (2002)
we can claim that
d_{{BL}}(\mathbf{Z}_{N,l},\mathbf{Z}_{l})\to 0
, as
N\rightarrow\infty
, for
l=1,2
. By triangle inequality and (
E.41
),

d_{{BL}}\left(\mathbf{U}_{N,l},~\mathbf{Z}_{l}\right)\leq d_{{BL}}\left(\mathbf{U}_{N,l},\mathbf{Z}_{N,l}\right)+d_{{BL}}\left(\mathbf{Z}_{N,l},\mathbf{Z}_{l}\right)\to 0,\hskip 8.50012pt\text{as $N\rightarrow\infty$, for $l=1,2$.}

Again, using Theorem 11.3.3 from
Dudley (2002)
, we can say that

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{N,l}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbf{Z}_{l},\hskip 8.50012pt\text{for $l=1,2$.}

(E.42)

Using Lemma
D.14
, we can claim that
\sigma^{2}_{N,l}(H)\rightarrow f_{l}(c:H)(1-f_{l}(c:H))\cdot\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:H)
, for
l=1,2
, where
\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:H)
is defined in (
3.18
). From the definition of the pivotal quantity in (
1.3
) we can write (using (
E.42
) and the continuous mapping theorem)

\displaystyle T_{N,l}(H)

\displaystyle=\frac{\widehat{S}_{N,l}(H)-f_{l}(c:H)\cdot S_{N}(H)}{\sigma_{N,l}(H)}

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-f_{l}(c:H))\cdot\widehat{S}_{N,l}(H)-f_{l}(c:H)\cdot(S_{N}(H)-\widehat{S}_{N,l}(H))}{\sigma_{N,l}(H)}

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-f_{l}(c:H))\cdot U_{N,l,1}-f_{l}(c:H)\cdot U_{N,l,2}}{\sigma_{N,l}(H)}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}\frac{(1-f_{l}(c:H))\cdot Z_{l,1}-f_{l}(c:H)\cdot Z_{l,2}}{\left[f_{l}(c:H)(1-f_{l}(c:H))\cdot\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:H)\right]^{1/2}},\hskip 8.50012pt\text{for $l=1,2$.}

This completes the proof.

∎

Proof of Theorem
3.4
(b): Intermediately sparse sampling.

In this case
\alpha\in(0,1)
. In the induced case, it is assumed that the sparsity levels
(\alpha,\beta)
are within the boundary region

F_{1}(H)=\left\{(\alpha,\beta):0<\alpha<1,~\beta=\frac{R\cdot(1-\alpha)}{T}\right\},\hskip 8.50012pt\text{(see \eqref{F1F2-set}).}

In the ego-centric case,
(\alpha,\beta)
are within the boundary region

\displaystyle F_{2}(H)

\displaystyle=\left\{(\alpha,\beta):0<\alpha<\widetilde{\alpha}_{H},~\beta=\left(1-\frac{\tau(H)\cdot\alpha}{R}\right)\cdot\frac{R}{T}\right\},

where
\widetilde{\alpha}_{H}
is defined (
3.17
) and
H
satisfies the Assumption
(A.5)
. Write

\displaystyle\widehat{S}_{N,l}(H)-f_{l}(p_{N}:H)\cdot S_{N}(H)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H)-f_{l}(p_{N}:H)\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H)

\displaystyle\equiv E_{N,l,1}-E_{N,l,2},\hskip 8.50012pt\text{(say), for $l=1,2$.}

(E.43)

Recall (see (
2.6
)) that
p_{N}=cN^{-\alpha}
for some

\alpha\in(0,1)
and
c>0
. When
(\alpha,\beta)\in F_{l}(H)
,
l=1,2
, we can use Assumptions
(A.2)
and
(A.3)
to write

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left(E_{N,l,1}\right)=\mathbf{E}\left(E_{N,l,2}\right)=f_{l}(p_{N}:H)\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}\pi_{N,\alpha_{s_{i}},\alpha_{s_{j}}}

\displaystyle=f_{l}(p_{N}:H)\cdot N^{R-T\beta}\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{R}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}N^{\beta}\pi_{N,u_{i},u_{j}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{R}\lambda_{N,u_{i}}+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\to\phi_{l}(c:H)\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{R}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}c_{u_{i},u_{j}}\prod_{i=1}^{R}\lambda_{u_{i}}=\phi_{l}(c:H)\cdot\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:H),~\text{as $N\rightarrow\infty$, for $l=1,2$,}

(E.44)

where,
\phi_{1}(c:H)=c^{R}
,
\phi_{2}(c:H)=\lvert\mathcal{D}_{\min}(H)\rvert\cdot c^{\tau(H)}
, and
\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:H)
is defined in (
3.18
) and
|\mathcal{D}_{\min}(H)|
is the size of the collection of all minimum vertex cover sets for
H
. Note,
|\mathcal{D}_{\min}(H)|
is a finite quantity depending on
R
and
\tau(H)
. We will show that
E_{N,l,2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\rightarrow}}\phi_{l}(c:H)\cdot\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:H)
(cf. (
E.43
)). As
H
is strictly balanced and for
l=2
,
H
satisfies Assumption
(A.5)
. Then, for
(\alpha,\beta)\in F_{l}(H)
, we can write (for
l=1
and
l=2
)

\displaystyle\mathbf{Var}\left(E_{N,l,2}\right)=[f_{l}(p_{N}:H)]^{2}\cdot\mathbf{Var}\left(\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H)\right)

\displaystyle=[f_{l}(p_{N}:H)]^{2}\sum_{t=2}^{R}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{cov}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)

\displaystyle=[f_{l}(p_{N}:H)]^{2}\sum_{t=2}^{R}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\left[\mathbf{E}\left\{Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right\}-\right\{\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H)\right)\left\}{}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\phi^{2}_{l}(p_{N}:H)\cdot a_{\max}(H)\sum_{t=2}^{R}N^{2R-t-\beta(2T-m(t:H))}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}a_{\max}(H)c^{2R}\cdot N^{2R-2R\alpha-2T\beta}\sum_{t=2}^{R}N^{-t+\beta m(t:H)}&\hskip 8.50012pt\text{if $l=1$,}\\
|\mathcal{D}_{\min}(H)|^{2}c^{2\tau(H)}a_{\max}(H)\cdot N^{2R-2\tau(H)\alpha-2T\beta}\sum_{t=2}^{R}N^{-t+\beta m(t:H)}&\hskip 8.50012pt\text{if $l=2$,}\end{cases}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}a_{\max}(H)c^{2R}\sum_{t=2}^{R}N^{-t+\left(\frac{R}{T}(1-\alpha)\right)m(t:H)}&\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 8.50012pt\text{if $l=1$,}\\
|\mathcal{D}_{\min}(H)|^{2}c^{2\tau(H)}a_{\max}(H)\cdot\sum_{t=2}^{R}N^{-t+\left(\frac{R}{T}\left(1-\frac{\tau(H)}{R}\alpha\right)\right)m(t:H)}&\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 8.50012pt\text{if $l=2$,}\end{cases}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}a_{\max}(H)c^{2R}\sum_{t=2}^{R}N^{m(t:H)\left\{-\frac{t}{m(t:H)}+\frac{R}{T}(1-\alpha)\right\}}&\hskip 8.50012pt\text{if $l=1$,}\\
|\mathcal{D}_{\min}(H)|^{2}c^{2\tau(H)}a_{\max}(H)\cdot\sum_{t=2}^{R}N^{m(t:H)\left\{-\frac{t}{m(t:H)}+\frac{R}{T}\left(1-\frac{\tau(H)}{R}\alpha\right)\right\}}&\hskip 8.50012pt\text{if $l=2$,}\end{cases}

(E.45)

where,
a_{\max}(H)=\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{2T}
converges to a finite positive constant (due to Assumption
(A.3)
), depending only on
H
and the model parameters. As
H
is strictly balanced,
\frac{t}{m(t:H)}>\frac{R}{T}
for all
t\in\{2,\ldots,R-1\}
, and at
t=R
,
\frac{R}{T}>\frac{(1-\delta)\cdot R}{T}
for any
\delta\in(0,1)
. This implies

-\frac{t}{m(t:H)}+\frac{R}{T}\cdot(1-\delta)<0,\hskip 8.50012pt\text{for all $t=2,\ldots,R$,}

where
\delta=\alpha
, if
l=1
, and
\delta=\tau(H)\alpha/R
, if
l=2
. Hence, the exponent of
N
(for both
l=1
and
l=2
) in the upper bound provided in (
E.45
) will be strictly negative. Therefore,
\mathbf{Var}\left(E_{N,l,2}\right)\to 0
as
N\to\infty
, for
l=1,2
. Hence,
E_{N,l,2}-\mathbf{E}\left(E_{N,l,2}\right)=o_{P}(1)
, for
l=1,2
(see (
E.43
)).
Next, applying Theorem 6.23 of
Janson et al. (2000)
(see Theorem
C.2
) on
E_{N,l,1}
(cf. (
E.43
)) for both choices of
l
, we obtain the following bound on the total variation distance between the random variable
E_{N,l,1}
and
V_{N,l}
, where
V_{N,l}\sim\text{Poisson}\left(f_{l}(p_{N}:H)\cdot\kappa_{N}(\bm{\Pi}_{N},\bm{\lambda}_{N}:H)\right)
, (for both
l=1,2
),

\displaystyle d_{{TV}}\left(E_{N,l,1},V_{N,l}\right)\leq\min\left\{1,\frac{1}{f_{l}(p_{N}:H)\kappa_{N}(\bm{\Pi}_{N},\bm{\lambda}_{N}:H)}\right\}\bigg(\left\{\mathbf{Var}\left(E_{N,l,1}\right)-\mathbf{E}(E_{N,l,1})\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}+2\sum_{\mathbf{s}_{1}\in{[N]}_{R}}\sum_{\mathbf{s}_{2}\in N(\mathbf{s}_{1})}\eta_{N,l}\left(\mathbf{s}_{1}:p_{N},\bm{\Pi}_{N},H\right)\eta_{N,l}\left(\mathbf{s}_{2}:p_{N},\bm{\Pi}_{N},H\right)+2\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\left[\eta_{N,l}\left(\mathbf{s}:p_{N},\bm{\Pi}_{N},H\right)\right]^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\min\left\{1,\frac{1}{f_{l}(p_{N}:H)\kappa_{N}(\bm{\Pi}_{N},\bm{\lambda}_{N}:H)}\right\}\bigg(\left\{\mathbf{Var}\left(E_{N,l,1}\right)-\mathbf{E}(E_{N,l,1})\right\}+2\cdot G_{N,l,1}+2\cdot G_{N,l,2}\bigg),~\text{(say)},

(E.46)

where,
\eta_{N,l}\left(\mathbf{s}:p_{N},\bm{\Pi}_{N},H\right)=\mathbf{E}(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H))
, for
l=1,2
. It remains to show that the upper bound in (
E.46
) converges to zero for
l=1
and
2
, as
N\to\infty
. First, in case of induced network formation (
l=1
), for all
(\alpha,\beta)\in F_{1}(H)
, we study the quantity
|\mathbf{Var}(E_{N,1,1})-\mathbf{E}(E_{N,1,1})|
from (
E.46
) in the following way.

\displaystyle\lvert\mathbf{Var}(E_{N,1,1})-\mathbf{E}(E_{N,1,1})\rvert

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{t=1}^{R}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),~W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)-\mathbf{E}(E_{N,1,1})\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),~W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 8.50012pt{}+\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{R}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),~W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)-\mathbf{E}(E_{N,1,1})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\bigg\{\mathbf{E}\left(W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 17.00024pt{}-\mathbf{E}\left(W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\right)\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\right)\mathbf{E}\left(W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\bigg\}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\left|\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{Var}\left(W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H)\right)-\mathbf{E}(E_{N,1,1})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\bigg\{\mathbf{E}\left(W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\bigg\}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\left|\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{Var}\left(W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H)\right)-\mathbf{E}(E_{N,1,1})\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\left(\frac{c}{N^{\alpha}}\right)^{2R-t}\left(1-\left(\frac{c}{N^{\alpha}}\right)^{-t}\right)\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\left|\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{Var}\left(W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H)\right)-\mathbf{E}(E_{N,1,1})\right|.

(E.47)

Then under Assumptions
(A.2)
and
(A.3)
, using Lemma
D.5
in the first term of (
E.47
), we can say that there exists a positive integer
M_{5}
, depending only on the graph
H
and the model parameters, such that for all
N\geq M_{5}
,

r.h.s. of (
E.47
)

\displaystyle\leq\left|a_{\max}(H)\cdot c^{2R}\cdot N^{-g_{1,\alpha,\beta,H}(R)}\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}N^{{}g_{1,\alpha,\beta,H}(t)}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\left|\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{E}(W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H))-\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\bigg(\mathbf{E}(W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H))\bigg)^{2}-\mathbf{E}(E_{N,1,1})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|a_{\max}(H)\cdot c^{2R}\cdot N^{-g_{1,\alpha,\beta,H}(R)}\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}N^{{}g_{1,\alpha,\beta,H}(t)}\right|+\left|\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\bigg(\mathbf{E}(W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H))\bigg)^{2}\right|

(E.48)

\displaystyle=a_{\max}(H)\cdot c^{2R}\cdot\left(N^{-g_{1,\alpha,\beta,H}(R)}\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}N^{{}g_{1,\alpha,\beta,H}(t)}+N^{-R+2g_{1,\alpha,\beta,H}(R)}\right),

(E.49)

where,
a_{\max}(H)
is a finite positive constant (defined in (
E.45
)). Next, we study the quantity
|\mathbf{Var}(E_{N,l,1})-\mathbf{E}(E_{N,l,1})|
from (
E.47
) in case of ego-centric network formation (
l=2
), that is

\displaystyle\lvert\mathbf{Var}(E_{N,2,1})-\mathbf{E}(E_{N,2,1})\rvert

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{t=1}^{R}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),~W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)-\mathbf{E}(E_{N,2,1})\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),~W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 8.50012pt{}+\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{R}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),~W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)-\mathbf{E}(E_{N,2,1})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\bigg\{\mathbf{E}\left(W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 17.00024pt{}-\mathbf{E}\left(W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\right)\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\right)\mathbf{E}\left(W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\bigg\}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\left|\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{Var}\left(W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H)\right)-\mathbf{E}(E_{N,2,1})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\bigg\{\mathbf{E}\left(W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\bigg\}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\left|\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{Var}\left(W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H)\right)-\mathbf{E}(E_{N,2,1})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|a_{\max}(H)\cdot c^{2\tau(H)}\left|\mathcal{D}(H)\right|^{2}N^{-g_{2,\alpha,\beta,H}(R)}\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}N^{{}g_{2,\alpha,\beta,H}(t)}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\left|\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{E}(W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H))-\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\bigg(\mathbf{E}(W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H))\bigg)^{2}-\mathbf{E}(E_{N,2,1})\right|.

(E.50)

Then under Assumption
(A.2)
and
(A.3)
, along with the Assumption
(A.5)
over the graph
H
, using Lemma
D.11
in the first term of (
E.50
), we can say that there exists a positive integer
M_{6}
, depending on the graph and model parameters, such that for all
N\geq M_{6}
,

\displaystyle\text{r.h.s. of \eqref{1st-term-TV-1}}\leq\left|a_{\max}(H)\cdot c^{2\tau(H)}\left|\mathcal{D}(H)\right|^{2}N^{-g_{2,\alpha,\beta,H}(R)}\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}N^{{}g_{2,\alpha,\beta,H}(t)}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\left|\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\bigg(\mathbf{E}(W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H))\bigg)^{2}\right|

(E.51)

\displaystyle=a_{\max}(H)\cdot c^{2\tau(H)}\left|\mathcal{D}(H)\right|^{2}\left(N^{-g_{2,\alpha,\beta,H}(R)}\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}N^{{}g_{2,\alpha,\beta,H}(t)}+N^{-R+2g_{2,\alpha,\beta,H}(R)}\right),

(E.52)

where,
|\mathcal{D}(H)|
is the size of the collection of all possible vertex covers of
H
, which is a finite quantity. Next, when
(\alpha,\beta)\in F_{l}(H)
, we have
g_{l,\alpha,\beta,H}(R)=0
for
l=1,2
. Moreover, for
(\alpha,\beta)\in F_{l}(H)
,
l=1,2
, and
t\in\{1,\ldots,R-1\}
, writing
\beta
in terms of
\alpha
we have the following.

\displaystyle g_{1,\alpha,\beta,H}(t)

\displaystyle=(1-\alpha)\left(-\frac{t}{m(t:H)}+\frac{R}{T}\right)m(t:H),\hskip 8.50012pt\text{and}

\displaystyle g_{2,\alpha,\beta,H}(t)

\displaystyle=m(t:H)\left\{\left(-\frac{t}{m(t:H)}+\frac{R}{T}\right)+\left(\frac{\min(\tau(H),t)}{m(t:H)}-\frac{\tau(H)}{T}\right)\alpha\right\}.

Since
\alpha\in(0,1)
in the induced case and

\alpha\in(0,\widetilde{\alpha}_{H})
in the egocentric case, it follows that
g_{l,\alpha,\beta,H}(t)<0
for all
t\in\{1,\ldots,R-1\}
. Therefore,
\{\mathbf{Var}(E_{N,l,1})-\mathbf{E}(E_{N,l,1})\}\to 0
as
N\rightarrow\infty
.

\displaystyle\{\mathbf{Var}(E_{N,l,1})-\mathbf{E}(E_{N,l,1})\}\to 0,\hskip 8.50012pt\text{as $N\to\infty$ for $l=1,2$.}

The expression for the term
G_{N,l,2},~l=1,2
(see (
E.46
)) is the same as the second term in (
E.48
) and (
E.51
) respectively; hence
G_{N,l,2}\to 0
, under the same assumptions. Using Assumptions
(A.2)
and
(A.3)
and the fact that
(\alpha,\beta)\in F_{l}(H),~l=1,2
, we obtain the following

\displaystyle G_{N,l,1}=\sum_{\mathbf{s}_{1}\in{[N]}_{R}}\sum_{\mathbf{s}_{2}\in N(\mathbf{s}_{1})}\eta_{N}\left(\mathbf{s}_{1}:p_{N},\bm{\Pi}_{N},H\right)\eta_{N}\left(\mathbf{s}_{2}:p_{N},\bm{\Pi}_{N},H\right)

\displaystyle\leq R\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{1}}\mathbf{E}(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\cdot\mathbf{E}(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))

\displaystyle=R\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{1}}f^{2}_{l}(p_{N}:H)\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}\pi_{\alpha_{s_{1,i}},\alpha_{s_{1,j}}}\pi_{\alpha_{s_{2,i}},\alpha_{s_{2,j}}}\leq Rf^{2}_{l}(p_{N}:H)\cdot\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{2T}N^{2R-1-2T\beta}

\displaystyle\leq R\cdot\max\left\{c^{2R},c^{2\tau(H)}|\mathcal{D}(H)|^{2}\right\}\cdot\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{2T}N^{2g_{l,\alpha,\beta,H}(R)-1}

\displaystyle=R\cdot\max\{c^{2R},|\mathcal{D}(H)|^{2}\}\cdot\left(N^{2\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]}\pi^{2}_{i,j}\right)N^{-1}\rightarrow 0,\hskip 8.50012pt\text{as $N\rightarrow\infty$.}

Thus, all terms in the r.h.s. of (
E.46
) go to zero as
N\rightarrow\infty
, and we can claim

\displaystyle d_{TV}\left(E_{N,l,1},V_{N,l}\right)\to 0,\hskip 8.50012pt\text{as $N\to\infty$, for $l=1,2$.}

(E.53)

Define two random variables
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
, where
V_{l}\sim{\text{Poisson}}\left(\phi_{l}(c:H)\cdot\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:H)\right)
, for
l=1,2
(cf. (
E.44
)). As
\mathbf{E}\left(V_{N,l}\right)\to\mathbf{E}(V_{l})
, for
l=1,2
, it implies
V_{N,l}\xrightarrow{d}V_{l}
, for
l=1,2
. Now, using Lemma
D.15
we can claim that
d_{TV}(V_{N,l},V_{l})\to 0
, as
N\to\infty
, for
l=1,2
. By triangle inequality and (
E.53
), we can write

\displaystyle d_{{TV}}\left(E_{N,l,1},V_{l}\right)

\displaystyle\leq d_{{TV}}\left(E_{N,l,1},V_{N,l}\right)+d_{{TV}}\left(V_{N,l},V_{l}\right)\to 0,\hskip 8.50012pt\text{as $N\rightarrow\infty$, for $l=1,2$.}

(E.54)

Using Lemma
D.14
, we can claim that
\sigma^{2}_{N,l}(H)\to\phi_{l}(c:H)\cdot\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:H)
for
l=1,2
, where
\kappa(\mathbf{C},\lambda:H)
is defined in (
3.18
) and
\phi_{l}(c:H)
is defined in (
E.44
), for
l=1,2
.
Combining this with the fact that
E_{N,l,2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\rightarrow}}\phi_{l}(c:H)\cdot\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:H)
(see (
E.44
)) and using (
E.54
), we can write

\displaystyle T_{N,l}(H)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}\frac{V_{l}-\phi_{l}(c:H)\cdot\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:H)}{\left[\phi_{l}(c:H)\cdot\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:H)\right]^{1/2}},\hskip 8.50012pt\text{for $l=1,2$.}

This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.4
part (c): Extremely sparse sampling in case of
\tau(H)=1
.

As
\tau(H)=1
,
H=\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1}
is a star graph on
R
vertices (see Lemma
D.16
for a proof of this fact). We focus only on the case of ego-centric network formation and the sampling and model sparsity levels are
\alpha=1
and
\beta\in[0,1)
. This implies,
p_{N}=c/N
, for all
N\geq 1
. Note that due to the edge structure of the star graph, we have
f_{2}(p_{N}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})=p_{N}+{(1-p_{N})}\cdot p^{R-1}_{N}
(cf. (
3.6
)) and for any
\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}
we can write (see (
E.7
)),

\displaystyle W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})

\displaystyle=\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})}\max\{W_{N,s_{i}},W_{N,s_{j}}\}=W_{N,s_{1}}+(1-W_{N,s_{1}})\cdot\prod_{j=2}^{R}W_{N,s_{j}},\hskip 8.50012pt\text{and}

\displaystyle Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=2}^{R}Y_{s_{1},s_{j}}.

As a result (see (
E.7
)),

\displaystyle T_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})=\frac{\widehat{S}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})-f_{2}(p_{N}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})\cdot S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})}{\sigma_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\left(W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})-(p_{N}+(1-p_{N})p_{N}^{R-1})\right)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\left(W_{N,s_{1}}-p_{N}\right)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\sigma_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\left[(1-W_{N,s_{1}})W_{N,s_{2}}\cdots W_{N,s_{R}}-(1-p_{N})p_{N}^{R-1}\right]Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})

\displaystyle=\frac{G_{N,1}}{\sigma_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})}+\frac{G_{N,2}}{\sigma_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})},\hskip 8.50012pt\text{(say).}

(E.55)

Using Lemma
D.14
, we can claim

\displaystyle\sigma_{N,2}^{2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})=\mathbf{Var}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})-f_{2}(p_{N}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})\cdot S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})\right)

\displaystyle=N^{2(R-1)(1-\beta)}\left\{c\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{R},(v_{2},\ldots,v_{R})\in[K]^{R-1}}\prod_{j=2}^{R}N^{2\beta}\pi_{u_{1},u_{j}}\pi_{u_{1},v_{j}}\prod_{j=1}^{R}\lambda_{N,u_{j}}\prod_{j=2}^{R}\lambda_{N,v_{j}}\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right\},

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\frac{\sigma^{2}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})}{N^{2(R-1)(1-\beta)}}\to\nu(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}),\hskip 8.50012pt\text{as $N\rightarrow\infty$,}

(E.56)

where,
\nu(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda})
is defined in (
3.22
).
Note that
\mathbf{E}(G_{N,1})=\mathbf{E}(G_{N,2})=0
, and

\displaystyle\mathbf{Var}\left(G_{N,2}\right)=\mathbf{Var}\left(\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\left[(1-W_{N,s_{1}})W_{N,s_{2}}\cdots W_{N,s_{R}}-(1-p_{N})p_{N}^{R-1}\right]Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{R}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\bigg|\mathbf{cov}\left((1-W_{N,s_{1,1}})W_{N,s_{1,2}}\cdots W_{N,s_{1,R}},(1-W_{N,s_{2,1}})W_{N,s_{2,2}}\cdots W_{N,s_{2,R}}\right)\bigg|

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1}))Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})).

(E.57)

Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we can write

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbf{cov}\left((1-W_{N,s_{1,1}})W_{N,s_{1,2}}\cdots W_{N,s_{1,R}},(1-W_{N,s_{2,1}})W_{N,s_{2,2}}\cdots W_{N,s_{2,R}}\right)\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{Var}\left((1-W_{N,s_{1,1}})W_{N,s_{1,2}}\cdots W_{N,s_{1,R}}\right)=N^{-(R-1)}c^{R-1}\left(1-\frac{c}{N}\right)\left(1-\left(\frac{c}{N}\right)^{R-1}\left(1-\frac{c}{N}\right)\right).

Then, using (
E.57
) and dividing by the variance term
\sigma^{2}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})
in (
E.56
), and using the fact that
\beta\in[0,1)
, we can claim

\displaystyle\mathbf{Var}\left(G_{N,2}\right)\leq\sum_{t=1}^{R}N^{2R-t}N^{-(R-1)}N^{-\beta(2(R-1)-(t-1))}=N^{-(R-1)}N^{2R-2(R-1)\beta}\sum_{t=1}^{R}N^{-t+\beta m(t:H)}

\displaystyle=N^{-(R-1)}N^{2R-2(R-1)\beta}\sum_{t=1}^{R}N^{m(t:H)\left(-\frac{t}{m(t:H)}+\beta\right)}=N^{-(R-1)}N^{2R-2(R-1)\beta}N^{-R+\beta(R-1)}=N^{1-(R-1)\beta}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\frac{\mathbf{Var}\left(G_{N,2}\right)}{\sigma^{2}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})}\leq\frac{N^{1-(R-1)\beta}}{N^{2(R-1)(1-\beta)}}=N^{-(2R-3)+(R-1)\beta}\rightarrow 0,\hskip 8.50012pt\text{as $N\rightarrow\infty$.}

Returning to (
E.55
), we can now claim that

\displaystyle\frac{G_{N,2}}{\sigma_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})}=o_{P}(1).

(E.58)

We split the
G_{N,1}
term in (
E.55
) as follows

\displaystyle\frac{G_{N,1}}{\sigma_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})}=\frac{1}{\sigma_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})}\left(\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}W_{N,s_{1}}\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}W_{N,s_{1}}\bigg(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:\mathbb{K}_{R-1})-\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:\mathbb{K}_{R-1})\right)\bigg)-p_{N}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})}\left(L_{N,1,1}+L_{N,1,2}+L_{N,1,3}\right),~\text{(say)}.

(E.59)

Note that,
\mathbf{E}(L_{N,1,2})=0
. Using the definition of
\mathcal{M}_{t}
(see (
E.97
)) and using Assumptions
(A.2)
and
(A.3)
, we can write

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbf{Var}\left(L_{N,1,2}\right)}{\sigma^{2}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})}=\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})}\mathbf{Var}\left(\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}W_{N,s_{1}}\bigg(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})-\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:\mathbb{K}_{R-1})\right)\bigg)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})}\sum_{t=1}^{R}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{E}(W_{N,s_{1,1}}W_{N,s_{2,1}})\cdot\mathbf{cov}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1}),Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})}\sum_{t=2}^{R}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{E}(W_{N,s_{1,1}}W_{N,s_{2,1}})\cdot\mathbf{cov}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1}),Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{d_{\max}(H)}{N^{2(R-1)(1-\beta)}}\sum_{t=2}^{R}N^{2R-t-1}N^{-\beta(2(R-1)-(t-1))}

\displaystyle=\frac{d_{\max}(H)}{N^{2(R-1)(1-\beta)}}\sum_{t=2}^{R}N^{(2R-t-1)(1-\beta)}

\displaystyle=d_{\max}(H)\cdot\frac{N^{(2R-3)(1-\beta)}}{N^{2(R-1)(1-\beta)}}\to 0,\hskip 8.50012pt\text{as $N\rightarrow\infty$,}

where,
d_{\max}(H)=c^{2}\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{2(R-1)}
is a finite positive constant that depends on
H
and the underlying model parameters. Hence,

\displaystyle\frac{L_{N,1,2}}{\sigma_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})}=o_{P}(1).

(E.60)

Next, we analyze the term
L_{N,1,3}/\sigma_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})
in the expression (
E.59
). Using Assumptions
(A.2)
,
(A.3)

and the variance expression written in (
E.56
), we can claim the following

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left(\frac{L_{N,1,3}}{\sigma_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})}\right)\to\frac{c\cdot\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})}{\left[\nu(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda})\right]^{1/2}},\hskip 8.50012pt\text{and}

\displaystyle\mathbf{Var}\left(\frac{L_{N,1,3}}{\sigma_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})}\right)=O\left(\frac{N^{-2}\sum_{t=2}^{R}N^{-\beta(2R-t-1)}}{N^{2(R-1)(1-\beta)}}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{N^{(R-1)(1-\beta)}N^{R+1}}\right)\to 0,\hskip 8.50012pt\text{as $N\to\infty$,}

where
\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:H)
defined in (
3.18
). Hence,

\displaystyle\frac{L_{N,1,3}}{\sigma_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\rightarrow}}\frac{c\cdot\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})}{\left[\nu(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda})\right]^{1/2}}.

(E.61)

Next, it remains to analyze the term
L_{N,1,1}/\sigma_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})
. Using the convergence result for
\sigma_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})
in (
E.56
) we can write

\displaystyle\frac{L_{N,1,1}}{N^{(R-1)(1-\beta)}}=\frac{1}{N^{(1-\beta)(R-1)}}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}W_{N,s_{1}}\prod_{j=2}^{R}\pi_{\alpha_{s_{1}},\alpha_{s_{j}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{N^{(R-1)-\beta(R-1)}}{N^{(1-\beta)(R-1)}}\left(\sum_{u_{1}=1}^{K}\sum_{s_{1}=1}^{N}W_{N,s_{1}}\mathbf{1}(\alpha_{s_{1}}=u_{1})\left(\sum_{u_{2},\ldots,u_{R}=1}^{R}\prod_{j=2}^{R}N^{\beta}\pi_{u_{1},u_{j}}\left(\prod_{j=2}^{R}\lambda_{N,u_{j}}+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{u_{1}=1}^{K}\sum_{s_{1}=1}^{N}W_{N,s_{1}}\mathbf{1}(\alpha_{s_{1}}=u_{1})\left(\sum_{u_{2},\ldots,u_{R}=1}^{R}\prod_{j=2}^{R}N^{\beta}\pi_{u_{1},u_{j}}\left(\prod_{j=2}^{R}\lambda_{N,u_{j}}+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{u_{1}=1}^{K}\sum_{s_{1}=1}^{N}W_{N,s_{1}}\mathbf{1}(\alpha_{s_{1}}=u_{1})\cdot a_{N,u_{1}},~\text{(say)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s_{1}=1}^{N}W_{N,s_{1}}\mathbf{1}(\alpha_{s_{1}}=1)a_{N,1}+\cdots+\sum_{s_{1}=1}^{N}W_{N,s_{1}}\mathbf{1}(\alpha_{s_{1}}=K)a_{N,K}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s_{1}=1}^{N}W_{N,s_{1}}\left(\mathbf{1}(\alpha_{s_{1}}=1)a_{N,1}+\cdots+\mathbf{1}(\alpha_{s_{1}}=K)\right)a_{N,K}\equiv J_{N},~\text{(say)}.

where, for fixed
u_{1}\in[K]^{R}
, using Assumptions
(A.2)
and
(A.3)
, we can write

\displaystyle a_{N,u_{1}}=\sum_{u_{2},\ldots,u_{R}=1}^{R}\prod_{j=2}^{R}N^{\beta}\pi_{u_{1},u_{j}}\left(\prod_{j=2}^{R}\lambda_{N,u_{j}}+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)\to a_{u_{1}}\equiv\sum_{u_{2},\ldots,u_{R}=1}^{R}\prod_{j=2}^{R}c_{u_{1},u_{j}}\prod_{j=2}^{R}\lambda_{u_{j}}.

(E.62)

Now, using Assumptions
(A.2)
and
(A.3)
, the characteristic function of
J_{N}
can be expressed as,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}(\exp(itJ_{N}))=\mathbf{E}\left(\exp\left(it\sum_{s_{1}=1}^{N}W_{N,s_{1}}\left(\mathbf{1}(\alpha_{s_{1}}=1)a_{N,1}+\cdots+\mathbf{1}(\alpha_{s_{1}}=K)\right)a_{N,K}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\prod_{s_{1}=1}^{N}\mathbf{E}\left(\exp\left(itW_{N,s_{1}}\left(\mathbf{1}(\alpha_{s_{1}}=1)a_{N,1}+\cdots+\mathbf{1}(\alpha_{s_{1}}=K)a_{N,K}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\prod_{s_{1}=1}^{N}\left(\exp\left(it[\mathbf{1}(\alpha_{s_{1}}=1)a_{N,1}+\cdots+\mathbf{1}(\alpha_{s_{1}}=K)a_{N,K}]\right)\frac{c}{N}+\left(1-\frac{c}{N}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\prod_{u=1}^{K}\left(\left(\frac{c\cdot(\exp\left(ita_{N,u}\right)-1)}{N}+1\right)^{N}\right)^{N_{u}/N}

\displaystyle\to\exp\left(c\sum_{u_{1}=1}^{K}\lambda_{u_{1}}\left(e^{ita_{u_{1}}}-1\right)\right),~\text{as $N\rightarrow\infty$, for all $t\in\mathbb{R}$.}

(E.63)

The above expression (in (
E.63
)) is the characteristic function of the limiting random variable
\sum_{u_{1}=1}^{K}a_{u_{1}}\cdot Z_{u_{1}}
, where
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{K}
are independent and
Z_{u_{1}}\sim\text{Poisson}(c\cdot\lambda_{u_{1}})
for all
u_{1}\in[K]
. Therefore, using (
E.56
) we can claim

\displaystyle\frac{L_{N,1,1}}{\sigma_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}\frac{1}{[\nu(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:H)]^{1/2}}\sum_{u_{1}=1}^{K}a_{u_{1}}Z_{u_{1}}.

(E.64)

Hence, combining (
E.58
), (
E.60
),(
E.61
) and (
E.64
), also using the expression of
\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})
(cf. (
3.18
)), we can write

\displaystyle T_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}\frac{1}{[\nu(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:H)]^{1/2}}\left(\sum_{u_{1}=1}^{K}a_{u_{1}}(Z_{u_{1}}-c\cdot\lambda_{u_{1}})\right).

where
a_{u_{1}}
defined in (
E.62
) and
\nu(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:H)
defined in (
E.56
). This completes the proof.

∎

Proof of Theorem
3.5
.

Even though this proof considers the estimated clustering coefficient in the induced case, it includes arguments which are usable in both the induced and the ego-centric cases. The clustering coefficient of the population network
G_{N}
is defined as

\displaystyle\Gamma_{N}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\frac{S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}{S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}\displaystyle&\text{if $S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})>0$,}\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

The sample based estimated clustering coefficient is defined as

\displaystyle\widehat{\Gamma}_{N,l}=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{3})/f_{l}(p_{N},\mathbb{K}_{3})}{\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})/f_{l}(p_{N}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}\displaystyle&\text{if $\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})>0$,}\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases},\hskip 8.50012pt\text{for $l=1,2$,}

where,
\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})
and
\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{3})
,
l=1,2
, are defined in (
3.5
), with

\displaystyle f_{l}(p_{N}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})=\begin{cases}p_{N}^{3}&\text{if $l=1$,}\\
p_{N}^{2}(1-p_{N})+p_{N}&\text{if $l=2$,}\end{cases}\hskip 8.50012pt\text{and}\hskip 8.50012pt\ f_{l}(p_{N}:\mathbb{K}_{3})=\begin{cases}p_{N}^{3}&\text{if $l=1$,}\\
3p_{N}^{2}(1-p_{N})+p^{3}_{N}&\text{if $l=2$.}\end{cases}

(E.65)

From the definition provided above, one can write (for both
l=1
and
l=2
),

\displaystyle\widehat{\Gamma}_{N,l}-\Gamma_{N}=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}{\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}\frac{1}{b_{l}(p_{N})}-\frac{S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}{S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}\displaystyle&\hskip 8.50012pt\text{if $S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})>0$ and $\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})>0$},\\
\hskip 17.00024pt{}0&\hskip 8.50012pt\text{if $S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})=0$},\\
\displaystyle{}-\frac{S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}{S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}\displaystyle&\hskip 8.50012pt\text{if $S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})>0$ and $\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})=0$,}\end{cases},

where,

\displaystyle b_{l}(p_{N})=\frac{f_{l}(p_{N}:\mathbb{K}_{3})}{f_{l}(p_{N}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})},\hskip 8.50012pt\text{for $l=1,2$.}

Define the sequences of events

\displaystyle D_{1,N}=\left[{S}_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})=0\right]\hskip 8.50012pt\text{and}\hskip 8.50012ptD_{l,2,N}=\left[\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})=0\right],\hskip 8.50012pt\text{for each $N\geq 1$, and for $l=1,2$.}

(E.66)

Then, for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
and the scaling sequence
\{r_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta):N\geq 1\}
,
l=1,2
, defined in (
3.31
) and (
3.36
), we can write

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(r_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\left(\widehat{\Gamma}_{N,l}-\Gamma_{N}\right)\leq t\right)=\mathbf{P}\left(r_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\left(\widehat{\Gamma}_{N,l}-\Gamma_{N}\right)\leq t\mid{\left(D_{l,2,N}\right)}^{c}\cap D^{c}_{1,N}\right)\mathbf{P}\left({\left(D_{l,2,N}\right)}^{c}\cap D^{c}_{1,N}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt{}+\mathbf{P}\left(r_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\left(\widehat{\Gamma}_{N,l}-\Gamma_{N}\right)\leq t\mid D_{1,N}\right)\mathbf{P}\left(D_{1,N}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\mathbf{P}\left(r_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\left(\widehat{\Gamma}_{N,l}-\Gamma_{N}\right)\leq t\mid D_{1,N}^{c}\cap D_{l,2,N}\right)\mathbf{P}\left(D_{1,N}^{c}\cap D_{l,2,N}\right)

\displaystyle\equiv x_{1,N}(t)+x_{2,N}(t)+x_{3,N}(t),\hskip 8.50012pt\text{(say).}

(E.67)

Consider the second and third terms in the r.h.s. of (
E.67
). Note that
D_{1,N}\subseteq D_{l,2,N}
, for both
l=1,2
, since zero wedges in the population network
G_{N}
will imply that there will be zero wedges in the observed induced (or ego-centric) subgraph. This implies
\mathbf{P}(D_{1,N})\leq\mathbf{P}(D_{l,2,N})
, for
l=1,2
. Note that for
l=1,2
, we can write

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\right)=p_{N}^{3}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in[N]_{3}}\pi_{N,\alpha_{s_{1}},\alpha_{s_{2}}}\pi_{N,\alpha_{s_{1}},\alpha_{s_{3}}}

\displaystyle=N^{3-3\alpha-2\beta}c^{3}\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{3}}N^{2\beta}\pi_{N,u_{1},u_{2}}\pi_{N,u_{1},u_{3}}\left(\lambda_{N,u_{1}}\lambda_{N,u_{2}}\lambda_{N,u_{3}}+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right),\hskip 8.50012pt\text{and}

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\right)=\left(p_{N}+(1-p_{N})p_{N}^{2}\right)\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in[N]_{3}}\pi_{N,\alpha_{s_{1}},\alpha_{s_{2}}}\pi_{N,\alpha_{s_{1}},\alpha_{s_{3}}}

\displaystyle=N^{3-\alpha-2\beta}c\left(1+\left(1-\frac{c}{N^{\alpha}}\right)\frac{c}{N^{\alpha}}\right)\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{3}}N^{2\beta}\pi_{N,u_{1},u_{2}}\pi_{N,u_{1},u_{3}}\left(\lambda_{N,u_{1}}\lambda_{N,u_{2}}\lambda_{N,u_{3}}+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right).

Also, from the definition of
g_{l,\alpha,\beta,H}(\cdot)
in (
1.5
),
g_{1,\alpha,\beta,\mathbb{K}_{1,2}}(3)=-3+3\alpha+2\beta
, and
g_{2,\alpha,\beta,\mathbb{K}_{1,2}}(3)=-3+\alpha+2\beta
. Using this, we can say that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbf{E}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\right)}{N^{g_{1,\alpha,\beta,\mathbb{K}_{1,2}}(3)}}=c^{3}\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{3}}c_{u_{1},u_{2}}c_{u_{1},u_{3}}\cdot\lambda_{u_{1}}\lambda_{u_{2}}\lambda_{u_{3}}+R_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})=\mu_{1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})+R_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2}),~\text{and},

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbf{E}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\right)}{N^{g_{2,\alpha,\beta,\mathbb{K}_{1,2}}(3)}}=c\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{3}}c_{u_{1},u_{2}}c_{u_{1},u_{3}}\cdot\lambda_{u_{1}}\lambda_{u_{2}}\lambda_{u_{3}}+R_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})=\mu_{2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})+R_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2}),\hskip 8.50012pt\text{(say),}

where,

\displaystyle R_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})

\displaystyle=\left\{c^{3}\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{3}}N^{2\beta}\pi_{N,u_{1},u_{2}}\pi_{N,u_{1},u_{3}}\left(\lambda_{N,u_{1}}\lambda_{N,u_{2}}\lambda_{N,u_{3}}+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt{}\left.{}-c^{3}\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{3}}c_{u_{1},u_{2}}c_{u_{1},u_{3}}\cdot\lambda_{u_{1}}\lambda_{u_{2}}\lambda_{u_{3}}\right\},~\text{and,}

\displaystyle R_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})

\displaystyle=\left\{c\left(1+\left(1-\frac{c}{N^{\alpha}}\right)\frac{c}{N^{\alpha}}\right)\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{3}}N^{2\beta}\pi_{N,u_{1},u_{2}}\pi_{N,u_{1},u_{3}}\left(\lambda_{N,u_{1}}\lambda_{N,u_{2}}\lambda_{N,u_{3}}+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt{}\left.{}-c\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{3}}c_{u_{1},u_{2}}c_{u_{1},u_{3}}\cdot\lambda_{u_{1}}\lambda_{u_{2}}\lambda_{u_{3}}\right\}.

Using Assumptions
(A.2)
and
(A.3)
, we can say that
|R_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})|\to 0
as
N\rightarrow\infty
for
l=1,2
. Due to Lemma
D.10
, the triangle and wedge satisfy the Assumption
(A.5)
. Now, using Proposition
D.19
, we can write,

\displaystyle b_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\left(\frac{\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})-\mathbf{E}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\right)}{N^{\frac{1}{2}g_{l,\alpha,\beta,\mathbb{K}_{1,2}}(3)}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}N(0,\psi^{2}_{l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})),\hskip 8.50012pt\text{for $l=1,2$,}

(E.68)

where,

\displaystyle b_{N,1}(\alpha,\beta)=\begin{cases}N^{\frac{1}{2}(1-\alpha)}&~\text{if $(\alpha,\beta)\in C_{1,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})$,}\\
N^{\frac{3}{2}(1-\alpha)-\beta}&~\text{if $(\alpha,\beta)\in C_{1,3}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})$,}\end{cases}~\text{and}~b_{N,2}(\alpha,\beta)=\begin{cases}N^{\frac{1}{2}(1-\alpha)}&~\text{if $(\alpha,\beta)\in C_{2,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})$,}\\
N^{\frac{1}{2}(3-\alpha-2\beta)}&~\text{if $(\alpha,\beta)\in C_{2,3}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})$.}\end{cases}

Then from the expression (
E.67
),
\mathbf{P}\left(D_{l,2,N}\right)
has the following expression,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(D_{l,2,N}\right)=\mathbf{P}\left(b_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot\frac{\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})-\mathbf{E}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\right)}{N^{\frac{1}{2}g_{l,\alpha,\beta,\mathbb{K}_{1,2}}(3)}}={}-b_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{E}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\right)}{N^{\frac{1}{2}g_{l,\alpha,\beta,\mathbb{K}_{1,2}}(3)}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\left(b_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot\frac{\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})-\mathbf{E}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\right)}{N^{\frac{1}{2}g_{l,\alpha,\beta,\mathbb{K}_{1,2}}(3)}}={}-b_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot\left(\mu_{l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})+R_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\left[\mathbf{P}\left(b_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot\frac{\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})-\mathbf{E}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\right)}{N^{\frac{1}{2}g_{l,\alpha,\beta,\mathbb{K}_{1,2}}(3)}}\leq{}-b_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot\left(\mu_{l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})+R_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\right)\right)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt\left.{}-\Phi\left({}-\frac{b_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)}{\psi_{l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}\cdot\left(\mu_{l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})+R_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt{}-\left[\mathbf{P}\left(b_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot\frac{\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})-\mathbf{E}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\right)}{N^{\frac{1}{2}g_{l,\alpha,\beta,\mathbb{K}_{1,2}}(3)}}<{}-b_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot\left(\mu_{l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})+R_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\right)\right)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt\left.{}-\Phi\left({}-\frac{b_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)}{\psi_{l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}\cdot\left(\mu_{l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})+R_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\equiv e_{l,1,N}-e_{l,2,N},\hskip 8.50012pt\text{(say), for $l=1,2$.}

Following the CLT result in Proposition
D.19
, we can use Polya’s theorem (see Lemma 8.2.6 of
Athreya and Lahiri (2006)
) to write

|e_{l,1,N}|\leq\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbf{P}\left(b_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot\frac{\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})-\mathbf{E}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\right)}{N^{\frac{1}{2}g_{l,\alpha,\beta,\mathbb{K}_{1,2}}(3)}}\leq t\right)-\Phi\left(\frac{t}{\psi_{l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}\right)\right|\rightarrow 0,\hskip 8.50012pt\text{as $N\rightarrow\infty$,}

for
l=1,2
. In order to handle
e_{l,2,N}
, we use Lemma
D.18
(with
G
being the standard normal cdf). This implies

\displaystyle|e_{l,2,N}|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbf{P}\left(b_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot\frac{\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})-\mathbf{E}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\right)}{N^{\frac{1}{2}g_{l,\alpha,\beta,\mathbb{K}_{1,2}}(3)}}<t\right)-\Phi\left(\frac{t}{\psi_{l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbf{P}\left(b_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot\frac{\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})-\mathbf{E}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\right)}{N^{\frac{1}{2}g_{l,\alpha,\beta,\mathbb{K}_{1,2}}(3)}}\leq t\right)-\Phi\left(\frac{t}{\psi_{l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}\right)\right|\rightarrow 0,\hskip 8.50012pt\text{as $N\rightarrow\infty$,}

for
l=1,2
. This shows that
\mathbf{P}(D_{1,N})\leq\mathbf{P}\left(D_{l,2,N}\right)\rightarrow 0
, as
N\rightarrow\infty
, for each
l=1,2
. This implies
x_{2,N}(t)\rightarrow 0
, as
N\rightarrow\infty
, for each
t\in\mathbb{R}
(cf. (
E.67
)). It also shows that
\mathbf{P}\left(D^{c}_{1,N}\cap D_{l,2,N}\right)\leq\mathbf{P}\left(D_{l,2,N}\right)\rightarrow 0
, in the third term of (
E.67
), thus ensuring that
x_{3,N}(t)\rightarrow 0
, for each
t\in\mathbb{R}
. Continuing from (
E.67
), now we can write (using previous arguments)

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(r_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\left(\widehat{\Gamma}_{N,l}-\Gamma_{N}\right)\leq t\right)=r_{1,N}(t)+o(1)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\left(r_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\left(\widehat{\Gamma}_{N,l}-\Gamma_{N}\right)\leq t,\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})>0,S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})>0\right)+o(1).

(E.69)

Now, define the following random vectors for all
N\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{X}}_{N,l}=\left(\widehat{X}_{N,l,1},\widehat{X}_{N,l,2}\right)^{T}=\left(\frac{\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}{N^{3-2\beta}f_{l}(p_{N}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})},\frac{\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}{N^{3-3\beta}f_{l}(p_{N}:\mathbb{K}_{3})}\right)^{T},\hskip 8.50012pt\text{for $l=1,2$, and}

(E.70)

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}_{N}=(X_{N,1},X_{N,2})^{T}=\left(\frac{S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}{N^{3-2\beta}},\frac{S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}{N^{3-3\beta}}\right)^{T}.

Note that, from Assumptions
(A.2)
and
(A.3)
we can write

\bm{\theta}_{N}\equiv\mathbf{E}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{X}}_{N,l}\right)=\mathbf{E}\left(\mathbf{X}_{N}\right)=\left(\theta_{1,N},\theta_{2,N}\right)^{T}
, where,

\displaystyle\theta_{1,N}=\frac{1}{N^{3-2\beta}}\sum_{i\neq j\neq k}\pi_{\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j}}\pi_{\alpha_{i},\alpha_{k}}\to\theta_{1}\equiv\sum_{u,v,w=1}^{K}c_{u,v}c_{u,w}\cdot\lambda_{u}\lambda_{v}\lambda_{w}\in(0,\infty),\hskip 8.50012pt\text{and}

(E.71)

\displaystyle\theta_{2,N}=\frac{1}{N^{3-3\beta}}\sum_{i\neq j\neq k}\pi_{\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j}}\pi_{\alpha_{i},\alpha_{k}}\pi_{\alpha_{j},\alpha_{k}}\to\theta_{2}\equiv\sum_{u,v,w=1}^{K}c_{u,v}c_{u,w}c_{v,w}\cdot\lambda_{u}\lambda_{v}\lambda_{w}\in(0,\infty).

Define the map,

f(x,y)=\begin{cases}\frac{y}{x}&\text{for all $x\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}$ and $y\in\mathbb{R}$,}\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Note that
f(x,y)
is continuous and differentiable in
\mathcal{D}=\{(x,y):x\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\},~y\in\mathbb{R}\}
.
From (
3.31
) and (
3.36
), write,

q_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)=\frac{r_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)}{N^{\beta}},\hskip 8.50012pt\text{for $l=1,2$, for all $N\geq 1$.}

Note that using the above relation and (
E.70
), we can write

\displaystyle r_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\left(\widehat{\Gamma}_{N,l}-\Gamma_{N}\right)=q_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot N^{\beta}\cdot\left(\widehat{\Gamma}_{N,l}-\Gamma_{N}\right)=q_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)N^{\beta}\left(\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}{\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}\frac{1}{b_{l}(p_{N})}-\frac{S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}{S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}\displaystyle\right)

\displaystyle=q_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\left(\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{3})/\{N^{3-3\beta}f_{l}(p_{N}:\mathbb{K}_{3})\}}{\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})/\{N^{3-2\beta}f_{l}(p_{N}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\}}-\frac{S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{3})/N^{3-3\beta}}{S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})/N^{3-2\beta}}\displaystyle\right)=q_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\left[f\left(\widehat{\mathbf{X}}_{N,l}\right)-f(\mathbf{X}_{N})\right].

From the expression (
E.69
), using (
E.70
) and (
E.71
), we can write the following for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(r_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\left(\widehat{\Gamma}_{N,l}-\Gamma_{N}\right)\leq t,~\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})>0,~S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})>0\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\left(q_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\left[f\left(\widehat{\mathbf{X}}_{N,l}\right)-f(\mathbf{X}_{N})\right]\leq t,~\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})>0,S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{2})>0\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\left(q_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\left[f\left(\widehat{\mathbf{X}}_{N,l}\right)-f(\bm{\theta}_{N})-\left\{f(\mathbf{X}_{N})-f(\bm{\theta}_{N})\right\}\right]\leq t,~\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})>0,S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{2})>0\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\left(q_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\left[-\frac{\theta_{2,N}}{(\theta_{1,N})^{2}}\left(\widehat{X}_{N,l,1}-X_{N,1}\right)+\frac{1}{\theta_{1,N}}\left(\widehat{X}_{N,l,2}-X_{N,2}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\Upsilon}_{N,l}+\frac{1}{2}\Upsilon_{N}\right]\leq t,\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt\left.~\widehat{X}_{N,l,1}>0,~X_{N,1}>0\right),

(E.72)

where,

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\widehat{\Upsilon}_{N,l}=\widehat{\mathbf{Z}}^{T}_{N,l}\mathbf{H}_{f}\left(\widehat{\xi}_{N,l},\widehat{\nu}_{N,l}\right)\widehat{\mathbf{Z}}_{N,l},\hskip 8.50012pt\Upsilon_{N}=\mathbf{Z}^{T}_{N}\mathbf{H}_{f}\left({\xi}_{N},{\nu}_{N}\right)\mathbf{Z}_{N},

(E.73)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{Z}}_{N,l}={\left(\widehat{X}_{N,l,1}-\theta_{1,N},\widehat{X}_{N,l,2}-\theta_{2,N}\right)}^{T},\hskip 8.50012pt\mathbf{Z}_{N,l}={\left(X_{N,1}-\theta_{1,N},X_{N,2}-\theta_{2,N}\right)}^{T},

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\left(\widehat{\xi}_{N,1},\widehat{\nu}_{N,1}\right)}^{T}=\left(\theta_{1,N}+\delta_{1}\left(\widehat{X}_{N,l,1}-\theta_{1,N}\right),\theta_{2,N}+\delta_{1}\left(\widehat{X}_{N,l,2}-\theta_{2,N}\right)\right)^{T},\ \text{for some $\delta_{1}\in(0,1)$,}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\left({\xi}_{N},{\nu}_{N}\right)}^{T}={\left(\theta_{1,N}+\delta_{2}\left({X}_{N,1}-\theta_{1,N}\right),\theta_{2,N}+\delta_{2}\left({X}_{N,2}-\theta_{2,N}\right)\right)}^{T},\hskip 8.50012pt\text{for some $\delta_{2}\in(0,1)$, and}

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}_{f}(x,y)=\begin{pmatrix}-\frac{2y}{x^{3}}&\frac{1}{x^{2}}\\
\frac{1}{x^{2}}&0\end{pmatrix}.

Next, let us define the random variables (cf. (
E.70
)),

\displaystyle T_{N,l}\left(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3}\right)=-\frac{\theta_{2,N}}{(\theta_{1,N})^{2}}\left(\widehat{X}_{N,l,1}-X_{N,1}\right)+\frac{1}{\theta_{1,N}}\left(\widehat{X}_{N,l,2}-X_{N,2}\right),\hskip 8.50012pt\text{for all $N\geq 1$, and $l=1,2$.}

(E.74)

For any
\epsilon>0
, consider events,
A_{l,1,N}=\left\{\left|\widehat{X}_{N,l,1}-\theta_{1,N}\right|\leq\epsilon\right\}
,
1,2
, and
B_{1,N}=\left\{|X_{N,1}-\theta_{1,N}|\leq\epsilon\right\}
, and
C_{1,N}=\left\{\widehat{X}_{N,l,1}>0,X_{N,1}>0\right\}
. Thus, by construction
A_{l,1,N}\cap B_{1,N}\subseteq C_{1,N}
. Note that, using Lemma
D.13
, we can say that for
l=1,2
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(A^{c}_{l,1,N}\right)=\mathbf{P}\left(\left|\widehat{X}_{N,l,1}-\theta_{1,N}\right|>\epsilon\right)=o(1)\hskip 8.50012pt\text{and}\hskip 8.50012pt\mathbf{P}\left(B^{c}_{N,1}\right)=\mathbf{P}\left(|{X}_{N,1}-\theta_{1,N}|>\epsilon\right)=o(1).

Then, from the expression (
E.72
), we can write

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(q_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\left[T_{N,l}\left(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\Upsilon}_{N,l}+\frac{1}{2}\Upsilon_{N}\right]\leq t,\widehat{X}_{N,l,1}>0,X_{N,1}>0\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\left(q_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\left[T_{N,l}\left(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\Upsilon}_{N,l}+\frac{1}{2}\Upsilon_{N}\right]\leq t,A_{l,1,N}\cap B_{1,N}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\mathbf{P}\left(q_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\left[T_{N,l}\left(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\Upsilon}_{N,l}+\frac{1}{2}\Upsilon_{N}\right]\leq t,B_{1,N}\cap C_{1,N}~|~A_{l,1,N}^{c}\right)\mathbf{P}(A_{l,1,N}^{c})

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\mathbf{P}\left(q_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\left[T_{N,l}\left(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\Upsilon}_{N,l}+\frac{1}{2}\Upsilon_{N}\right]\leq t,A_{l,1,N}\cap C_{1,N}~|~B_{1,N}^{c}\right)\mathbf{P}(B_{1,N}^{c})

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\mathbf{P}\left(q_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\left[T_{N,l}\left(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\Upsilon}_{N,l}+\frac{1}{2}\Upsilon_{N}\right]\leq t,A^{c}_{l,1,N}\cap C_{1,N}~|~B_{1,N}^{c}\right)\mathbf{P}(B_{1,N}^{c})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\left(q_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot T_{N,l}\left(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3}\right)+\frac{q_{N,l}(\alpha,\beta)}{2}\left\{\widehat{\Upsilon}_{N,l}+\Upsilon_{N}\right\}\leq t,~A_{l,1,N}\cap B_{1,N}\right)+o(1).

(E.75)

From (
E.71
), we know
\theta_{1,N}\to\theta_{1}
. Fix
\epsilon=\theta_{1}/2
. Then there exists a
M\geq 1
, such that for all
N>M
, when the event
A_{l,1,N}\cap B_{1,N}
happens, then

\displaystyle\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}<\theta_{1,N}+\delta_{1}\bigl(\widehat{X}_{N,l,1}-\theta_{1,N}\bigr)<\frac{3\theta_{1}}{2}\hskip 8.50012pt\text{and}\hskip 8.50012pt\frac{\theta_{1}}{2}<\theta_{1,N}+\delta_{2}\bigl({X}_{N,1}-\theta_{1,N}\bigr)<\frac{3\theta_{1}}{2}.

(E.76)

Now consider the case of induced network formation (
l=1
).
Recall the definition of
R_{1,j}
,
j=1,\ldots,5
, from (
3.26
). Using Lemma
D.12
, there exists an
M_{5}
such that for all
N>M_{5}
, we can write

\displaystyle a_{4}\cdot N^{5(1-\alpha)-4\beta}\leq\sigma^{2}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\leq a_{5}\cdot N^{5(1-\alpha)-4\beta}\hskip 8.50012pt\text{when $(\alpha,\beta)\in\bigcup_{i=1}^{5}R_{1,i}$}\hskip 8.50012pt~\text{and}

\displaystyle a_{6}\cdot N^{5(1-\alpha)-6\beta}\leq\sigma^{2}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})\leq a_{7}\cdot N^{5(1-\alpha)-6\beta}\hskip 8.50012pt\text{when $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,1}\cup R_{1,2}$, and}

(E.77)

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})\geq\begin{cases}a_{8}\cdot N^{3(1-\alpha)-3\beta}\hskip 8.50012pt~&\text{when $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,3}$},\\
a_{9}\hskip 8.50012pt~&\text{when $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,4}\cup R_{1,5}$},\end{cases}

where,
a_{4},a_{5},a_{6},a_{7},a_{8},a_{9}
, are finite positive constants arising from Lemma
D.12
. Therefore, using the definition of
f_{1}(p_{N}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})
(cf. (
E.65
)), we can write

\displaystyle\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})

\displaystyle\equiv\mathbf{Var}\left(\widehat{X}_{N,1,1}-X_{N,1}\right)=\frac{\mathbf{Var}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})-f_{1}(p_{N}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\right)}{N^{6-4\beta}f^{2}_{1}(p_{N}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}=\frac{\mathbf{Var}\left(T_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\right)}{N^{6(1-\alpha)-4\beta}c^{6}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma^{2}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}{c^{6}\cdot N^{6(1-\alpha)-4\beta}},\hskip 8.50012pt\text{and}

\displaystyle\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})

\displaystyle\equiv\mathbf{Var}\left(\widehat{X}_{N,1,2}-X_{N,2}\right)=\frac{\mathbf{Var}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{2})-f_{1}(p_{N}:\mathbb{K}_{3})S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{3})\right)}{N^{6-6\beta}f^{2}_{1}(p_{N}:\mathbb{K}_{3})}=\frac{\mathbf{Var}\left(T_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})\right)}{N^{6(1-\alpha)-6\beta}c^{6}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma^{2}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}{c^{6}\cdot N^{6(1-\alpha)-6\beta}}.

(E.78)

Using (
E.77
) and (
E.78
), we can claim that for all
N>M_{5}
,
