\displaystyle\frac{a_{4}}{a_{7}}\leq\frac{\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}{\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}\leq\frac{a_{5}}{a_{6}},\hskip 8.50012pt\text{if $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,1}\cup R_{1,2}$, and}

\displaystyle\frac{\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}{\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}\leq\begin{cases}\frac{a_{5}}{a_{8}}\cdot N^{2\left(1-\alpha-\frac{\beta}{2/3}\right)}~&\text{when $(\alpha,\beta)\in{R}_{1,3}$,}\\
\frac{a_{5}}{a_{9}}\frac{1}{N^{\beta}}~&\text{when $(\alpha,\beta)\in{R}_{1,4}$,}\\
\frac{a_{5}}{a_{9}}\frac{1}{N}&\text{when $(\alpha,\beta)\in{R}_{1,5}$.}\end{cases}

(E.79)

This implies that the limiting behavior of
T_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{1,3})
(see (
E.74
)) is jointly influenced by the wedge- and triangle count based terms
(\widehat{X}_{N,1,1}-X_{N,1})
and
(\widehat{X}_{N,1,2}-X_{N,2})
, when
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,1}\cup R_{1,2}
, and is influenced only by the triangle based term
(\widehat{X}_{N,1,2}-X_{N,2})
, when
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,3}\cup R_{1,4}\cup R_{1,5}
. Thus, we need to analyze separately the limiting behavior of
T_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})
in these regions. Due to (
E.75
), firstly we obtain the asymptotic distribution of

\displaystyle q_{N,1}(\alpha,\beta)(\alpha,\beta)\cdot T_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})=q_{N,1}(\alpha,\beta)\left[-\frac{\theta_{2,N}}{(\theta_{1,N})^{2}}\left(\widehat{X}_{N,1,1}-X_{N,1}\right)+\frac{1}{\theta_{1,N}}\left(\widehat{X}_{N,1,2}-X_{N,2}\right)\right].

(E.80)

In case of
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,1}\cup R_{1,2}
, using Proposition
D.17
, and using (
E.71
), we can write,

\displaystyle N^{\gamma(\alpha,\beta)}\mathbf{Var}\!\bigl(T_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})\bigr)\;\to\;\rho^{2}_{1,j}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3}),\hskip 8.50012pt\text{if $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,j}$, for $j=1,2$,}

where

\gamma(\alpha,\beta)=\begin{cases}1&\text{if $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,1}$,}\\
(1-\alpha)&\text{if $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,2}$,}\end{cases}

and
\rho^{2}_{1,j}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})
,
j=1,2
, are defined in (
3.28
) and (
3.29
) respectively. In addition, using Proposition
D.17
we can say that

N^{\gamma(\alpha,\beta)/2}\,T_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}N\left(0,\rho^{2}_{1,j}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})\right),\hskip 17.00024pt\text{if $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,j}$, for $j=1,2$.}

Thus, when
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,1}\cup R_{1,2}
, we will have
q_{N,1}(\alpha,\beta)=N^{(1-\alpha)/2}
, for all
N\geq 1
, for
\alpha\in[0,1)
. Now consider the case where
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,3}\cup R_{1,4}\cup R_{1,5}
. From (
E.80
) we can write

\displaystyle\frac{T_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\theta_{2,N}}{(\theta_{1,N})^{2}}\frac{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}\frac{\left(\widehat{X}_{N,1,1}-X_{N,1}\right)}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}+\frac{1}{\theta_{1,N}}\frac{1}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}\left(\widehat{X}_{N,1,2}-X_{N,2}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\theta_{2,N}}{(\theta_{1,N})^{2}}\frac{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}T_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})+\frac{1}{\theta_{1,N}}T_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3}).

Now using (
E.79
) and Corollary
3.2
, we can write

\displaystyle\frac{T_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\theta_{2,N}}{(\theta_{1,N})^{2}}\cdot O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\cdot O_{P}(1)+\frac{1}{\theta_{1,N}}T_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow~q_{N,1}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot T_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\theta_{2,N}}{(\theta_{1,N})^{2}}\cdot O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\cdot O_{P}(1)\cdot\{q_{N,1}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})\}

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\frac{q_{N,1}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}{\theta_{1,N}}\cdot T_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3}).

(E.81)

From Corollary (
3.2
),
T_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}N(0,1)
. Using Lemma
D.12
and for
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,3}
, taking

, we can say that

\displaystyle N^{3(1-\alpha-\beta)/2}\cdot\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})=\frac{\sigma_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}{N^{3(1-\alpha-\beta)/2}}=O(1).

Thus, using the above arguments for
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,3}
and using Slutsky’s Theorem, we can say that

\displaystyle N^{3(1-\alpha-\beta)/2}\cdot T_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}N(0,~\rho^{2}_{1,3}(\mathbb{K}_{3})),\hskip 8.50012pt\text{as $N\to\infty$,}

where

\displaystyle\frac{N^{3(1-\alpha-\beta)/2}\cdot\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}{\theta_{1,N}}\to\rho_{1,3}(\mathbb{K}_{3}),

(E.82)

and
\rho_{1,3}(\mathbb{K}_{3})
defined in (
3.30
). In case of
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,4}\cup R_{1,5}
, using Theorem
3.4
, we can write

\displaystyle T_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}\frac{V_{1,\mathbb{K}_{3}}-c^{3}\cdot\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:\mathbb{K}_{3})}{[c^{3}\cdot\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:\mathbb{K}_{3})]^{1/2}},\hskip 17.00024pt{}\text{for $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,4}$, and}

\displaystyle T_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}\frac{\left(1-c^{3}\right)\cdot U_{1,1,\mathbb{K}_{3}}-c^{3}\cdot U_{2,1,\mathbb{K}_{3}}}{[c^{3}\cdot\{1-c^{3}\}\cdot\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:\mathbb{K}_{3})]^{1/2}},\hskip 17.00024pt{}\text{when $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,5}$,}

where,
V_{1,\mathbb{K}_{3}}
,
U_{1,1,\mathbb{K}_{3}}
and

are defined in Theorem
3.4
, and
\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda},\mathbb{K}_{3})
is defined in (

). Note that, in this case,
q_{N,1}(\alpha,\beta)=1
for
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,4}\cup R_{1,5}
. Thus, from (
E.81
) and Slutsky’s Theorem, we can write,

\displaystyle T_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}V_{1,\alpha,\beta},~\text{for $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,4}\cup R_{1,5}$.}

where
V_{1,\alpha,\beta}
is defined in (
3.31
) and note that as
N\to\infty
, we can write,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})=\sigma^{2}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})\to\begin{cases}c^{3}\cdot\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:\mathbb{K}_{3}),&~\text{if $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,4}$,}\\
c^{3}\cdot\{1-c^{3}\}\cdot\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:\mathbb{K}_{3}),&~\text{if $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,5}$.}\end{cases}

(E.83)

Next we will show that for
(\alpha,\beta)\in\bigcup_{i=1}^{5}R_{1,i}
the remainder terms
\widehat{\Upsilon}_{N,1}
and
\Upsilon_{N,1}
in (
E.75
) converge to zero in probability. Firstly, note that, from (

) and Lemma

, we can write

\displaystyle\widehat{\Upsilon}_{N,1}

\displaystyle=\frac{2(\theta_{2,N}+\delta(\widehat{X}_{N,1,2}-\theta_{2,N}))}{(\theta_{1,N}+\delta(\widehat{X}_{N,1,1}-\theta_{1,N}))^{3}}\left\{\left(\widehat{X}_{N,1,1}-\theta_{1,N}\right)\right\}\cdot\left(\widehat{X}_{N,1,1}-\theta_{1,N}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt{}+\frac{2}{(\theta_{1,N}+\delta(\widehat{X}_{N,1,1}-\theta_{1,N}))^{2}}\left\{\left(\widehat{X}_{N,1,1}-\theta_{1,N}\right)\right\}\left(\widehat{X}_{N,1,2}-\theta_{2,N}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{2(\theta_{2,N}+\delta\cdot o_{P}(1))}{(\theta_{1,N}+\delta(\widehat{X}_{N,1,1}-\theta_{1,N}))^{3}}\left\{\left(\widehat{X}_{N,1,1}-\theta_{1,N}\right)\right\}\cdot o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt{}+\frac{2}{(\theta_{1,N}+\delta(\widehat{X}_{N,1,1}-\theta_{1,N}))^{2}}\left\{\left(\widehat{X}_{N,1,1}-\theta_{1,N}\right)\right\}\cdot o_{P}(1).

(E.84)

Similarly, using Lemma
D.13
, we can say that

\displaystyle{\Upsilon}_{N,1}

\displaystyle=\frac{2(\theta_{2,N}+\delta\cdot o_{P}(1))}{(\theta_{1,N}+\delta({X}_{N,1}-\theta_{1,N}))^{3}}\left\{({X}_{N,1}-\theta_{1,N})\right\}\cdot o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt{}+\frac{2}{(\theta_{1,N}+\delta({X}_{N,1}-\theta_{1,N}))^{2}}\left\{({X}_{N,1}-\theta_{1,N})\right\}\cdot o_{P}(1).

(E.85)

A direct computation of the variances of
S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})
and
S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{3})
shows that there exists an
M_{6}\geq 1
such that for all
N>M_{6}
, we can write

\displaystyle\omega^{2}_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})=\mathbf{Var}(X_{N,1})=\frac{\mathbf{Var}(S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2}))}{N^{6-4\beta}}\leq a_{10}\cdot\frac{N^{4-3\beta}}{N^{6-4\beta}}=\frac{a_{10}}{N^{2-\beta}}\hskip 8.50012pt\text{if $\beta\in[0,1]$, and}

\displaystyle\omega^{2}_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{3})=\mathbf{Var}(X_{N,2})=\frac{\mathbf{Var}(S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{3}))}{N^{6-6\beta}}\leq\begin{cases}&a_{11}\cdot\frac{N^{4-5\beta}}{N^{6-6\beta}}=\frac{a_{10}}{N^{2-\beta}}\hskip 8.50012pt\text{if $\beta\in[0,1/2)$},\\
&a_{12}\cdot\frac{N^{3-3\beta}}{N^{6-6\beta}}=\frac{a_{11}}{N^{3(1-\beta)}}\hskip 8.50012pt\text{if $\beta\in[1/2,1)$},\\
&a_{13}\hskip 8.50012pt\text{if $\beta=1$,}\end{cases}

(E.86)

where,
a_{10}=a_{10}(\beta,\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})
and
a_{i}=a_{i}(\beta,\mathbb{K}_{3},\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})
,
i=11,12,13
, are finite positive constants depending on the graph
H
and the model parameters. When
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,1}\cup R_{1,2}
, then
q_{N,1}(\alpha,\beta)=N^{\frac{1}{2}(1-\alpha)}
. Then from the expression (
E.84
), using (
E.77
),(
E.78
), (
E.86
) and Lemma
D.13
, we can write

\displaystyle N^{\frac{1}{2}(1-\alpha)}(\widehat{X}_{N,1,1}-\theta_{1,N})

\displaystyle=\frac{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}{N^{-\frac{1}{2}(1-\alpha)}}\cdot T_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})+\frac{\omega_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}{N^{-\frac{1}{2}(1-\alpha)}}\cdot\frac{(X_{1,N}-\theta_{1,N})}{\omega(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)O_{P}(1)+\frac{O_{P}(1)}{N^{\frac{1}{2}(1-\beta+\alpha)}}=O_{P}(1).

From (
E.76
), on the event
A_{1,1,N}\cap B_{1,N}
, the denominator terms within
\widehat{\Upsilon}_{N,1}
and
\Upsilon_{N}
remain bounded away from zero. Hence, we can say that
N^{\frac{1}{2}(1-\alpha)}\widehat{\Upsilon}_{N,1}=o_{P}(1)
, when
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,1}\cup R_{1,2}
. Similarly, using (
E.86
) and Lemma
D.13
, from (
E.85
), we can say that

\displaystyle N^{\frac{1}{2}(1-\alpha)}(X_{N,1}-\theta_{1,N})=\frac{\omega_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}{N^{-\frac{1}{2}(1-\alpha)}}\frac{X_{N,1}-\theta_{1,N}}{\omega_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}=\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{2}(1-\beta+\alpha)}}\cdot O_{P}(1).

Similarly, from (
E.76
) we can say that
N^{\frac{1}{2}(1-\alpha)}{\Upsilon}_{N,1}=o_{P}(1)
, in the region
R_{1,1}\cup R_{1,2}
. It remains to show that
q_{N,1}(\alpha,\beta)(\widehat{\Upsilon}_{N,1}+\Upsilon_{N,1})=o_{P}(1)
, when
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,3}\cup R_{1,4}\cup R_{1,5}
. First note that, using (
E.78
) and (
E.86
), for
N>\max\{M_{5},M_{6}\}
, we can say the following

\displaystyle\frac{\omega^{2}_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}{\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}\leq\begin{cases}a_{14}\cdot N^{(2-3\beta-2\alpha)-(1-\beta)-\alpha}&\text{when $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,3}$,}\\
a_{15}\cdot N^{-(2-\beta)}&\text{when $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,4}\cup R_{1,5}$.}\end{cases}

(E.87)

Then, from (
E.84
) and using (
E.79
), we can write

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{\Upsilon}_{N,1}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}

\displaystyle=\frac{2(\theta_{2,N}+\delta(\widehat{X}_{N,1,2}-\theta_{2,N}))}{(\theta_{1,N}+\delta(\widehat{X}_{N,2,1}-\theta_{1,N}))^{3}}\left\{\frac{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}\left(\frac{\widehat{X}_{N,1,1}-\theta_{1,N}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}\right)\right\}\cdot o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt{}+\frac{2}{(\theta_{1,N}+\delta(\widehat{X}_{N,1,1}-\theta_{1,N}))^{2}}\left\{\frac{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}\left(\frac{\widehat{X}_{N,1,1}-\theta_{1,N}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}\right)\right\}\cdot o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle=\frac{2(\theta_{2,N}+\delta\cdot o_{P}(1))}{(\theta_{1,N}+\delta(\widehat{X}_{N,1,1}-\theta_{1,N}))^{3}}\left\{O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\cdot\left(\frac{\widehat{X}_{N,1,1}-\theta_{1,N}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}\right)\right\}\cdot o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt{}+\frac{2}{(\theta_{1,N}+\delta(\widehat{X}_{N,1,1}-\theta_{1,N}))^{2}}\left\{O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\cdot\left(\frac{\widehat{X}_{N,1,1}-\theta_{1,N}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}\right)\right\}o_{P}(1).

In the above expression and on the event
A_{1,1,N}\cap B_{1,N}
(cf. (
E.76
)), the denominator terms are bounded away from zero. Using Corollary
3.2
, Lemma
D.13
, (
E.86
) and (
E.78
), we can say that, for
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,3}\cup R_{1,4}\cup R_{1,5}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{X}_{N,1,1}-\theta_{1,N}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}=T_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})+\frac{\omega_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}\left(\frac{X_{N,1}-\theta_{1,N}}{\omega_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}\right)=O_{P}(1)+\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{2}(1-\beta+\alpha)}}\cdot O_{P}(1)=O_{P}(1).

Hence, we can say that in the region
R_{1,3}\cup R_{1,4}\cup R_{1,5}
,
\frac{\widehat{\Upsilon}_{N,1}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}=o_{P}(1)
. From (
E.82
) and (
E.83
) we know that
q_{N,1}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})=O(1)
when
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,3}\cup R_{1,4}\cup R_{1,5}
. Thus, in this region
q_{N,1}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot\widehat{\Upsilon}_{N,1}=o_{P}(1)
. Similarly, from (
E.85
), using (
E.87
) and Lemma
D.13
, we can write

\displaystyle\frac{{\Upsilon}_{N,1}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}

\displaystyle=\frac{2(\theta_{2,N}+\delta\cdot o_{P}(1))}{(\theta_{1,N}+\delta({X}_{N,1}-\theta_{1,N}))^{3}}\left\{\frac{\omega_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}\left(\frac{{X}_{N,1}-\theta_{1,N}}{\omega_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}\right)\right\}\cdot o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt{}+\frac{2}{(\theta_{1,N}+\delta({X}_{N,1}-\theta_{1,N}))^{2}}\left\{\frac{\omega_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}\left(\frac{{X}_{N,1}-\theta_{1,N}}{\omega_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}\right)\right\}\cdot o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle=\frac{2(\theta_{2,N}+\delta\cdot o_{P}(1))}{(\theta_{1,N}+\delta({X}_{N,1}-\theta_{1,N}))^{3}}\left\{O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)O_{P}(1)\right\}\cdot o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt{}+\frac{2}{(\theta_{1,N}+\delta({X}_{N,1}-\theta_{1,N}))^{2}}\left\{O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)O_{P}(1)\right\}\cdot o_{P}(1)=o_{P}(1).

Therefore, using (
E.76
), we can say that
q_{N,1}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot\Upsilon_{N,1}=o_{P}(1)
, when
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,3}\cup R_{1,4}\cup R_{1,5}
. Hence, we show that for
(\alpha,\beta)\in\bigcup_{i=1}^{5}R_{1,i}
, the remainder terms in the expression (
E.75
) converge to zero in probability. Therefore, from the expression (
E.75
), and using Slutsky’s theorem, we can write,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(q_{N,1}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot T_{N,1}\left(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3}\right)+\frac{q_{N,1}(\alpha,\beta)}{2}\left\{\widehat{\Delta}_{N,1}+\Delta_{N}\right\}\leq t,~A_{1,1,N}\cap B_{1,N}\right)+o(1)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\left(q_{N,1}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot T_{N,1}\left(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3}\right)\leq t,~A_{1,1,N}\cap B_{1,N}\right)+o(1)=\mathbf{P}\left(q_{N,1}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot T_{N,1}\left(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3}\right)\leq t\right)+o(1).

This completes the proof of Theorem (
3.5
).
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.6
.

We consider ego-centric network formation (
l=2
). Recall the definition of
R_{2,j},~j=1,\ldots,6
, from (
3.26
).
Using Lemma
D.12
, there exists an
M_{7}
such that for all
N>M_{7}
, we can write

\displaystyle a_{16}\cdot N^{5-\alpha-4\beta}\leq\sigma^{2}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\leq a_{17}\cdot N^{5-\alpha-4\beta},~\text{when $(\alpha,\beta)\in\bigcup_{i=1}^{6}R_{2,i}$,}~\text{and}

\displaystyle a_{18}\cdot N^{5-3\alpha-6\beta}\sigma^{2}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3})\leq a_{19}\cdot N^{5-3\alpha-6\beta},~\text{when $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,1}\cup R_{2,2}$}

(E.88)

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3})\geq\begin{cases}a_{20}\cdot N^{4-2\alpha-5\beta},~&\text{when $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,3}$},\\
a_{21}\cdot N^{3-2\alpha-3\beta},~&\text{when $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,4}$},\\
a_{22},~&\text{when $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,5}\cup R_{2,6}$},\end{cases}

where
a_{16},a_{17},a_{18},a_{19},a_{20},a_{21},a_{22}
, are finite positive constants arising from Lemma
D.12
). Therefore, using the definition of
f_{2}(p_{N}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})
and
f_{2}(p_{N}:\mathbb{K}_{3})
(cf. (
E.65
)), we can write

\displaystyle\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})=\mathbf{Var}\left(\widehat{X}_{N,2,1}-X_{N,1}\right)=\frac{\mathbf{Var}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})-f_{2}(p_{N}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\right)}{N^{6-4\beta}f^{2}_{2}(p_{N}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbf{Var}\left(T_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\right)}{N^{6-2\alpha-4\beta}c^{2}(1+(1-c/N^{\alpha})(c/N^{\alpha}))}=\frac{\sigma^{2}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}{N^{6-2\alpha-4\beta}c^{2}(1+(1-c/N^{\alpha})(c/N^{\alpha}))}\hskip 8.50012pt\text{and,}

\displaystyle~\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3})=\mathbf{Var}\left(\widehat{X}_{N,2,2}-X_{N,2}\right)=\frac{\mathbf{Var}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3})-f_{2}(p_{N}:\mathbb{K}_{3})S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{3})\right)}{N^{6-6\beta}f^{2}_{2}(p_{N}:\mathbb{K}_{3})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbf{Var}\left(T_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3})\right)}{N^{6-4\alpha-6\beta}c^{4}(3+2(c/N^{\alpha}))}=\frac{\sigma^{2}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}{c^{4}\cdot N^{6-4\alpha-6\beta}(3+2(c/N^{\alpha}))}.

(E.89)

Using (
E.88
) and (
E.89
), we can claim that for all
N>M_{7}
,

\displaystyle\frac{a_{16}}{a_{19}}\leq\frac{\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}{\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}\leq\frac{a_{17}}{a_{18}},\hskip 8.50012pt\text{if $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,1}\cup R_{2,2}$,~~ and,}

\displaystyle\frac{\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}{\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}\leq\begin{cases}\frac{a_{17}}{a_{20}}N^{-\left(1-\alpha-\beta\right)},~&\text{when $(\alpha,\beta)\in{R}_{2,3}$}\\
\frac{a_{17}}{a_{21}}N^{2(1-\frac{\alpha}{2}-\frac{3}{2}\beta)},~&\text{when $(\alpha,\beta)\in{R}_{2,4}$}\\
\frac{a_{17}}{a_{22}}\frac{1}{N^{1-\alpha}},~&\text{when $(\alpha,\beta)\in{R}_{2,5}$.}\\
\frac{a_{17}}{a_{22}}\frac{1}{N},&\text{when $(\alpha,\beta)\in{R}_{2,6}$}.\end{cases}

(E.90)

This implies that the limiting behavior of
T_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})
(see (
E.74
)) is jointly influenced by the wedge and triangle count based terms
(\widehat{X}_{N,2,1}-X_{N,1})
and
(\widehat{X}_{N,2,2}-X_{N,2})
, when
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,1}\cup R_{2,2}
, and is influence only by triangle based term
(\widehat{X}_{N,2,1}-X_{N,2})
when
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,3}\cup R_{2,4}\cup R_{2,5}\cup R_{2,6}
. Thus, we separately analyze the limiting behavior of
T_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})
in these regions. Due to (
E.75
), firstly we obtain the asymptotic distribution of

\displaystyle q_{N,2}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot T_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})=q_{N,2}\left[-\frac{\theta_{2,N}}{(\theta_{1,N})^{2}}\left(\widehat{X}_{N,2,1}-X_{N,1}\right)+\frac{1}{\theta_{1,N}}\left(\widehat{X}_{N,2,2}-X_{N,2}\right)\right].

(E.91)

In case of
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,1}\cup R_{2,2}
, using Proposition
D.17
, and using the fact (
E.71
), we can write,

\displaystyle N^{\zeta(\alpha,\beta)}\mathbf{Var}\!\bigl(T_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})\bigr)\;\to\;\rho^{2}_{2,j}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3}),\hskip 17.00024pt(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,j},\ j=1,2,

where

\displaystyle\zeta(\alpha,\beta)=\begin{cases}1,&\hskip 8.50012pt\text{if $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,1}$,}\\
(1-\alpha),&\hskip 8.50012pt\text{if $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,2}$,}\end{cases}

and
\rho^{2}_{2,j}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})
defined in (
3.33
) and (
3.34
) respectively. In addition, using Proposition (
D.17
) we can say that

N^{\zeta(\alpha,\beta)/2}\,T_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}N\left(0,\rho^{2}_{2,j}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})\right),\hskip 17.00024pt\text{if}~~(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,j},\ j=1,2.

Thus, when
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,1}\cup R_{2,2}
, we will have
q_{N,2}(\alpha,\beta)=N^{\frac{1}{2}(1-\alpha)}
, for all
N\geq 1
, for
\alpha\in[0,1)
. Now consider the case where
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,3}\cup R_{2,4}\cup R_{2,5}\cup R_{2,6}
. From (
E.91
), we can write

\displaystyle\frac{T_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\theta_{2,N}}{(\theta_{1,N})^{2}}\frac{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}\frac{\left(\widehat{X}_{N,2,1}-X_{N,1}\right)}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}+\frac{1}{\theta_{1,N}}\frac{1}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}\left(\widehat{X}_{N,2,2}-X_{N,2}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\theta_{2,N}}{(\theta_{1,N})^{2}}\frac{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}\cdot T_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})+\frac{1}{\theta_{1,N}}\cdot T_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3}).

Now using (
E.90
) and Corollary
3.2
, we can write

\displaystyle\frac{T_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\theta_{2,N}}{(\theta_{1,N})^{2}}\cdot O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\cdot O_{P}(1)+\frac{1}{\theta_{1,N}}\cdot T_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow~q_{N,2}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot T_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\theta_{2,N}}{(\theta_{2,N})^{2}}\cdot O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\cdot O_{P}(1)\cdot\{q_{N,2}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3})\}

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\frac{q_{N,2}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}{\theta_{1,N}}\cdot T_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3}).

(E.92)

From Corollary (
3.2
),
T_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}N(0,1)
. Using Lemma
D.12
and for

\displaystyle q_{N,2}(\alpha,\beta)=\begin{cases}N^{(4-2\alpha-5\beta)/2},&~\text{if $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,3}$}\\
N^{(3-2\alpha-3\beta)/2},&~\text{if $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,4}$,}\end{cases}

we can say that

\displaystyle N^{(4-2\alpha-5\beta)/2}\cdot\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3})=\frac{\sigma_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}{N^{(2-2\alpha-\beta)/2}}=O(1),~\text{and}~N^{(3-2\alpha-3\beta)/2}\cdot\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3})=\frac{\sigma_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}{N^{(3-2\alpha-3\beta)/2}}=O(1).

Thus, using above arguments for
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,3}\cup R_{2,4}
and using Slutsky’s Theorem, we can say that

\displaystyle N^{(4-2\alpha-5\beta)/2}\cdot T_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}N(0,~\rho^{2}_{2,3}(\mathbb{K}_{3})),~\text{and}

\displaystyle N^{3-2\alpha-3\beta)/2}\cdot T_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}N(0,~\rho^{2}_{2,4}(\mathbb{K}_{3}))~\text{as $N\to\infty$,}

where,

\displaystyle\frac{N^{(4-2\alpha-5\beta)/2}\cdot\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}{\theta_{1,N}}\to\rho_{1,3}(\mathbb{K}_{3})\hskip 8.50012pt\text{and}\hskip 8.50012pt\frac{N^{3-2\alpha-3\beta)/2}\cdot\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}{\theta_{1,N}}\to\rho_{1,3}(\mathbb{K}_{3}),

(E.93)

where
\rho_{2,3}(\mathbb{K}_{3})
and
\rho_{2,4}(\mathbb{K}_{3})
are defined in (
3.35
). In case of
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,5}\cup R_{2,6}
, using the Theorem
3.4
, we can write

\displaystyle T_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}\frac{V_{2,\mathbb{K}_{3}}-3c^{2}\cdot\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:\mathbb{K}_{3})}{[3c^{2}\cdot\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:\mathbb{K}_{3})]^{1/2}}\hskip 17.00024pt{}\text{for $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,5}$, and,}

\displaystyle T_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}\frac{\left(1-(3c^{2}-2c^{3})\right)\cdot U_{1,2,\mathbb{K}_{3}}-(3c^{2}-2p^{3})\cdot U_{2,2,\mathbb{K}_{3}}}{[(3c^{2}-2c^{3})\cdot\{1-c^{3}\}\cdot\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:\mathbb{K}_{3})]^{1/2}},\hskip 17.00024pt{}\text{when $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,6}$,}

where
V_{2,\mathbb{K}_{3}}
,
U_{1,2,\mathbb{K}_{3}}
and
U_{2,2,\mathbb{K}_{3}}
is defined in Theorem
3.4
, and
\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda},\mathbb{K}_{3})
is defined in (
3.18
). Note that in this case,
q_{N,2}(\alpha,\beta)=1
for
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,5}\cup R_{2,6}
. Thus, from (
E.81
) and Slutsky’s Theorem, we can write,

\displaystyle T_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}V_{2,\alpha,\beta},~\text{for $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,5}\cup R_{2,6}$.}

where
V_{2,\alpha,\beta}
is defined in (
3.36
) and note that as
N\to\infty
, we can write,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3})=\sigma^{2}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3})\to\begin{cases}3c^{2}\cdot\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:\mathbb{K}_{3}),&~\text{if $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,5}$,}\\
(3c^{2}-2c^{3})\cdot\{1-(3c^{2}-2c^{3})\}\cdot\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:\mathbb{K}_{3}),&~\text{if $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,6}$.}\end{cases}

(E.94)

Next, we will show that for
(\alpha,\beta)\in\bigcup_{i=1}^{6}R_{2,i}
the remainder term
\widehat{\Upsilon}_{N,2}
in (
E.75
) converges to zero in probability. First, note that from (
E.73
) and Lemma
D.13
, we can write

\displaystyle\widehat{\Upsilon}_{N,2}=\frac{2(\theta_{2,N}+\delta(\widehat{X}_{N,2,2}-\theta_{2,N}))}{(\theta_{1,N}+\delta(\widehat{X}_{N,2,1}-\theta_{1,N}))^{3}}\left\{\left(\widehat{X}_{N,2,1}-\theta_{1,N}\right)\right\}\cdot\left(\widehat{X}_{N,2,1}-\theta_{1,N}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt{}+\frac{2}{(\theta_{1,N}+\delta(\widehat{X}_{N,2,1}-\theta_{1,N}))^{2}}\left\{\left(\widehat{X}_{N,2,1}-\theta_{1,N}\right)\right\}\left(\widehat{X}_{N,2,2}-\theta_{2,N}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{2(\theta_{2,N}+\delta\cdot o_{P}(1))}{(\theta_{1,N}+\delta(\widehat{X}_{N,2,1}-\theta_{1,N}))^{3}}\left\{\left(\widehat{X}_{N,2,1}-\theta_{1,N}\right)\right\}\cdot o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt{}+\frac{2}{(\theta_{1,N}+\delta(\widehat{X}_{N,2,1}-\theta_{1,N}))^{2}}\left\{\left(\widehat{X}_{N,2,1}-\theta_{1,N}\right)\right\}\cdot o_{P}(1).

(E.95)

When
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,1}\cup R_{2,2}
, then
q_{N,2}(\alpha,\beta)=N^{\frac{1}{2}(1-\alpha)}
. Then from the expression (
E.95
), using (
E.88
),(
E.89
), (
E.86
) and Lemma
D.13
, we can write

\displaystyle N^{\frac{1}{2}(1-\alpha)}(\widehat{X}_{N,2,1}-\theta_{1,N})

\displaystyle=\frac{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}{N^{-\frac{1}{2}(1-\alpha)}}T_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})+\frac{\omega_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}{N^{-\frac{1}{2}(1-\alpha)}}\frac{X_{1,N}-\theta_{1,N}}{\omega(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}=O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)O_{P}(1)+\frac{O_{P}(1)}{N^{\frac{1}{2}(1-\beta+\alpha)}}

\displaystyle=O_{P}(1).

From (
E.76
), on the event
A_{1,2,N}\cap B_{1,N}
, the denominator term with in
\widehat{\Upsilon}_{N,2}
remains bounded away from zero. Hence, we can say that
N^{\frac{1}{2}(1-\alpha)}\widehat{\Upsilon}_{N,2}=o_{P}(1)
, when
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,1}\cup R_{2,2}
.
It remains to show that
q_{N,2}(\alpha,\beta)\widehat{\Upsilon}_{N,2}=o_{P}(1)
, when
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,3}\cup R_{2,4}\cup R_{2,5}\cup R_{2,6}
. First note that, using (
E.78
) and (
E.86
), for
N>\max\{M_{6},M_{7}\}
, we can say the following

\displaystyle\frac{\omega^{2}_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}{\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}\leq\begin{cases}a_{23}\cdot N^{2(1-\alpha-2\beta)},~&\text{when $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,3}$},\\
a_{24}\cdot N^{1-2\alpha-2\beta},~&\text{when $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,4}$},\\
a_{25}\cdot N^{-(2-\beta)},~&\text{when $(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,5}\cup R_{2,6}$}.\end{cases}

(E.96)

Then, from (
E.95
) and using (
E.90
) and (
E.96
), we can write

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{\Upsilon}_{N,2}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}=\frac{2(\theta_{2,N}+\delta o_{P}(1))}{(\theta_{1,N}+\delta(\widehat{X}_{N,2,1}-\theta_{1,N}))^{3}}\left\{\frac{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}\left(\frac{\widehat{X}_{N,2,1}-\theta_{1,N}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}\right)\right\}\cdot o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt{}+\frac{2}{(\theta_{1,N}+\delta(\widehat{X}_{N,2,1}-\theta_{1,N}))^{2}}\left\{\frac{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}\left(\frac{\widehat{X}_{N,2,1}-\theta_{1,N}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}\right)\right\}\cdot o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle=\frac{2(\theta_{2,N}+\delta\cdot o_{P}(1))}{(\theta_{1,N}+\delta(\widehat{X}_{N,2,1}-\theta_{1,N}))^{3}}\left\{O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\cdot\left(\frac{\widehat{X}_{N,2,1}-\theta_{1,N}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}\right)\right\}\cdot o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt{}+\frac{2}{(\theta_{1,N}+\delta(\widehat{X}_{N,2,1}-\theta_{1,N}))^{2}}\left\{O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\cdot\left(\frac{\widehat{X}_{N,2,1}-\theta_{1,N}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}\right)\right\}o_{P}(1).

In the above expression and on the event
A_{1,2,N}\cap B_{1,N}
, the denominator terms are bounded away from zero. Using Corollary
3.2
, Lemma
D.13
, (
E.86
) and (
E.78
), we can say that, for
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,3}\cup R_{2,4}\cup R_{2,5}\cup R_{2,6}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{X}_{N,2,1}-\theta_{1,N}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}=T_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})+\frac{\omega_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}\left(\frac{X_{N,1}-\theta_{1,N}}{\omega_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}\right)=O_{P}(1)+\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{2}(1-\beta+\alpha)}}\cdot O_{P}(1)=O_{P}(1).

Hence, we can say that in the region
R_{2,3}\cup R_{2,4}\cup R_{2,5}\cup R_{2,6}
,
\frac{\widehat{\Upsilon}_{N,2}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3})}=o_{P}(1)
. From (
E.93
) and (
E.94
) we know that
q_{N,2}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot\widetilde{\sigma}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{3})=O(1)
, when
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,3}\cup R_{2,4}\cup R_{2,5}\cup R_{2,6}
. Thus, in this region
q_{N,2}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot\widehat{\Upsilon}_{N,2}=o_{P}(1)
.
Hence, for
(\alpha,\beta)\in\bigcup_{i=1}^{6}R_{2,i}
, the remainder term in the expression (
E.75
) converges to zero in probability. Therefore, from the expression (
E.75
), using Slutsky’s theorem, we can write,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(q_{N,2}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot T_{N,2}\left(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3}\right)+\frac{q_{N,2}(\alpha,\beta)}{2}\left\{\widehat{\Upsilon}_{N,2}+\Upsilon_{N}\right\}\leq t,~A_{1,2,N}\cap B_{1,N}\right)+o(1)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\left(q_{N,2}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot T_{N,2}\left(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3}\right)\leq t,~A_{1,2,N}\cap B_{1,N}\right)+o(1)=\mathbf{P}\left(q_{N,2}(\alpha,\beta)\cdot T_{N,2}\left(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3}\right)\leq t\right)+o(1).

This completes the proof of Theorem
3.6
.
∎

Appendix C: Results from other sources

For the sake of completeness, we state certain results from other sources that have been widely used in our proofs. The first result (Theorem
C.1
) provides a non-asymptotic bound on the
d_{1}
distance (see (
3.8
)) between a scaled sum of dependent random variables and a standard normal random variable. The proof of Theorem
C.1
is available in
Barbour et al. (1989)
.

Theorem C.1

(Theorem 6.32. in
Janson et al. (2000)
)
.

Suppose that
S=\sum_{\alpha\in A}X_{\alpha}
, where
\{X_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in A}
is a family of random variables with dependency graph
L
and assume that,
\mathbf{E}(X_{\alpha})=0
, for all
\alpha\in A
. Let
\sigma^{2}=\mathbf{Var}(S)
, and assume that
0<\sigma^{2}<\infty
. Then, for some universal constant
C

\displaystyle d_{1}\left(\sigma^{-1}S,Z\right)\leq\frac{C}{\sigma^{3}}\sum_{\alpha\in A}\sum_{\beta,\gamma\in N(\alpha)}\left[\mathbf{E}|X_{\alpha}X_{\beta}X_{\gamma}|+\mathbf{E}|X_{\alpha}X_{\beta}|\mathbf{E}|X_{\gamma}|\right],

where,
d_{1}
is defined in (
3.8
),
N(\alpha)
is the closed dependency neighborhood of
\alpha
for any
\alpha\in A
, and
Z\sim N(0,1)
.

The next result (Theorem
C.2
) provides a non-asymptotic bound on the total variation distance between a sum of dependent Bernoulli random variables and another Poisson random variable.

Theorem C.2

(Theorem 6.23 form
Janson et al. (2000)
)
.

Suppose that
X=\sum_{\alpha\in A}\mathbf{1}_{\alpha}
, where the
\mathbf{1}_{\alpha}
are random indicator variable with a dependency graph
L
. Then, with
\pi_{\alpha}=\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{1}_{\alpha})
and
\lambda=\mathbf{E}(X)=\sum_{\alpha\in A}\pi_{\alpha}
(and with the summation over ordered pairs
(\alpha,\beta)
),

\displaystyle d_{{TV}}(X,U)\leq\min(\lambda^{-1},1)\left(\mathbf{Var}(X)-\mathbf{E}(X)+2\sum_{\alpha\in A}\sum_{\beta\in N(\alpha)}\pi_{\alpha}\pi_{\alpha}+2\sum_{\alpha\in A}\pi_{\alpha}^{2}\right),

where,
d_{TV}
denotes the total variation distance (cf. (
E.6
)),
N(\alpha)
denotes the closed dependency neighborhood of any
\alpha\in A
and
U\sim
Poisson
(\lambda)
.

Theorem
C.3
provides a construction of the multivariate size biased coupling, which is used to obtain a multivariate Poisson approximation result for a random vector, where the components are sums of dependent Bernoulli random variables, and the components are themselves dependent. We state the result for a two-dimensional random vector. The proof is available in
Nualart and Zhang (2025)
.

Theorem C.3

(Lemma 2.4 in
Nualart and Zhang (2025)
for
d=2
)
.

Let
\mathbf{S}={(S_{1},S_{2})}^{T}
, where,

S_{1}=\sum_{j=1}^{n_{1}}X_{j}^{1},\hskip 8.50012pt\text{and}\hskip 8.50012ptS_{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{n_{2}}X_{j}^{2},

where, we assume that
\{X^{i}_{j}:1\leq j\leq n_{i}\}
,
i=1,2
, are Bernoulli random variables with
\mathbf{P}(X_{j}^{i}=1)=p_{i,j}\in(0,1)
. For all
i=1,2
and
l=1,\ldots,n_{i}
, consider the following random vectors

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}^{1,(1,l)}=\left(\left(X_{j}^{1,(1,l)}\right)_{j=1,j\neq l}^{n_{1}}\right)^{T}\hskip 8.50012pt\text{and}\hskip 8.50012pt\mathbf{S}^{2,(2,l)}=\left(\left(X_{j}^{1,(2,l)}\right)_{j=1}^{n_{1}},\left(X_{j}^{2,(2,l)}\right)_{j=1,j\neq l}^{n_{2}}\right)^{T},

which are defined on the same probability space as the random vectors

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}^{1,l}=\left((X_{j}^{1})_{j=1,j\neq l}^{n_{1}}\right)^{T}\hskip 8.50012pt\text{and}\hskip 8.50012pt\mathbf{S}^{2,l}=\left(\left(X_{j}^{1}\right)_{j=1}^{n_{1}},(X_{j}^{2})_{j=1,j\neq l}^{n_{2}}\right)^{T}.

Assume that

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}^{1,(1,l)}\overset{d}{=}[\mathbf{S}^{1,l}\,|\,X^{1}_{l}=1]\hskip 8.50012pt\text{and}\hskip 8.50012pt\mathbf{S}^{2,(2,l)}\overset{d}{=}[\mathbf{S}^{2,l}\,|\,X^{2}_{l}=1].

Let
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
be two independent random variables, which are also independent of all other random variables, such that for all
i=1,2
, and
j=1,\ldots,n_{i}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(I_{i}=j)=\frac{p_{i,j}}{\lambda_{i}},~\text{where}~\lambda_{i}=\mathbf{E}(S_{i})=\sum_{j=1}^{n_{i}}p_{i,j}.

Define the two-dimensional triangular array,
\widetilde{\mathbf{S}}=\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{S}}^{1},\widetilde{\mathbf{S}}^{2}\right)
, where

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{S}}^{1}=\left(\sum_{j=1,j\neq I_{1}}^{n_{1}}X_{j}^{1,(1,I_{1})}+1\right)\hskip 8.50012pt\text{and}\hskip 8.50012pt\widetilde{\mathbf{S}}^{2}=\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n_{1}}X_{j}^{1,(2,I_{2})},\sum_{j=1,j\neq I_{2}}^{n_{2}}X_{j}^{2,(2,I_{2})}+1\right)^{T}.

Then,
\widetilde{\mathbf{S}}
is a size-biased coupling of
\mathbf{S}
.

The next result (Theorem
C.4
) provides a bound on the Wasserstein distance
d_{W}
(see (
E.4
)) between a bivariate random vector of the form
\mathbf{S}={(S_{1},S_{2})}^{T}
, considered in Theorem A.3 above, and a bivariate random vector
\mathbf{Z}={(Z_{1},Z_{2})}^{T}
, where
Z_{1}
and
Z_{2}
are independent and each
Z_{i}
,
i=1,2
, is a Poisson random variable. A proof is available in
Nualart and Zhang (2025)
(see Theorem 2.6) or
Pianoforte and Turin (2023)
(see Theorem 1.1).

Theorem C.4

(Theorem 2.6 in
Nualart and Zhang (2025)
for
d=2
)
.

Let
\mathbf{S}={(S_{1},S_{2})}^{T}
be a two-dimensional random vector taking values in
\mathbb{N}^{2}_{0}
, with
\mathbf{E}(S_{i})=\kappa_{i}>0
, for
i=1,2
. Let
\widetilde{\mathbf{S}}
be a size-biased coupling of
\mathbf{S}
(see Theorem
(A.3)
above). Then,

\displaystyle d_{{W}}\left(\mathbf{S},\mathbf{Z}\right)\leq\min\{1,\kappa_{1}\}\cdot\mathbf{E}\left|\widetilde{S}_{1}^{1}-1-S_{1}\right|+\min\{1,\kappa_{2}\}\cdot\mathbf{E}\left|\widetilde{S}_{2}^{2}-1-S_{2}\right|+2\kappa_{2}\cdot\mathbf{E}\left|\widetilde{S}_{1}^{2}-S_{1}\right|,

where,
\mathbf{Z}={(Z_{1},Z_{2})}^{T}
,
d_{W}
is defined in (
E.4
), and
Z_{1}
and
Z_{2}
are independent Poisson random variables with means
\kappa_{1}
and
\kappa_{2}
respectively.

Appendix D: Supplementary lemmas

Lemma D.5
.

Let
H
be a fixed, undirected, simple and connected graph with
V(H)=\{1,\ldots,R\}
and edge set
E(H)\subseteq{[V(H)]}^{2}
, where
R\geq 2
, is a fixed positive integer. For each
t\in\{0,1,\ldots,R\}
, define the collection of ordered pairs of vectors

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{t}=\left\{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2}):\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2}\in{[N]}_{R},~|A(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cap A(\mathbf{s}_{2})|=t\right\}.

(E.97)

Then, for
t\in\{2,\ldots,R\}
, there exists an
M\in\mathbb{N}
such that for all
N\geq M
,

\displaystyle L_{t}(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})\leq\frac{1}{N^{2R-t-\beta(2T-m(t:H))}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\leq U_{t}(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C}).

(E.98)

where,
L_{t}(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})
and
U_{t}(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})
are two positive constants depending on
\beta
,
H
and the model parameters
\bm{\lambda}
and
\mathbf{C}
.

Proof of Lemma
D.5
.

Since
|A(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cap A(\mathbf{s}_{2})|=t\in\{2,\ldots,R\}
, thus there exists
t
common elements between
\mathbf{s}_{1}
and
\mathbf{s}_{2}
. Fix any
\mathbf{j}=(j_{1},\ldots,j_{t})^{T}\in[R]_{<,t}
and
\mathbf{k}=(k_{1},\ldots,k_{t})^{T}\in[R]_{<,t}
(see (
E.1
)), where
\mathbf{j}
and
\mathbf{k}
denote the positions at which the common elements appear within
\mathbf{s}_{1}
and
\mathbf{s}_{2}
respectively.
Consider the set
[t]=\{1,\ldots,t\}
, and define

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{t}=\left\{\xi:[t]\mapsto(\xi(1),\ldots,\xi(t))\in{[t]}_{t}~\big|~\text{$\xi$ is a permutation of $\{1,\ldots,t\}$}\right\},

(E.99)

as the collection of all possible permutations of
\{1,\ldots,t\}
. Note
|\mathcal{S}_{t}|=t!
. For each such
\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}\in{[R]}_{<,t}
and any permutation
\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}
, define the following subsets of
\mathcal{M}_{t}
(see (
E.97
)),

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})

\displaystyle=\left\{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2}):\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2}\in\mathcal{M}_{t},\ A\left({(\mathbf{s}_{1})}_{\mathbf{j}}\right)=A\left({(\mathbf{s}_{2})}_{\mathbf{k}}\right)\right\},\hskip 8.50012pt\text{and}

(E.100)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)

\displaystyle=\left\{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2}):\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2}\in\mathcal{M}_{t},\ s_{1,j_{1}}=s_{2,k_{\xi(1)}},\ldots,s_{1,j_{t}}=s_{2,k_{\xi(t)}}\right\}.

For a fixed
t\in\{2,\ldots,R\}
, the sets
\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})
and
\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j}^{\prime},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})
are disjoint if,
\mathbf{j}\neq\mathbf{j}^{\prime}
or
\mathbf{k}\neq\mathbf{k}^{\prime}
.
Also note that,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{t}=\bigcup_{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}\in[R]_{<,t}}\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\hskip 8.50012pt\text{and}\hskip 8.50012pt\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})=\bigcup_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi).

Fix any
(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)
and write,
\mathbf{s}_{i}=\left(s_{i,1},\ldots,s_{i,R}\right)^{T}\in[N]_{R}
, for
i=1,2
. Let
A(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cap A(\mathbf{s}_{2})=\{a_{1},\ldots,a_{t}\}
, where
a_{i}
’s are distinct and
a_{i}\in[N]
for each
i=1,\ldots,t
. For any
\mathbf{j}=(j_{1},\ldots,j_{t})^{T}\in{[R]}_{<,t}
and
\mathbf{k}=(k_{1},\ldots,k_{t})^{T}\in{[R]}_{<,t}
, we write
A(\mathbf{j})=\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{t}\}
, and
A(\mathbf{k})=\{k_{1},\ldots,k_{t}\}
. For simplicity of notation, we will write the product,

\prod_{j\in{[R]}\setminus\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{t}\}}\equiv\prod_{j\in{[R]}\setminus A(\mathbf{j})},

and similarly for the case of the product over
k\in{[R]}\setminus\{k_{1},\ldots,k_{t}\}
.
Next, we use the sets
A(\mathbf{j})
and
A(\mathbf{k})
as vertex sets.
From each of these sets, we construct induced subgraphs of
H
that contain exactly
t
vertices.
We denote these induced subgraphs by
B(\mathbf{j},t)
and
B(\mathbf{k},t)

corresponding to vertex set
A(\mathbf{j})
and
A(\mathbf{k})
, respectively.
With these definitions in place, l.h.s. of (
E.98
) has the following expression.

\displaystyle\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)=\sum_{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}\in[R]_{<,t}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}\in[R]_{<,t}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}\in[R]_{<,t}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\right)\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}\right).

(E.101)

In expression (
E.101
), the product
\left(\prod_{{i,j}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\right)
consists of random variables from the set
A_{1}={Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}:{i,j}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}
, and the product
\left(\prod_{{i,j}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}\right)
consists of random variables from the set
A_{2}={Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}:{i,j}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}
. For any edge
{i,j}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{j},t))
, two cases arise: either
i\in V(B(\mathbf{j},t))
and
j\notin V(B(\mathbf{j},t))
, or both
i,j\notin V(B(\mathbf{j},t))
. Similarly, for the set
A_{2}
, for any edge
{a,b}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))
, two cases arise: either
a\in V(B(\mathbf{k},t))
and
b\notin V(B(\mathbf{k},t))
, or both
a,b\notin V(B(\mathbf{k},t))
. Then, for all
(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)
, the index sets of the random variables from
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
satisfy the following relation:
|{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\cap{s_{2,a},s_{2,b}}|
is either
0
or
1
, depending on the positions of the vertices
i,j
and
a,b
. But we know that the random variables
Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}
and
Y_{s_{2,a},s_{2,b}}
are dependent if and only if
|{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\cap{s_{2,a},s_{2,b}}|=2
.
Next, we define two sets for fixed
t\in\{1,\ldots,R\}
,

\displaystyle J_{t}=\{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}):~\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}\in[R]_{<,t},~B(\mathbf{j},t)\simeq B(\mathbf{k},t)\}~\text{and},

(E.102)

\displaystyle M_{t}=\{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}):~\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}\in[R]_{<,t},~|E(B(\mathbf{j},t))|=|E(B(\mathbf{k},t))|=m(t:H)\}.

where
m(t:H)
defined in (
3.2
).
Therefore, when
(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}
, then for a fixed
\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}
,

\displaystyle\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}=\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}.

Therefore, the sum in (
E.101
) can be written as follows:

\displaystyle\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}\in[R]_{<,t}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\right)\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\right)\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}^{c}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\right)\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\right)\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt{}+\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}^{c}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\right)\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}^{c}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\right)\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}\right)

\displaystyle=V_{1,N}+V_{2,N}+V_{3,N},~\text{(say).}

(E.103)

First, we analyze the
V_{1,N}
form
E.103
, that is,

\displaystyle V_{1,N}=\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\right)\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}\pi_{N,\alpha_{s_{1,i}},\alpha_{s_{1,j}}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}\pi_{N,\alpha_{s_{1,i}},\alpha_{s_{1,j}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}\pi_{N,\alpha_{s_{2,i}},\alpha_{s_{2,j}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}\pi_{N,\alpha_{s_{1,i}},\alpha_{s_{1,j}}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}\pi_{N,\alpha_{s_{2,i}},\alpha_{s_{2,j}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\sum_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\in[K]^{R}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}\pi_{N,u_{i},u_{j}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}\pi_{N,v_{i},v_{j}}

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\left\{\prod_{r=1}^{t}\mathbf{1}(\alpha_{a_{r}}=u_{j_{r}})\prod_{j\in[R]\setminus A(\mathbf{j})}\mathbf{1}(\alpha_{s_{1,j}}=u_{j})\prod_{r=1}^{t}\mathbf{1}(\alpha_{a_{r}}=v_{k_{\xi(r)}})\prod_{k\in[R]\setminus A(\mathbf{k})}\mathbf{1}(\alpha_{s_{2,k}}=v_{k})\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\sum_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\in[K]^{R}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}\pi_{N,u_{i},u_{j}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}\pi_{N,v_{i},v_{j}}\prod_{r=1}^{t}\mathbf{1}(u_{j_{r}}=v_{k_{\xi(r)}})

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\left\{\prod_{j\in[R]}\mathbf{1}(\alpha_{s_{1,j}}=u_{j})\prod_{k\in[R]\setminus A(\mathbf{k})}\mathbf{1}(\alpha_{s_{2,k}}=v_{k})\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\in[K]^{R}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}\pi_{N,u_{i},u_{j}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}\pi_{N,v_{i},v_{j}}\prod_{r=1}^{t}\mathbf{1}(u_{j_{r}}=v_{k_{\xi(r)}})

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\left\{\prod_{j\in[R]}\mathbf{1}(\alpha_{s_{1,j}}=u_{j})\prod_{k\in[R]\setminus A(\mathbf{k})}\mathbf{1}(\alpha_{s_{2,k}}=v_{k})\right\}

\displaystyle=N^{2R-t}\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\in[K]^{R}}N^{-\beta(2T-|E(B(\mathbf{k},t))|)}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}N^{\beta}\pi_{N,u_{i},u_{j}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}N^{\beta}\pi_{N,v_{i},v_{j}}

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\prod_{r=1}^{t}\mathbf{1}(u_{j_{r}}=v_{k_{\xi(r)}})\left\{\prod_{j\in[R]}\lambda_{N,u_{j}}\prod_{k\in[R]\setminus A(\mathbf{k})}\lambda_{N,v_{k}}+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=N^{2R-t-\beta(2T-m(t:H))}\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\in[K]^{R}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}N^{\beta}\pi_{N,u_{i},u_{j}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}N^{\beta}\pi_{N,v_{i},v_{j}}

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\prod_{r=1}^{t}\mathbf{1}(u_{j_{r}}=v_{k_{\xi(r)}})\left\{\prod_{j\in[R]}\lambda_{N,u_{j}}\prod_{k\in[R]\setminus A(\mathbf{k})}\lambda_{N,v_{k}}+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right\}.

(E.104)

Using Assumptions
(A.2)
and
(A.3)
, for all
\varepsilon>0
there exists an
M_{1,t}
such that for all
N>M_{1,t}
,

\displaystyle L_{t,1}(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})-\frac{\varepsilon}{3}<\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\in[K]^{R}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}N^{\beta}\pi_{N,u_{i},u_{j}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}N^{\beta}\pi_{N,v_{i},v_{j}}

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\prod_{r=1}^{t}\mathbf{1}(u_{j_{r}}=v_{k_{\xi(r)}})\left\{\prod_{j\in[R]}\lambda_{N,u_{j}}\prod_{k\in[R]\setminus A(\mathbf{k})}\lambda_{N,v_{k}}+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right\}<L_{t,1}(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})+\frac{\varepsilon}{3}.

where, the constant
L_{t,1}(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})
has the following form,

\displaystyle L_{t,1}(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})=

\displaystyle\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\in[K]^{R}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}c_{u_{i},u_{j}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}c_{v_{i},v_{j}}

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\prod_{r=1}^{t}\mathbf{1}(u_{j_{r}}=v_{k_{\xi(r)}})\cdot\prod_{j\in[R]}\lambda_{u_{j}}\prod_{k\in[R]\setminus A(\mathbf{k})}\lambda_{v_{k}}.

Thus, from (
E.104
), we can write

\displaystyle L_{t,1}(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})-\frac{\varepsilon}{3}<\frac{V_{1,N}}{N^{2R-t-\beta(2T-m(t:H))}}<L_{t,1}(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})+\frac{\varepsilon}{3}

(E.105)

Next, from (
E.103
), we analyze the explicit expression of
V_{2,N}
, that is,

\displaystyle V_{2,N}=\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}^{c}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\right)\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}^{c}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\right)\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}^{c}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\in[K]^{R}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}\pi_{N,u_{i},u_{j}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}\pi_{N,v_{i},v_{j}}\prod_{r=1}^{t}\mathbf{1}(u_{j_{r}}=v_{k_{\xi(r)}})

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\left\{\prod_{j\in[R]}\mathbf{1}(\alpha_{s_{1,j}}=u_{j})\prod_{k\in[R]\setminus A(\mathbf{k})}\mathbf{1}(\alpha_{s_{2,k}}=v_{k})\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}^{c}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\in[K]^{R}}N^{2R-t-\beta(2T-|E(B(\mathbf{k},t))|)}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}N^{\beta}\pi_{N,u_{i},u_{j}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}N^{\beta}\pi_{N,v_{i},v_{j}}

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\prod_{r=1}^{t}\mathbf{1}(u_{j_{r}}=v_{k_{\xi(r)}})\left\{\prod_{j\in[R]}\lambda_{N,u_{j}}\prod_{k\in[R]\setminus A(\mathbf{k})}\lambda_{N,v_{k}}+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right\}.

(E.106)

For all
(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}^{c}
,
|E(B(\mathbf{j},t))|,|E(B(\mathbf{k},t))|<m(t:H)
. Hence, using Assumption
(A.2)
and
(A.3)
, for all
\varepsilon>0
there exists an
M_{2,t}
such that for all
N>M_{2,t}
,

\displaystyle-\frac{\varepsilon}{3}<

\displaystyle\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}^{c}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\in[K]^{R}}\frac{1}{N^{\beta(m(t:H)-|E(B(\mathbf{k},t))|)}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}N^{\beta}\pi_{N,u_{i},u_{j}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}N^{\beta}\pi_{N,v_{i},v_{j}}

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\prod_{r=1}^{t}\mathbf{1}(u_{j_{r}}=v_{k_{\xi(r)}})\left\{\prod_{j\in[R]}\lambda_{N,u_{j}}\prod_{k\in[R]\setminus A(\mathbf{k})}\lambda_{N,v_{k}}+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right\}<\frac{\varepsilon}{3}.

Thus, from the expression (
E.106
), we can write

\displaystyle-\frac{\varepsilon}{3}<\frac{V_{2,N}}{N^{2R-t-\beta(2T-m(t:H))}}<\frac{\varepsilon}{3}.

(E.107)

Now consider the term
V_{3,N}
in (
E.103
). In this case,
(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}^{c}
. Here we can decompose
J_{t}^{c}
into
j_{t}^{\cap}M_{t}
and
J_{t}^{c}\cap M^{c}_{t}
. We focus only on the part
J_{t}^{c}\cap M_{t}
, because the effect of the part
J_{t}^{c}\cap M_{t}^{c}
in the summation is negligible trivially. Since
B(\mathbf{j},t)
and
B(\mathbf{k},t)
are not isomorphic for all
\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}\in J_{t}^{c}\cap M_{t}
, then there exists at least one edge, say
\{r_{1},s_{1}\}
from
E(B(\mathbf{j},t))
and
\{r_{2},s_{2}\}
from
E(B(\mathbf{k},t))
, for which if we define a bijection
\phi:V(B(\mathbf{j},t))\mapsto V(B(\mathbf{k},t))
, then
\{\phi(r_{1}),\phi(s_{1})\}\neq\{r_{2},s_{2}\}
; that is, some subgraph of
B(\mathbf{k},t)
is isomorphic to some subgraph of
B(\mathbf{j},t)
. That implies, for
(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}^{c}\cap M_{t}

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{s_{1,r},s_{1,s}}\right)\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{s_{2,r_{2}},s_{2,s_{2}}}\right)\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{j},t))\setminus\{r_{1},s_{1}\}}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{k},t))\setminus\{r_{2},s_{2}\}}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}\right).

Without loss of generality, if we assume that after removing the edges
\{r_{1},s_{1}\}
from the graph
B(\mathbf{j},t)
and
\{r_{2},s_{2}\}
from the graph
B(\mathbf{k},t)
, the remaining graph becomes isomorphic, and
|E(B(\mathbf{j},t))|=|E(B(\mathbf{k},t))|=m(t:H)
then we can write the following,

\displaystyle\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}^{c}\cap M_{t}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\right)\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}^{c}\cap M_{t}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{s_{1,r},s_{1,s}}\right)\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{s_{2,r_{2}},s_{2,s_{2}}}\right)\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{j},t))\setminus\{r_{1},s_{1}\}}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}Y_{s_{1,i},s_{1,j}}\right)\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}Y_{s_{2,i},s_{2,j}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}^{c}\cap M_{t}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\pi_{\alpha_{s_{1,r}},\alpha_{s_{1,s}}}\cdot\pi_{\alpha_{s_{2,r_{2}}},\alpha_{s_{2,s_{2}}}}\cdot\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{j},t))\setminus\{r_{1},s_{1}\}}\pi_{\alpha_{s_{1,i}},\alpha_{s_{1,j}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}\pi_{\alpha_{s_{1,i}},\alpha_{s_{1,j}}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}\pi_{\alpha_{s_{2,i}},\alpha_{s_{2,j}}}

\displaystyle=N^{-2\beta}N^{-\beta(m(t:H)-1)}N^{-2\beta(T-m(t:H))}\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}^{c}\cap M_{t}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}N^{2\beta}\pi_{\alpha_{s_{1,r}},\alpha_{s_{1,s}}}\cdot\pi_{\alpha_{s_{2,r_{2}}},\alpha_{s_{2,s_{2}}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(B(\mathbf{j},t))\setminus\{r_{1},s_{1}\}}N^{\beta}\pi_{\alpha_{s_{1,i}},\alpha_{s_{1,j}}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{j},t))}N^{\beta}\pi_{\alpha_{s_{1,i}},\alpha_{s_{1,j}}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)\setminus E(B(\mathbf{k},t))}N^{\beta}\pi_{\alpha_{s_{2,i}},\alpha_{s_{2,j}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{2T}\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}^{c}\cap M_{t}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}N^{2R-t-\beta(2T-m(t:H)+1)}.

(E.108)

Using
(A.2)
and
(A.3)
, for all
\varepsilon_{t}>0
there exists an
M_{3}
such that for all
N>M_{3}
,

\displaystyle-\frac{\varepsilon_{t}}{3}<\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{2T}\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}^{c}\cap M_{t}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\frac{N^{2R-t-\beta(2T-m(t:H)+1)}}{N^{2R-t-\beta(2T-m(t:H))}}<\frac{\varepsilon_{t}}{3}.

The upper bound is applied in (
E.108
), is applied over all the terms of
V_{3,N}
and there are finite number of terms in
V_{3,N}
. Thus, from the expression in (
E.108
), we can write,

\displaystyle-\frac{\varepsilon_{t}}{3}<0<\frac{V_{3,N}}{N^{2R-t-\beta(2T-m(t:H))}}<\frac{\varepsilon_{t}}{3}.

(E.109)

Therefore, combining (
E.105
), (
E.107
) and (
E.109
), and taking
N>\max\{M_{1},M_{2},M_{3}\}
, using Lemma
D.5
we can write

\displaystyle L_{t,1}(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})-\varepsilon_{t}<\frac{\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)}{N^{2R-t-\beta(2T-m(t:H))}}<L_{t,1}(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})+\varepsilon_{t}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma D.6
.

Let
H(\mathbf{s}_{i})
denote a graph with vertex set
V(H(\mathbf{s}_{i}))=\{s_{i,1},\ldots,s_{i,R}\}
and edge set
E(H(\mathbf{s}_{i}))=\bigl\{\{s_{i,a},s_{i,b}\}:\{a,b\}\in E(H)\bigr\}
, for
i=1,2,3
. Assume that
|V(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\cap V(H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))|=k_{1}
and
|V(H(\mathbf{s}_{3}))\cap(V(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\cup V(H(\mathbf{s}_{2})))|=k_{2}
, where
k_{1},k_{2}\in[R]
. Then

|E(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\cap E(H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))|\leq m(k_{1}:H)\hskip 8.50012pt\text{and}\hskip 8.50012pt|E(H(\mathbf{s}_{3}))\cap(E(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\cup E(H(\mathbf{s}_{2})))|\leq m(k_{2}:H),

where
m(\cdot:H)
is defined in (
3.2
).

Proof of Lemma
D.6
.

Let
B_{1},\ldots,B_{\binom{R}{k_{1}}}
denote all possible
k_{1}
vertex subsets from
V(H)
. Let
H_{r}
denote the corresponding induced subgraph formed by the vertices in
B_{r}
, for
r=1,\ldots,\binom{R}{k_{1}}
.
There must exist some
r_{0}\in\{1,\ldots,\binom{R}{k_{1}}\}
, such that
H_{r_{0}}
is isomorphic to
H(\mathbf{s}_{1})
. Then

|E(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\cap E(H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))|\leq|E(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))|=|E(H_{r_{0}})|\leq\max_{r}|E(H_{r})|\leq m(k_{1}:H).

Similarly, There must exist some
r_{1}\in\{1,\ldots,\binom{R}{k_{2}}\}
, such that
H_{r_{1}}
is isomorphic to
H(\mathbf{s}_{3})
. Then

|E(H(\mathbf{s}_{3}))\cap(E(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\cup E(H(\mathbf{s}_{2})))|\leq|E(H(\mathbf{s}_{3}))|=|E(H_{r_{1}})|\leq\max_{r}|E(H_{r})|\leq m(k_{2}:H),

and it will lead to the second bound.
∎

Before stating the next result, we provide some needed definitions. Let
D
be a vertex cover of a graph
H
, and assume
u\in D
. An edge
\{u,v\}\in E(H)
is called a
private edge
of
u
if
v\not\in D
and
u
is the only vertex in
D
that covers the edge
\{u,v\}
. If
u\in D
and
u
does not have a private edge, then
u
is redundant and
D\setminus\{u\}
will be a vertex cover of
H
. A vertex cover
D
of
H
is called
minimal
if no proper subset of
D
is a vertex cover of
H
. Any minimum vertex cover is minimal, but the converse is not true. Lemma
D.7
deals with minimal vertex covers, but the statement is also true for any minimum vertex cover.

Lemma D.7
.

Let
H
be a graph with a vertex set
V(H)
. A vertex cover
D\subseteq V(H)
of graph
H
is minimal if and only if each vertex
v\in D
has a private edge in
H
.

Proof of Lemma
D.7
.

Suppose
D
is an minimal vertex cover of
H
. If possible, assume that there exists a
v\in D
, such that
v
has no private edge. This implies, all edges which are incident on
v
must be covered by some other vertex is
D\setminus\{v\}
. Any other edge, which was not incident on
v
was already covered by
D\setminus\{v\}
. Thus
D\setminus\{v\}
is a vertex cover of
H
, and it is proper subset of
D
, which contradicts the minimality of
D
. Hence, there can not exist any such
v\in D
and every vertex in
D
must have a private edge.

Conversely, assume that every
v\in D
has a private edge. We will show that
D
is minimal vertex cover of
H
. Suppose
D
is not a minimal vertex cover of
H
, then there exists
v\in D
such that
D_{1}=D\setminus\{v\}
is still a vertex cover. But since
v\in D
has a private edge
\{u,v\}
with
u\notin D
. Therefore the edge
\{u,v\}
will not be covered by
D_{1}
, contradicting the fact that
D_{1}
is a vertex cover of
H
. Hence, no vertex can be removed, so
D
must be minimal. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma D.8
.

Let
H
be a connected graph with a minimum vertex cover size
\tau(H)
. Consider any
\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2},\mathbf{s}_{3}\in{[N]}_{R}
. Let
H(\mathbf{s}_{1})
,
H(\mathbf{s}_{2})
and
H(\mathbf{s}_{3})
be three isomorphic copies of
H
, with vertex sets
V(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))=A(\mathbf{s}_{1})
,
V(H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))=A(\mathbf{s}_{2})
and
V(H(\mathbf{s}_{3}))=A(\mathbf{s}_{3})
, respectively. Suppose

\left.\begin{aligned} &\left|V(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\cap V(H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))\right|=k_{1},\hskip 8.50012pt\text{and}\\
&\left|V(H(\mathbf{s}_{3}))\cap\left[V(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\cup V(H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))\right]\right|=k_{2},\end{aligned}\right\}\hskip 8.50012pt\text{for some $k_{1},k_{2}\in[R]$.}

Define the union graphs,

G_{1}=\bigcup_{i=1}^{2}H(\mathbf{s}_{i})
and
G_{2}=\bigcup_{i=1}^{3}H(\mathbf{s}_{i})
.

(a)

Let
D_{1}
be any arbitrary minimum vertex cover of
G_{1}
. Then,

\displaystyle|D_{1}|\geq 2\cdot\tau(H)-\min\{\tau(H),k_{1}\}.

(E.110)

(b)

There exists a minimum vertex cover
D_{2}
of
G_{2}
, such that the following holds

\displaystyle|D_{2}|\geq 3\cdot\tau(H)-\min\{\tau(H),k_{1}\}-\min\{\tau(H),k_{2}\}.

(E.111)

Proof of Lemma (
D.8
).

Consider part (a) of the Lemma. Let
D_{1}
be any minimum vertex cover of
G_{1}=H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2})
. Then, firstly note that
|V(H(\mathbf{s}_{i}))\cap D_{1}|

is a vertex cover of
H(\mathbf{s}_{i})
for both
i=1,2
. Thus we can write,

\displaystyle|V(H(\mathbf{s}_{i}))\cap D_{1}|\geq\tau(H),~\text{for $i=1,2$,}\hskip 8.50012pt\text{and}\hskip 8.50012pt\left|\left(V(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\cup V(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\right)\cap D_{1}\right|=|D_{1}|\geq\tau(H).

Also we know that,
|V(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\cap V(H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))|=k_{1}
for some
k_{1}\in[R]
. Thus

\displaystyle|(V(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\cap V(H(\mathbf{s}_{1})))\cap D_{1}|\leq k_{1}.

Thus, we can write,

\displaystyle|D_{1}|=|D_{1}\cap(V(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\cup V(H(\mathbf{s}_{2})))|

\displaystyle=|D_{1}\cap V(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))|+|D_{1}\cap V(H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))|-|D_{1}\cap(V(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\cap V(H(\mathbf{s}_{2})))|\geq 2\cdot\tau(H)-k_{1}.

Therefore, for any
\mathbf{s}_{1}
and
\mathbf{s}_{2}
satisfying
|V(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\cap V(H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))|=k_{1}
, we can write,

\displaystyle|D_{1}|\geq\max\{\tau(H),2\cdot\tau(H)-k_{1}\}=2\cdot\tau(H)-\min\{\tau(H),k_{1}\}.

This completes the proof of part (a).

To prove part (b), we consider the following. Let
U_{1}
be any minimum vertex cover of
H(\mathbf{s}_{3})
. As
H
and
H(\mathbf{s}_{3})
are isomorphic, we must have
|U_{1}|=\tau(H)
. Since
U_{1}
is a minimum vertex cover for
H(\mathbf{s}_{3})
, for each
v\in U_{1}
there exists a
private edge

e=\{v,a\}\in E(H(\mathbf{s}_{3}))
(see Lemma
D.7
). This means that
U_{1}\setminus\{v\}
will not cover
e
. Consider the following collection of vertices in
V(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\cup V(H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))\cup V(H(\mathbf{s}_{3}))
,

A_{1}\equiv U_{1}\cup\left(\left\{V(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\cup V(H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))\right\}\setminus V(H(\mathbf{s}_{3}))\right).

Note,
A_{1}
is a vertex cover of
\bigcup_{i=1}^{3}H(\mathbf{s}_{i})
. We can create a minimum vertex cover of
\bigcup_{i=1}^{3}H(\mathbf{s}_{i})
from
A_{1}
by removing any vertex from
A_{1}
, if it does not have a private edge in
\cup_{i=1}^{3}E(H(\mathbf{s}_{i}))
. Obviously, any
v\in U_{1}\cap A_{1}
can not be removed for the following reason. As
H(\mathbf{s}_{3})
is an induced subgraph of
\bigcup_{i=1}^{3}H(\mathbf{s}_{i})
, any edge in
E(H(\mathbf{s}_{3}))
is also an edge in
\bigcup_{i=1}^{3}E(H(\mathbf{s}_{i}))
. In addition, there exists a private edge
e=\{v,a\}\in E(H(\mathbf{s}_{3}))
, associated with the vertex
v
, where
a\not\in U_{1}
and
a\not\in\{V(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\cup V(H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))\}\setminus V(H(\mathbf{s}_{3}))
. Thus, if
v
is removed from
A_{1}
, the edge
e=\{v,a\}
cannot be covered. Thus, the resulting minimum vertex cover can be constructed by removing redundant vertices from
A_{1}\setminus U_{1}
. This involves checking each
v\in A_{1}\setminus U_{1}=\left\{V(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\cup V(H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))\right\}\setminus V(H(\mathbf{s}_{3}))
, and removing it if it does not have a private edge in
\bigcup_{i=1}^{3}E(H(\mathbf{s}_{i}))
. The resulting minimum vertex cover of
\bigcup_{i=1}^{3}H(\mathbf{s}_{i})
, denoted by
D_{2}
, will also contain
U_{1}
. By construction,

D_{2}=U_{1}\cup\left\{\text{a collection of vertices from $\left(\left\{V(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\cup V(H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))\right\}\setminus V(H(\mathbf{s}_{3}))\right)$}\right\}.

As a result,

\tau(H)=|U_{1}|=|D_{2}\cap V(H(\mathbf{s}_{3}))|\geq\left|D_{2}\cap V(H(\mathbf{s}_{3}))\cap\left(V(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\cup V(H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))\right)\right|.

Obviously,
\left|D_{2}\cap V(H(\mathbf{s}_{3}))\cap\left(V(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\cup V(H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))\right)\right|\leq|V(H(\mathbf{s}_{3}))\cap(V(H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))\cup V(H(\mathbf{s}_{1})))|\leq k_{2}
. Then we can write,

\displaystyle\left|D_{2}\cap V(H(\mathbf{s}_{3}))\cap\left(V(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\cup V(H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))\right)\right|\leq\min\{\tau(H),k_{2}\}.

(E.112)

Next, we can wan write,

\displaystyle\left|D_{2}\cap\left(V(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\cup V(H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))\right)\right|\geq\tau(H).

(E.113)

Also note that,
D_{2}\cap V(H(\mathbf{s}_{i}))
is a vertex cover cover of
H(\mathbf{s}_{i})
for
i=1,2
. Therefore, we can claim
|D_{2}\cap V(H(\mathbf{s}_{i}))|\geq\tau(H)
. Thus, using the fact
|(V(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cap V(\mathbf{s}_{2}))\cap D_{2}|\leq k_{1}
, we can write,

\displaystyle\left|D_{2}\cap\left(V(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\cup V(H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))\right)\right|\geq|V(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cap D_{2}|+|V(\mathbf{s}_{2})\cap D_{2}|-|(V(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cap V(\mathbf{s}_{2}))\cap D_{2}|\geq 2\tau(H)-k_{1}.

(E.114)

Combining (
E.113
) and (
E.114
), we can write,

\displaystyle\left|D_{2}\cap\left(V(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\cup V(H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))\right)\right|\geq\max\{\tau(H),2\cdot\tau(H)-k_{1}\}=2\cdot\tau(H)-\min\{\tau(H),k_{1}\}.

(E.115)

Therefore, combining (
E.112
) and (
E.115
), we can write,

\displaystyle\left|D_{2}\right|=\left|D_{2}\cap\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^{3}V(H(\mathbf{s}_{i}))\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|D_{2}\cap\left(V(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\cup V(H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))\right)\right|+\left|D_{2}\cap V(H(\mathbf{s}_{3}))\right|-\left|D_{2}\cap V(H(\mathbf{s}_{3}))\cap\left(V(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\cup V(H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))\right)\right|

\displaystyle\geq 3\cdot\tau(H)-\min\{\tau(H),k_{1}\}-\min\{\tau(H),k_{2}\}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma D.9

(Attainment of lower bound (
E.110
))
.

Let
H
be a fixed connected, simple, undirected graph with vertex set
V(H)=\{1,\dots,R\}
, edge set
E(H)
and minimum vertex cover size
\tau(H)
. Fix a
t\in[R]
. Consider any
\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2}\in{[N]}_{R}
and assume that
|A(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cap A(\mathbf{s}_{2})|=t
. For
i=1,2
, define the graph
H(\mathbf{s}_{i})
, with vertex set
A(\mathbf{s}_{i})
and edge set

E\big(H(\mathbf{s}_{i})\big)=\big\{\{s_{i,a},s_{i,b}\}:\{a,b\}\in E(H)\big\}.

Define the union graph
G(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})=H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2})
. Let
\xi_{0}
denote the identity permutation on
\{1,\ldots,t\}
. There exists an index vector
\mathbf{j}_{0}\in{[R]}_{t}
such that, for any

\displaystyle(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j}_{0},\mathbf{j}_{0},\xi_{0})=\left\{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2}):~\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2}\in{[N]}_{R},|A(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cap A(\mathbf{s}_{2})|=t,s_{1,k}=s_{2,k}~\text{for all $k\in A(\mathbf{j}_{0})$}\right\},

(E.116)

the union graph
G(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
satisfies the following property: there exists a minimum vertex cover
D_{0}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
of
G(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
such that

\displaystyle|D_{0}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})|=2\cdot\tau(H)-\min\{\tau(H),t\}.

(E.117)

Proof of Lemma
D.9
.

Firstly, consider the case where
t\in\{\tau(H),\ldots,R\}
. Without loss of generality, assume that
D_{0}=\{1,\ldots,\tau(H)\}
is a minimum vertex cover of
H
(if needed, this can be achieved by relabeling of vertices). Define the vector

\mathbf{j}_{0}={(1,\ldots,{\tau(H)},{\tau(H)+1},\ldots,t)}^{T}\in{[R]}_{t},

and pick any
(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j}_{0},\mathbf{j}_{0},\xi_{0})
(cf. (
E.116
)) and consider the union graph
G(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
. Define the collection

D_{0}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})=\{s_{1,1},\ldots,s_{1,\tau(H)}\},

consisting of the first
\tau(H)
components of the vector
\mathbf{s}_{1}={(s_{1,1},\ldots,s_{1,R})}^{T}
. Due to the fact that the first
t
components of
\mathbf{s}_{1}
and
\mathbf{s}_{2}
are equal, this implies
D_{0}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
also consists of the first
\tau(H)
components of
\mathbf{s}_{2}
. Since we have assumed
t\geq\tau(H)
, we have
|D_{0}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})|=\tau(H)=2\tau(H)-\min\{\tau(H),t\}
. We claim that
D_{0}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
is a vertex cover of
G(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})=H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2})
. This is because
D_{0}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
is a vertex cover of both
H(\mathbf{s}_{1})
and
H(\mathbf{s}_{2})
. This follows from the following reasoning.

If
\{s_{1,u},s_{1,v}\}\in E(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))
, then due to the above described construction of
H(\mathbf{s}_{1})
,
\{u,v\}\in E(H)
. As
D_{0}=\{1,\ldots,\tau(H)\}
is a minimum vertex cover of
H
, it implies that either
u
, or
v
, or both
u
and
v
are elements of
D_{0}=\{1,\ldots,\tau(H)\}
. From the definition of
D_{0}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
, it contains the first
\tau(H)
components of
\mathbf{s}_{1}={(s_{1,1},\ldots,s_{1,R})}^{T}
, and this implies that, either
s_{1,u}
, or
s_{1,v}
, or both
s_{1,u}
and
s_{1,v}
are elements of
D_{0}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})=\{s_{1,1},\ldots,s_{1,\tau(H)}\}
. This implies,
D_{0}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
is a vertex cover of
H(\mathbf{s}_{1})
. Similarly, if
\{s_{2,u},s_{2,v}\}\in E(H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))
, we must have
\{u,v\}\in E(H)
, and this implies that either
s_{2,u}
, or
s_{2,v}
, or both
s_{2,u}
and
s_{2,v}
are elements of
D_{0}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})=\{s_{1,1},\ldots,s_{1,\tau(H)}\}=\{s_{2,1},\ldots,s_{2,\tau(H)}\}
, where the equality in the last step arises due to the use of the identity mapping
\xi_{0}
in the definition of
\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j}_{0},\mathbf{j}_{0},\xi_{0})
(cf. (
E.116
)). This implies,
D_{0}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
is a vertex cover of
H(\mathbf{s}_{2})
. As a result
D_{0}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
is a vertex cover of the union graph
G(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
. Now, using Lemma
D.8
(a), we can claim that the lowest possible cardinality of any arbitrary minimum vertex cover of
G(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
will be
\tau(H)
. Since
D_{0}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
is a vertex cover of
G(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
and
|D_{0}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})|=\tau(H)
, it immediately implies that
D_{0}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
will be a minimum vertex cover of
G(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
.

Next, consider the case where
t\in\{1,\ldots,\tau(H)-1\}
. Without loss of generality we assume that
D_{0}=\{1,\ldots,\tau(H)\}
is a minimum vertex cover of
H
. Pick

\mathbf{j}_{0}={(1,\ldots,t)}^{T}.

Consider any
(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j}_{0},\mathbf{j}_{0},\xi_{0})
(cf. (
E.116
)) and construct the union graph
G(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})=H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2})
. Using previous arguments, we can claim

D_{i}(\mathbf{s}_{i})=\{s_{i,1},\ldots,s_{i,\tau(H)}\},~i=1,2,

is a minimum vertex cover of
H(\mathbf{s}_{i})
, for
i=1,2
. According to the definition of
\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j}_{0},\mathbf{j}_{0},\xi_{0})
(cf. (
E.116
)),
s_{1,k}=s_{2,k}
, for
k=1,\ldots,t
, and there is no other common component between
\mathbf{s}_{1}
and
\mathbf{s}_{2}
. Define the following collection of vertices in
G(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
,

D_{0}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})=D_{1}(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup D_{2}(\mathbf{s}_{2}).

We claim that
D_{0}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
is a minimal vertex cover of
G(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
. Firstly, note that
D_{0}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
is a vertex cover of both
G(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
. Note that, as per definition of
\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j}_{0},\mathbf{j}_{0},\xi_{0})

V(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})=D_{1}(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cap D_{2}(\mathbf{s}_{2})=\{s_{1,1},\ldots,s_{1,\tau(H)}\}\cap\{s_{2,1},\ldots,s_{2,\tau(H)}\}=\{s_{1,1},\ldots,s_{1,t}\}.

Write,

D_{0}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})=\left(D_{1}(\mathbf{s}_{1})\setminus V(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\right)\cup V(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\cup\left(D_{2}(\mathbf{s}_{2})\setminus V(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\right),

as the union of three disjoint sets. Note that
|D_{0}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})|=(\tau(H)-t)+t+(\tau(H)-t)=2\tau(H)-t=2\tau(H)-\min\{t,\tau(H)\}
. Pick any
u\in V(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
. Due to Lemma
D.7
and due to the fact that
u\in D_{1}(\mathbf{s}_{1})
and
D_{1}(\mathbf{s}_{1})
is a minimum vertex cover of
H(\mathbf{s}_{1})
, there exists a private edge
\{u,v_{1}\}\in E(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))
for vertex
u
. Similarly, there must exist a private edge
\{u,v_{2}\}\in E(H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))
. Obviously,
v_{1}\not\in D_{1}(\mathbf{s}_{1})
, otherwise
\{u,v_{1}\}
will not be a private edge of
u
. Similarly,
v_{2}\not\in D_{2}(\mathbf{s}_{2})
. Also, we must have
v_{1}\neq v_{2}
. As a result, if
u
is removed from
D_{0}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
, then there will be at least two edges in
E(H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))
which will not be covered by
D_{0}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\setminus\{u\}
. Similarly, if
u\in D_{1}(\mathbf{s}_{1})\setminus V(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
, there will exist at least one edge in
E(H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))
that will not be covered by
D_{0}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\setminus\{u\}
, and a similar argument applies for
u\in D_{2}(\mathbf{s}_{2})\setminus V(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
. As a result, we have established the minimality of the vertex cover
D_{0}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
. By Lemma
D.8
(a),
D_{0}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
also attains the lowest possible cardinality of any vertex cover of the union graph
