G(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
. Hence, the minimal vertex cover
D_{0}(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
is also a minimum vertex cover of
G(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})
. This completes the proof.

∎

Lemma D.10
.

For any
R\geq 2
, the star graph
\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1}
and the complete graph
\mathbb{K}_{R}
on
R
vertices satisfy Assumption
(A.5)
.

Proof of Lemma
D.10
.

Consider a star graph
H=\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1}
with edges
\{\{1,2\},\{1,3\},\ldots,\{1,R\}\}
. Here,
\tau(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})=1
, and the minimum vertex cover is
D_{0}=\{1\}
. Consider the
t
vertex induced subgraph
H_{r_{0}}
of
H
, with vertex set
V(H_{r_{0}})=\{1,i_{2},\ldots,i_{t}\}
, for any
\{i_{2},\ldots,i_{t}\}\subseteq\{2,\ldots,R\}
. Note,
H_{r_{0}}
is also a star graph on
t
vertices and has the maximum number of edge
m(t:H)=(t-1)
. Thus,
H_{r_{0}}\in\mathcal{G}_{t,\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1}}
and
D_{0}\subseteq V(H_{r_{0}})=\{1,i_{1},\ldots,i_{t-1}\}
trivially. Since,
\tau(H)=1
, the case of
t<\tau(H)
does not arise.

When
H=\mathbb{K}_{R}
(complete graph) then
\tau(\mathbb{K}_{R})=R-1
, and we can choose any arbitrary set of
(R-1)
vertices from
[R]
to create a minimum vertex cover
D_{0}=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{R-1}\}
. First, note that any
H_{r}\in\mathcal{G}_{t,\mathbb{K}_{R}}
is a complete graph on
t
vertices. When
t<R-1
, then vertices of the graph
H_{r}
with
V(H_{r})=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{t}\}
also form a complete graph and
V(H_{r})\subseteq D_{0}
. If
t=R-1
, then any
H_{r}
with vertex set
V(H_{r})=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{R-1}\}
is a vertex cover of
\mathbb{K}_{R}
. Therefore in this case
D_{0}=V(H_{r})
. When
t=R
, then
D_{0}\subset V(\mathbb{K}_{R})
trivially.

∎

Lemma D.11
.

Suppose that Assumption
(A.5)
holds. Let
H
be a fixed, undirected, simple, and connected graph with
V(H)=\{1,\ldots,R\}
and the edge set
E(H)\subseteq[V(H)]^{2}
, where
R\geq 2
, is a fixed positive integer. Then, for
t\in\{1,\ldots R\}
, for any
(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}
, there exists an
M
such that for all
N\geq M
,

\displaystyle L_{t,2}(\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\displaystyle\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{E}\left(W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)}{N^{\displaystyle 2R-t-\alpha\left(2\tau(H)-\min\{\tau(H),t\}\right)-\beta\left(2T-m(t:H)\right)}}

\displaystyle\leq U_{t,2}(\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C}).

(E.118)

where,
L_{t,2}(\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})
and
U_{t,2}(\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})
are two positive constants depending on
\alpha,\beta,H
and the model parameters
\bm{\lambda}
and
\mathbf{C}
.

Proof.

Using the same approach and notation as in the proof of Lemma
D.5
and using the definition (
E.102
), we can write

\displaystyle\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{E}\left(W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}\in[R]_{<,t}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\mathbf{E}\left(W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}\in[R]_{<,t}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H(\mathbf{s}_{1}))\cup E(H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))}\max\{W_{N,i},W_{N,j}\}\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k}\in[R]_{<,t}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\left(\sum_{D\in\mathcal{D}(H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))}p_{N}^{|D|}(1-p_{N})^{2R-|D|}\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\left(\sum_{D\in\mathcal{D}(H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))}p_{N}^{|D|}(1-p_{N})^{2R-|D|}\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in(J_{t}\cap M_{t})^{c}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\left(\sum_{D\in\mathcal{D}(H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))}p_{N}^{|D|}(1-p_{N})^{2R-|D|}\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)

\displaystyle=A_{1,N}+A_{2,N},~\text{(say)}.

(E.119)

First, we analyze the design component of the term
A_{1,N}
from (
E.119
). To analyze the term, using Lemma
D.9
and Assumption
(A.5)
, we now show the existence of
(\mathbf{j}_{0},\mathbf{k}_{0})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}
and a permutation map
\xi_{0}\in\mathcal{S}_{t}
for which there is at least one minimum vertex cover of
H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2})
achieving the lower bound provided in (
E.117
), that is, of size
2\cdot\tau(H)-\min\{\tau(H),t\}
for
(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j}_{0},\mathbf{k}_{0},\xi_{0})
. Fix
t\in\{\tau(H),\ldots,R\}
. Now, since
H
satisfies Assumption
(A.5)
, we can say that there exist a
t
vertex induced subgraph
H_{r_{0}}\in\mathcal{G}_{t,H}
(cf. (
3.9
)) and a minimum vertex cover
D_{0}=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{\tau(H)}\}
of
H
, such that

D_{0}\subseteq V(H_{r_{0}})\subseteq[R]
. Then, we can write

V(H_{r_{0}})=A(\mathbf{j}_{0}),\hskip 8.50012pt\text{where}\hskip 8.50012pt\mathbf{j}_{0}={(i_{1},\ldots,i_{\tau(H)},j_{\tau(H)+1},\ldots,j_{t})}^{T},

and
j_{\tau(H)+1},\ldots,j_{t}\in[R]_{t}
, denote the remaining vertices of
H_{r_{0}}
. Take another position vector
\mathbf{k}_{0}=\mathbf{j}_{0}
and the identity permutation map
\xi_{0}\in\mathcal{S}_{t}
. By construction,
(\mathbf{j}_{0},\mathbf{k}_{0})=(\mathbf{j}_{0},\mathbf{j}_{0})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}
(cf. (
E.102
)), and by the definition of
J_{t}
and
M_{t}
, a
t
vertex induced subgraph of
H
, that is,
B(\mathbf{j}_{0},t)=H_{r_{0}}
and
|E(H_{r_{0}})|=|E(B(\mathbf{j}_{0},t))|=m(t:H)
. Then for any
(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j}_{0},\mathbf{j}_{0},\xi_{0})
, we can construct a union graph
H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2})
. Using Lemma
D.9
, we can claim that there is at least one minimum vertex cover of
H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2})
, say
D^{\star}
, which satisfies (
E.117
).

Next, fix
t\in\{1,\ldots,\tau(H)-1\}
. As
H
satisfies Assumption
(A.5)
, there exist a
H_{r_{0}}\in\mathcal{G}_{t,H}
and a minimum vertex cover
D_{0}=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{\tau(H)}\}
of
H
such that

\displaystyle V(H_{r_{0}})=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{t}\}\subset D_{0}.

In this case, take
\mathbf{j}_{0}=(i_{1},\ldots,i_{t})^{T}
and note that
A(\mathbf{j}_{0})=V(H_{r_{0}})
. Take
\mathbf{k}_{1}=(i_{1},\ldots,i_{t})^{T}=\mathbf{j}_{0}
and consider the identity permutation
\xi_{0}\in\mathcal{S}_{t}
. By construction,
(\mathbf{j}_{0},\mathbf{j}_{0})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}
. For this
(\mathbf{s}_{1,0},\mathbf{s}_{2,0})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j}_{0},\mathbf{j}_{0},\xi_{0})
, construct the union graph
H(\mathbf{s}_{1,0})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2,0})
. Using Lemma
D.9
, we can claim that, there is at least one minimum vertex cover of
H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2})
, say
D^{\star}
, which satisfies (
E.117
).
Then, for fixed
t\in[R]
, there is at least one
(\mathbf{j}_{0},\mathbf{k}_{0})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}
and
\xi_{0}\in\mathcal{S}_{t}
such that for all
(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j}_{0},\mathbf{k}_{0},\xi_{0})
, we can write the following.

\displaystyle\sum_{D\in\mathcal{D}(H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))}p_{N}^{|D|}(1-p_{N})^{2R-|D|}

\displaystyle=\sum_{D\in\mathcal{D}_{\min}(H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))}p_{N}^{|D|}(1-p_{N})^{2R-|D|}+\sum_{D\in\mathcal{D}(H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))\setminus\mathcal{D}_{\min}(H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))}p_{N}^{|D|}(1-p_{N})^{2R-|D|}

\displaystyle=|\mathcal{D}_{\min}(H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))|\cdot p_{N}^{|D^{\star}|}(1-p_{N})^{2R-|D^{\star}|}+\sum_{D\in\mathcal{D}(H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))\setminus\mathcal{D}_{\min}(H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))}p_{N}^{|D|}(1-p_{N})^{2R-|D|}

\displaystyle=p_{N}^{|D^{\star}|}\bigg\{|\mathcal{D}_{\min}(H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))|\cdot(1-p_{N})^{2R-|D^{\star}|}

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\sum_{D\in\mathcal{D}(H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))\setminus\mathcal{D}_{\min}(H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))}p_{N}^{|D|-|D^{\star}|}(1-p_{N})^{2R-|D|}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=p_{N}^{2\tau(H)-\min\{\tau(H),t\}}\cdot|\mathcal{D}_{\min}(H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))|\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{N^{\alpha}}\right)\right).

(E.120)

In the above expression
|\mathcal{D}_{\min}(H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))|
is bounded by a finite quantity
|\mathcal{D}(H)|^{2}
, which is a collection of vertex covers of
H
. For simplicity, we can write
a_{1}=|\mathcal{D}_{\min}(H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))|
, and note that the structure of the graph
H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2})
is solely dependent on the choice of the triplet
(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)
and therefore the value of
a_{1}
. Without loss of generality, since the sets
J_{t}\cap M_{t}
and
\mathcal{S}_{t}
are finite sets, assume that for one choice
(\mathbf{j}_{0},\mathbf{k}_{0})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}
and
\xi_{0}\in\mathcal{S}_{t}
there exists a minimum vertex cover
D^{\star}
in the collection
\mathcal{D}_{\min}(H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))
for
(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{j}_{0},\mathbf{k}_{0},\xi_{0})
such that
|D^{\star}|=2\tau(H)-\min\{\tau(H),t\}
. Then, the term
A_{1,N}
(see (
E.119
)) can be written as follows.

\displaystyle A_{1,N}=\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\left(\sum_{D\in\mathcal{D}(H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))}p_{N}^{|D|}(1-p_{N})^{|D|}\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j}_{0},\mathbf{k}_{0},\xi_{0})}\left(\sum_{D\in\mathcal{D}(H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))}p_{N}^{|D|}(1-p_{N})^{2R-|D|}\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)+

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}\\
(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\neq(\mathbf{j}_{0},\mathbf{k}_{0})\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}\\
\xi\neq\xi_{0}\end{subarray}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\left(\sum_{D\in\mathcal{D}(H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))}p_{N}^{|D|}(1-p_{N})^{2R-|D|}\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j}_{0},\mathbf{k}_{0},\xi_{0})}\left(\sum_{D\in\mathcal{D}(H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2}))}p_{N}^{|D|}(1-p_{N})^{2R-|D|}\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)+A_{2,1,N},~\text{(say)}

\displaystyle=a_{1}\cdot p_{N}^{2\tau(H)-\min\{\tau(H),t\}}\cdot\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{N^{\alpha}}\right)\right)\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j}_{0},\mathbf{k}_{0},\xi_{0})}\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)+A_{2,1,N}

\displaystyle=A_{1,1,N}+A_{2,1,N},~\text{(say)}

(E.121)

Firstly, we analyze the term
A_{1,1,N}
from (
E.121
). From the expression (
E.105
) (cf. Lemma
D.5
), we know that for all
\varepsilon>0
there exists an
M
such that for all
N\geq M
, and for all
(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}
and
\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}
, we can write,

\displaystyle L_{t,1}(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})-\varepsilon<\frac{\sum_{(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{t}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)}\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)}{N^{2R-t-\beta(2T-m(t:H))}}<U_{t,1}(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})-\varepsilon.

Using this fact, for fixed
t\in[R]
, and for
(\mathbf{j}_{0},\mathbf{j}_{0})\in J_{t}\cap M_{t}
,
\xi_{0}\in\mathcal{S}_{t}
and recall the fact
p_{N}=c\cdot N^{-\alpha}
, from (
E.121
), for all
\varepsilon_{1}>0
there exists an
M_{1}
such that for all
N>M_{1}
, we can write,

\displaystyle L_{t,1}(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})-\varepsilon<\frac{\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j}_{0},\mathbf{j}_{0},\xi_{0})}\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)}{N^{2R-t-\beta(2T-m(t:H))}}<U_{t,1}(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})-\varepsilon

\displaystyle\Rightarrow L_{t,1}(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})-\varepsilon<\frac{a_{1}\cdot p_{N}^{2\tau(H)-\min\{\tau(H),t\}}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j}_{0},\mathbf{j}_{0},\xi_{0})}\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)}{a_{1}\cdot p_{N}^{2\tau(H)-\min\{\tau(H),t\}}\cdot N^{2R-t-\beta(2T-m(t:H))}}

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}<U_{t,1}(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})-\varepsilon

\displaystyle\Rightarrow~\frac{L_{t,1}(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})-\varepsilon}{[a_{1}\cdot c^{2\tau(H)-\min\{\tau(H),t\}}]^{-1}}<\frac{A_{1,1,N}}{N^{2R-t-\alpha(2\tau(H)-\min\{\tau(H),t\})-\beta(2T-m(t:H))}}<\frac{U_{t,1}(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})-\varepsilon}{[a_{1}\cdot c^{2\tau(H)-\min\{\tau(H),t\}}]^{-1}}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow~L_{t,2}(\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})-\varepsilon_{1}<\frac{A_{1,1,N}}{N^{2R-t-\alpha(2\tau(H)-\min\{\tau(H),t\})-\beta(2T-m(t:H))}}<U_{t,2}(\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})-\varepsilon_{1}.

(E.122)

Next we analyze the term
A_{2,1,N}
from the expression (
E.121
). Since the sets
J_{t}\cap M_{t}
and
\mathcal{S}_{t}
are finite and we can assume without loss of generality that the only choice of
(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})
and
\xi
in
J_{t}\cap M_{t}
and
\mathcal{S}_{t}
respectively are
(\mathbf{j}_{0},\mathbf{j}_{0})
and
\xi_{0}
, for which we can construct a union graph
H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2})
using
(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}(\mathbf{j}_{0},\mathbf{k}_{0},\xi_{0})
and there is at least one minimum vertex cover of
H(\mathbf{s}_{1})\cup H(\mathbf{s}_{2})
of size
2\tau(H)-\min\{\tau(H),t\}
. In the expression of
A_{2,1,N}
, we are summing over
(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k},\xi)
triplet which are not equal to the triplet
(\mathbf{j}_{0},\mathbf{j}_{0},\xi_{0})
, therefore union graphs formed by this triplet has minimum vertex covers which is strictly greater than
2\tau(H)-\min\{\tau(H),t\}
. But the exponent of
N
from the model component in the expression
A_{2,1,N}
remains the same, that is,
2R-t-\beta(2T-m(t,H))
. Hence, for all
\varepsilon_{2}>0
, there exists an
M_{2}
such that for all
N>M_{2}
, we can write

\displaystyle-\varepsilon_{2}<\frac{A_{2,1,N}}{N^{2R-t-\alpha(2\tau(H)-\min\{\tau(H),t\})-\beta(2T-m(t:H))}}<\varepsilon_{2}.

(E.123)

Next, we analyze the term
A_{2,N}
from (
E.119
). In this case
(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in(J_{t}\cap M_{t})^{c}
. We have already proved in (
E.107
) and (
E.109
), that the exponent of
N
is larger than
2R-t-\beta(2T-m(t:H))
for all
(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{k})\in(J_{t}\cap M_{t})^{c}
. Irrespective of the value of the exponent of
N
in the design based term, which is always greater or equal to
2\tau(H)-\min\{\tau(H),t\}
. Thus, for all
\varepsilon_{3}>0
, there exists a
M_{3}
such that for all
N>M_{3}
, we can write,

\displaystyle-\varepsilon_{3}<\frac{A_{2,N}}{N^{2R-t-\alpha(2\tau(H)-\min\{\tau(H),t\})-\beta(2T-m(t:H))}}<\varepsilon_{3}.

(E.124)

Therefore, combining (
E.122
), (
E.123
) and (
E.124
), for all
N>\max\{M_{1},M_{2},M_{3}\}
, from the expression (
E.119
), we can write

\displaystyle L_{t,2}(\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})-\varepsilon

\displaystyle<\frac{\displaystyle\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{E}\left(W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)}{N^{2R-t-\alpha\left(2\tau(H)-\min\{\tau(H),t\}\right)-\beta\left(2T-m(t:H)\right)}}

\displaystyle<U_{t,2}(\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})-\varepsilon.

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma D.12

(Growth rate of the
\sigma^{2}_{N,l}(H)
)
.

Let
H
be a fixed, undirected, simple, and connected graph with
V(H)=\{1,\ldots,R\}
and the edge set
E(H)\subseteq{[V(H)]}^{2}
, where
R\geq 2
, is a fixed positive integer, and
|E(H)|=T
. Suppose that Assumptions
(A.1)
,
(A.2)
,
(A.3)
, and
(A.4)
hold and recall the definition of
\sigma^{2}_{N,l}(H)
(cf. (
3.12
)). Then

(a)

In the induced case (
l=1
), there exists an
M
, such that for all
N>M
,

\displaystyle L(1,\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})<\frac{\sigma^{2}_{N,1}(H)}{N^{\displaystyle-2g_{1,\alpha,\beta,H}(R)+g_{1,\alpha,\beta,H}(t_{1}(\alpha,\beta;H))}}<U(1,\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C}).

(E.125)

(b)

In the ego-centric case (
l=2
), along with the Assumption
(A.5)
over graph
H
, there exists an
M
, such that for all
N>M
,

\displaystyle L(2,\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})<\frac{\sigma^{2}_{N,2}(H)}{N^{\displaystyle-2g_{2,\alpha,\beta,H}(R)+g_{2,\alpha,\beta,H}(t_{2}(\alpha,\beta;H))}}<U(2,\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C}).

(E.126)

where,
L(l,\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})
and
U(l,\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})
are two positive constants depending on network formation design, sparsity level
\alpha
and
\beta
, graph
H
and the SBM parameters. Note that
g_{l,\alpha,\beta,H}(\cdot)
and
t_{l}(\alpha,\beta;H)
for
l=1,2
, are defined in (
1.5
).

Proof of Lemma
D.12
.

For
l=1,2
,

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{N,l}(H)=\mathbf{Var}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,l}(H)-f_{l}(p_{N}:H)\cdot S_{N}(H)\right)=\mathbf{Var}\left(\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\widetilde{W}_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{R}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{cov}\left(\widetilde{W}_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),\widetilde{W}_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{R}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{2};H)\right)\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right),

(E.127)

where,
\mathcal{M}_{t}
defined in (
E.97
). firstly, we analyze the design component from the expression (
E.127
). We begin with the induced case (
l=1
), that is for fixed
t\in[R]
and
(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}
, we can write,

\displaystyle\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{2};H)\right)=p_{N}^{2R-t}(1-p_{N}^{t})=\left(\frac{c}{N^{\alpha}}\right)^{2R-t}\left(1-\frac{c^{t}}{N^{t\alpha}}\right).

Now, for all
\varepsilon_{t}>0
, there exists an
M_{1,\varepsilon_{t}}
such for all
N>M_{1,\varepsilon_{t}}
, we can write,

\displaystyle c^{R}-\varepsilon_{t}\leq c^{2R-t}-\varepsilon_{t}\leq\frac{\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{2};H)\right)}{N^{-\alpha(2R-t)}}\leq c^{2R-t}+\varepsilon_{t}\leq c^{2R}+\varepsilon_{t},~\text{for all $t\in[R]$}.

(E.128)

Then using (
E.128
) and multiplying both side by
N^{-\alpha(2R-t)}\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))
and then taking summation over
t
and
(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}
and applying Lemma
D.5
, we can write,

\displaystyle c^{R}-\max\{\varepsilon_{t}:t\in[R]\}\leq\frac{\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{2};H)\right)}{N^{-\alpha(2R-t)}}\leq c^{2R}+\min\{\varepsilon_{t}:t\in[R]\}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\left\{c^{R}-\max\{\varepsilon_{t}:t\in[R]\}\right\}\sum_{t=1}^{R}N^{-\alpha(2R-t)}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))

\displaystyle\leq\sigma^{2}_{N,1}(H)\leq\left\{c^{R}-\min\{\varepsilon_{t}:t\in[R]\}\right\}\sum_{t=1}^{R}N^{-\alpha(2R-t)}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))

\displaystyle\Rightarrow L(1,\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})\sum_{t=1}^{R}N^{\displaystyle-2g_{1,\alpha,\beta,H}(R)+g_{1,\alpha,\beta,H}(t_{1}(\alpha,\beta;H))}\leq\sigma^{2}_{N,1}(H)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\leq U(1,\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})\sum_{t=1}^{R}N^{\displaystyle-2g_{1,\alpha,\beta,H}(R)+g_{1,\alpha,\beta,H}(t_{1}(\alpha,\beta;H))}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow L(1,\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})\leq\frac{\sigma^{2}_{N,1}(H)}{N^{\displaystyle-2g_{1,\alpha,\beta,H}(R)+g_{1,\alpha,\beta,H}(t_{1}(\alpha,\beta;H))}}\leq U(1,\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C}),

where,
L(1,\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})=(c^{R}-\max\{\varepsilon_{t}:t\in[R]\})\cdot L(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})
and
L(1,\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})=(c^{2R}-\min\{\varepsilon_{t}:t\in[R]\})\cdot U(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})
. This completes the proof for the induced case.

In the ego-centric case or for
l=2
and for
H
satisfying Assumption
(A.5)
, using Lemma
D.11
, there exist an
M_{t,2}
such that for all
N\geq M_{t,2}
, we can write,

\displaystyle L_{t,2}(\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\displaystyle\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{E}\left(W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)}{N^{\displaystyle 2R-t-\alpha\left(2\tau(H)-\min\{\tau(H),t\}\right)-\beta\left(2T-m(t:H)\right)}}

\displaystyle\leq U_{t,2}(\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})

\displaystyle L(2,\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\displaystyle\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{E}\left(W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)}{N^{\displaystyle 2R-t-\alpha\left(2\tau(H)-\min\{\tau(H),t\}\right)-\beta\left(2T-m(t:H)\right)}}

\displaystyle\leq U(2,\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C}),

(E.129)

where,
L(2,\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})=\max\{L_{t,2}(\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C}):t\in[R]\}
and
U(2,\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})=\max\{U_{t,2}(\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C}):t\in[R]\}
. Now, multiplying both side by
N^{\displaystyle 2R-t-\alpha\left(2\tau(H)-\min\{\tau(H),t\}\right)-\beta\left(2T-m(t:H)\right)}
in (
E.129
), then subtracting the term
N^{2R-t}f_{2}^{2}(p_{N}:H)\cdot(\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H)))^{2}
from both side and taking summation over
t\in[R]
, we get the following, for
N\geq\max\{M_{t,2}:t\in[R]\}

\displaystyle L(2,\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})\sum_{t=1}^{R}N^{\displaystyle-2g_{2,\alpha,\beta,H}(R)+g_{2,\alpha,\beta,H}(t_{2}(\alpha,\beta;H))}\leq\sigma^{2}_{N,2}(H)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\leq U(2,\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})\sum_{t=1}^{R}N^{\displaystyle-2g_{2,\alpha,\beta,H}(R)+g_{2,\alpha,\beta,H}(t_{2}(\alpha,\beta;H))}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow L(2,\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})\leq\frac{\sigma^{2}_{N,2}(H)}{N^{\displaystyle-2g_{2,\alpha,\beta,H}(R)+g_{2,\alpha,\beta,H}(t_{2}(\alpha,\beta;H))}}\leq U(2,\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C}),

where,
L(2,\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})
and
U(2,\alpha,\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})
are two positive constants depending on network formation design and the SBM parameters. This completes the proof of ego-centric case.

∎

Lemma D.13
.

Suppose that Assumptions
(A.1)
,
(A.2)
,
(A.3)
and
(A.4)
hold. Let
H
be a fixed, undirected, simple graph with
R
vertices and
T
edges.
\widehat{S}_{N,l}(H)
and
S_{N}(H)
denote the estimated and population subgraph
H
count (cf. (
3.5
)), respectively. Then

(a)

for induced (
l=1
) and ego-centric (
l=2
) network formation,

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{S}_{N,l}(H)-\mathbf{E}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,l}(H)\right)}{N^{-g_{l,\alpha,\beta,H}(R)}}=o_{P}(1).

For
l=2
case, in addition to all previously stated assumptions, the graph
H
must satisfy Assumption
(A.5)
.

(b)

Moreover, the population subgraph count
S_{N}(H)
(cf. (
3.5
)) satisfies the following,

\displaystyle\frac{S_{N}(H)-\mathbf{E}(S_{N}(H))}{\sqrt{\mathbf{Var}(S_{N}(H))}}=O_{P}(1)\hskip 8.50012pt\text{and}\hskip 8.50012pt\frac{{S}_{N}(H)-\mathbf{E}\left({S}_{N}(H)\right)}{N^{R-T\beta}}=o_{P}(1).

Proof of Lemma
D.13
.

First note that

\displaystyle g_{l,\alpha,\beta,H}(R)=\begin{cases}-R(1-\alpha)+T\beta,&~\text{if $l=1$,}\\
-R+\tau(H)\cdot\alpha+T\beta,&~\text{if $l=2$}.\end{cases}

First we will prove part (a). Using Assumption
(A.2)
,
(A.3)
, and Lemma
D.5
, we can write the following

\displaystyle\mathbf{Var}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,1}(H)\right)\leq\sum_{t=1}^{R}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{E}\left(W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}({\mathbf{s}_{2}:H})\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{R}c^{2R-t}N^{-\alpha(2R-t)}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}({\mathbf{s}_{2}:H})\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}\leq c^{2R}\cdot\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{2T}\sum_{t=1}^{R}N^{-2g_{1,\alpha,\beta,H}(R)+g_{1,\alpha,\beta,H}(t)}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow~\frac{\mathbf{Var}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,1}(H)\right)}{N^{-2g_{1,\alpha,\beta,H}(R)}}\leq c^{2R}\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{2T}\sum_{t=1}^{R}N^{g_{l,\alpha,\beta,H}(t)}\to 0,~\text{as $N\rightarrow\infty$.}

Therefore, from the above expression we can write,

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{S}_{N,1}(H)-\mathbf{E}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,1}(H)\right)}{N^{-g_{1,\alpha,\beta,H}(R)}}=o_{P}(1).

Next, in the ego-centric case (
l=2
), using Assumption
(A.2)
,
(A.3)
and Lemma
D.11
, we can write,

\displaystyle\mathbf{Var}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,2}(H)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{R}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{E}\left(W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}({\mathbf{s}_{2}:H})\right)

\displaystyle\leq c^{2\tau(H)}|\mathcal{D}(H)|^{2}\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{2T}\sum_{t=1}^{R}N^{-2g_{2,\alpha,\beta,H}(R)+g_{2,\alpha,\beta,H}(t)}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow~\frac{\mathbf{Var}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,2}(H)\right)}{N^{-2g_{2,\alpha,\beta,H}(R)}}

\displaystyle\leq c^{2\tau(H)}|\mathcal{D}(H)|^{2}\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{2T}\sum_{t=1}^{R}N^{g_{2,\alpha,\beta,H}(t)}\to 0,~\text{as $N\rightarrow\infty$.}

Therefore, from the above expression we can write,

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{S}_{N,2}(H)-\mathbf{E}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,2}(H)\right)}{N^{-g_{2,\alpha,\beta,H}(R)}}=o_{P}(1).

This completes the proof of part (a). Next, we prove part (b).
Note that,

\displaystyle\mathbf{Var}(S_{N}(H))

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=2}^{R}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{cov}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=2}^{R}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\left\{\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)-\left(\mathbf{E}\left(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\right)\right)^{2}\right\}.

Using Assumption
(A.2)
and Lemma
D.5
, there exist and
M
such that for all
N>M
, we can write,

\displaystyle L(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})\sum_{t=2}^{R}N^{2R-t-\beta(2T-m(t:H))}\leq\mathbf{Var}(S_{N}(H))\leq U(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})\sum_{t=2}^{R}N^{2R-t-\beta(2T-m(t:H))}

(E.130)

where,
L(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})=\min_{t}\{L_{t}(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})\}
and
U(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})=\max_{t}\{U_{t}(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})\}
are positive constants (see Lemma
D.5
)
Next, we apply Theorem
C.1
over the pivotal quantity,

T_{N}(H)=\frac{S_{N}(H)-\mathbf{E}(S_{N}(H))}{\sqrt{\mathbf{Var}(S_{N}(H))}},

and we get the following,

\displaystyle d_{1}\left(T_{N}(H,~Z)\right)\leq\frac{1}{[\mathbf{Var}(S_{N}(H)]^{3/2}}\sum_{\mathbf{s}_{1}\in{[N]}_{R}}\sum_{\mathbf{s}_{2},\mathbf{s}_{3}\in N(\mathbf{s}_{1})}\left(\mathbf{E}\left|\prod_{i=1}^{3}\widetilde{Y}_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{i}:H)\right|+\mathbf{E}\left|\prod_{i=1}^{2}\widetilde{Y}_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{i}:H)\right|\mathbf{E}|\widetilde{Y}_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{3}:H)|\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{[\mathbf{Var}(S_{N}(H)]^{3/2}}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=2}^{R}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2},\mathbf{s}_{3})\in\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}}\left(\mathbf{E}\left|\prod_{i=1}^{3}\widetilde{Y}_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{i}:H)\right|+\mathbf{E}\left|\prod_{i=1}^{2}\widetilde{Y}_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{i}:H)\right|\mathbf{E}|\widetilde{Y}_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{3}:H)|\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{[\mathbf{Var}(S_{N}(H)]^{3/2}}\left(P_{N,1}+P_{N,2}\right),~\text{(say)}

(E.131)

where,
\widetilde{Y}_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H)=Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H)-\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H))
. Note that, by similar argument as in (
E.11
) and using (
E.22
),

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left|\prod_{i=1}^{3}\widetilde{Y}_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{i}:H)\right|\leq 8\cdot\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{3T}N^{-\beta(3T-m(k_{1}:H)-m(k_{2}:H))}

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left|\prod_{i=1}^{2}\widetilde{Y}_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{i}:H)\right|\mathbf{E}|\widetilde{Y}_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{3}:H)|\leq 8\cdot\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{3T}N^{-\beta(3T-m(k_{1}:H)-m(k_{2}:H))}

(E.132)

Then from the expression in the the r.h.s. of (
E.131
) and combining (
E.130
) and (
E.132
), there exist an
M
such that for all
N>M
, we can write the following

\displaystyle d_{1}\left(T_{N}(H),Z\right)\leq O\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{2}(t(\beta;H)-\beta\cdot m(t(\beta;H):H))}}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N^{t(\beta:H)(1-m(H)\cdot\beta)}}}\right).

where,
t(\beta;H)=\argmaxA_{t\in\{2,\ldots,R\}}(t-\beta\cdot m(t:H))
and
m(H)=m(t(\beta;H):H)/t(\beta;H)
.
Thus, for
\beta<1/m(H)
,
T_{N}(H)=O_{P}(1)
. This completes the proof of first part of (c). To prove the second assertion in part (c) of Lemma
D.13
. Note that for any graph fixed graph
H
and for any
\beta<1/m(H)
, from the expression (
E.130
), we can write,

\displaystyle\mathbf{Var}\left(\frac{S_{N}(H)}{N^{R-T\beta}}\right)\leq U(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})\frac{1}{N^{2R-2T\beta}}\sum_{t=2}^{R}N^{2R-t-\beta(2T-m(t:H))}\to 0,\hskip 8.50012pt\text{as $N\to\infty$}.

Hence, for
0\leq\beta<1/m(H)
, we can claim that,
(S_{N}(H)-\mathbf{E}(S_{N}(H)))/N^{R-T\beta}=o_{p}(1)
.
For strictly balanced graph
H
,
m(H)=T/R
and for
\beta=R/T
, from the expression (
E.130
), we can write,

\displaystyle\mathbf{Var}\left(S_{N}(H)\right)\leq U(\beta,H,\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{C})\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right).

Thus, using
(A.2)
, for
N\rightarrow\infty
, we can claim
\mathbf{E}(S_{N})=O(1)
and
\mathbf{Var}(S_{N}(H))=O(1)
. In addition note that,
\mathbf{E}(S_{N}(H)-\mathbf{E}(S_{N}(H)))=0
. Then by Chebyshev inequality, we can write,

\mathbf{P}(|S_{N}(H)-\mathbf{E}(S_{N}(H))|>M)\leq\frac{\mathbf{Var}(S_{N}(H))}{M^{2}}=\frac{O(1)}{M^{2}}.

Hence, for all
M>0
, we can write,
\sup_{N}\mathbf{P}(|S_{N}(H)|>M)\leq\frac{O(1)}{M^{2}}
.
Then we can claim that

\sup_{N}\mathbf{P}(|S_{N}(H)|>M)<\varepsilon.

This completes the proof.

∎

Lemma D.14
.

Suppose that Assumptions
(A.1)
,
(A.2)
,
(A.3)
and
(A.4)
hold. Let
H
be a fixed, undirected, simple, and connected graph with
V(H)=\{1,\ldots,R\}
and the edge set
E(H)\subseteq[V(H)]^{2}
, where
R\geq 2
and
|E(H)|=T
. It is given that
H
is a strictly balanced graph and

\sigma^{2}_{N,l}(H)=\mathbf{Var}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,l}(H)-f_{l}(p_{N}:H)\cdot S_{N}(H)\right)\hskip 8.50012pt\text{for $l=1,2$}.

Then

(i)

If
(\alpha,\beta)=(0,R/T)
, then

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{N,l}(H)\to f_{l}(c:H)(1-f_{l}(c:H))\cdot\kappa_{N}(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:H)\hskip 8.50012pt\text{as $N\to\infty$.}

(ii)

If
(\alpha,\beta)\in F_{l}(H)
, defined in (
3.20
) and in addition for
l=2
, the graph
H
satisfies
(A.5)
, then

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{N,l}(H)\to\phi_{l}(c:H)\cdot\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:H)\hskip 8.50012pt\text{as $N\to\infty$.}

where
\phi_{l}(c:H)
defined in (
E.44
) and
\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:H)
defined in (
3.18
).

(iii)

For
\tau(H)=1
, and
\alpha=1
, in the ego-centric sub-network formation (
l=2
),

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{N,2}(H)

\displaystyle=N^{2(R-1)(1-\beta)}\left\{c\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{R}\\
(v_{2},\ldots,v_{R})\in[K]^{R-1}\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=2}^{R}N^{2\beta}\pi_{N,u_{1},u_{j}}\pi_{N,u_{1},v_{j}}\prod_{j=1}^{R}\lambda_{N,u_{j}}\prod_{j=2}^{R}\lambda_{N,v_{j}}\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right\}.

Proof of Lemma
D.14
.

For part (a), when
\alpha=0
and
\beta=R/T
and graph
H
is strictly balanced with
R
vertices and
T
edges. In this case
p_{N}=c\in(0,1)
. Then for
l=1,2
, we can write,

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{N,l}(H)=\mathbf{Var}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,l}(H)-f_{l}(c:H)\cdot S_{N}(H)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{Var}\left(\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\left(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}:H)-f_{l}(c:H)\right)Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{R}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))

\displaystyle+\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{R}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))

\displaystyle+\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{Var}\left(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}:H)\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H))

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))

\displaystyle+N^{R-T\beta}f_{l}(c:H)(1-f_{l}(c:H))\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{R}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}N^{\beta}\pi_{N,u_{i},u_{j}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{R}\lambda_{N,u_{i}}+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))

\displaystyle+f_{l}(c:H)(1-f_{l}(c:H))\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{R}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}N^{\beta}\pi_{N,u_{i},u_{j}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{R}\lambda_{N,u_{i}}+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=J_{N,l,1}+J_{N,l,2},~\text{(say)}.

Next, we will show that
J_{N,l,1}
converges to zero as
N\rightarrow\infty
. Using the facts
\alpha=0
and
\beta=R/T
, we can write

\displaystyle\left|J_{N,l,1}\right|\leq\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\left|\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\left|\mathbf{E}\left(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))\leq\sum_{t=1}^{R}\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]^{R}}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{2T}\cdot N^{2R-t-\beta(2T-m(t:H))}

\displaystyle=\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]^{R}}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{2T}\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}N^{-t+(R/T)m(t:H)}.

(E.133)

In the above expression
t/m(t:H)>R/T
for
t\in\{1,\ldots,R-1\}
since
H
is a strictly balanced graph. Thus, using Assumption
(A.2)
, we can claim that
|J_{N,l,1}|\to 0
, as
N\rightarrow\infty
. Also note that using Assumptions
(A.2)
and
(A.3)
we can say that

\displaystyle J_{N,l,2}

\displaystyle\to f_{l}(c:H)(1-f_{l}(c:H))\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{R}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}c_{u_{i},u_{j}}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{R}\lambda_{u_{i}}

\displaystyle=f_{l}(c:H)(1-f_{l}(c:H))\cdot\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:H),~\text{as $N\rightarrow\infty$ for $l=1,2$,}

where
\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:H)
defined in (
3.18
).
Hence, combining both the term
J_{N,l,1}
and
J_{N,l,2}
as
N\rightarrow\infty
, we can say that

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{N,l}(H)\to f_{l}(c:H)(1-f_{l}(c:H))\cdot\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:H),~\text{for $l=1,2$.}

This completes the proof of part (a). For part (b),
\alpha\in(0,1)
. In the induced case, it is assumed that the sparsity levels
(\alpha,\beta)
are within the boundary region

F_{1}(H)=\left\{(\alpha,\beta):0<\alpha<1,~\beta=\frac{R\cdot(1-\alpha)}{T}\right\},\hskip 8.50012pt\text{(see \eqref{F1F2-set}).}

In the ego-centric case,
(\alpha,\beta)
are within the boundary region

\displaystyle F_{2}(H)

\displaystyle=\left\{(\alpha,\beta):0<\alpha<\widetilde{\alpha}_{H},~\beta=\left(1-\frac{\tau(H)\cdot\alpha}{R}\right)\cdot\frac{R}{T}\right\},

where
\widetilde{\alpha}_{H}
is defined (
3.17
) and the graph
H
satisfies
(A.5)
. Then
\sigma^{2}_{N,l}(H)
has the following expression,

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{N,l}(H)=\sum_{t=1}^{R}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{R}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in{[N]}_{R}}\mathbf{Var}\left(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}:H)\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}:H))

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),W_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}+N^{R-T\beta}f_{l}(p_{N}:H)(1-f_{l}(p_{N}:H))\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{R}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}N^{\beta}\pi_{N,u_{i},u_{j}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{R}\lambda_{N,u_{i}}+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=B_{N,l,1}+B_{N,l,2},~\text{(say)}.

First, we will show that
B_{N,l,1}
converges to zero as
N\rightarrow\infty
. Using the fact
(\alpha,\beta)\in F_{1}(H)
, we can write

\displaystyle|B_{N,1,1}|\leq\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}|\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{E}\left(W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot W_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))

\displaystyle\leq a_{\max}(H)\cdot c^{2R}N^{-g_{1,\alpha,\beta,H}(R)}\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}N^{{}g_{1,\alpha,\beta,H}(t)},

and in case of
l=2
, when
(\alpha,\beta)\in F_{2}(H)
and
H
satisfy Assumption
(A.5)
, we can write

\displaystyle|B_{N,2,1}|\leq\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}|\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H),W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{E}\left(W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H)\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:H)\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:H))

\displaystyle\leq a_{\max}(H)\cdot c^{2\tau(H)}\left|\mathcal{D}(H)\right|^{2}N^{-g_{2,\alpha,\beta,H}(R)}\sum_{t=1}^{R-1}N^{{}g_{2,\alpha,\beta,H}(t)}

where,
a_{\max}(H)
is a finite positive constant (defined in (
E.45
)) and
|\mathcal{D}(H)|
is the size of the collection of all possible vertex covers of
H
, which is a finite quantity. Next, when
(\alpha,\beta)\in F_{l}(H)
, we have
g_{l,\alpha,\beta,H}(R)=0
for
l=1,2
. Moreover, for
(\alpha,\beta)\in F_{l}(H)
,
l=1,2
, and
t\in\{1,\ldots,R-1\}
, writing
\beta
in terms of
\alpha
we have the following.

\displaystyle g_{1,\alpha,\beta,H}(t)

\displaystyle=(1-\alpha)\left(-\frac{t}{m(t:H)}+\frac{R}{T}\right)m(t:H),\hskip 8.50012pt\text{and}

\displaystyle g_{2,\alpha,\beta,H}(t)

\displaystyle=m(t:H)\left\{\left(-\frac{t}{m(t:H)}+\frac{R}{T}\right)+\left(\frac{\min(\tau(H),t)}{m(t:H)}-\frac{\tau(H)}{T}\right)\alpha\right\}.

Since
\alpha\in(0,1)
in the induced case and

\alpha\in(0,\widetilde{\alpha}_{H})
in the egocentric case, it follows that
g_{l,\alpha,\beta,H}(t)<0
for all
t\in\{1,\ldots,R-1\}
. Thus,
B_{N,l,1}\to 0
as
N\rightarrow\infty
. Using Assumptions
(A.2)
and
(A.3)
, and the fact
(\alpha,\beta)\in F_{l}(H)
we can write

\displaystyle B_{N,l,2}

\displaystyle=N^{R-T\beta}f_{l}(p_{N}:H)(1-f_{l}(p_{N}:H))\sum_{\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{R}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E(H)}N^{\beta}\pi_{N,u_{i},u_{j}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{R}\lambda_{N,u_{i}}+B\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\to\phi_{l}(c:H)\cdot\kappa\left(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:H\right),~\text{for $l=1,2$,}

where
\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:H)
defined in (
3.18
).
Thus, combining the term
B_{N,l,1}
and
B_{N,l,2}
as
N\to\infty
, we can write

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{N,l}(H)\to\phi_{l}(c:H)\cdot\kappa(\mathbf{C},\bm{\lambda}:H).

This completes the proof of part (b). For part (c),
\alpha=1
and
\beta\in[0,1)
. In this case
\tau(H)=1
, so from Lemma
D.16
we can claim that
H=\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1}
. Then
\sigma_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})
has the following expression

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})=\mathbf{Var}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})-f_{2}(p_{N}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})\cdot S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=2}^{R}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1}),W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1}))

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{1}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1}),W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=2}^{R}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1}),W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1}))

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{1}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1}),W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1}))\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1}))

\displaystyle=Q_{N,1}+Q_{N,2},~\text{(say).}

Using Assumptions
(A.2)
and
(A.3)
, first analyze the term
Q_{N,1}
, that is,

\displaystyle|Q_{N,1}|=\sum_{t=2}^{R}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}|\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1}),W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1}))|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=2}^{R}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{t}}\mathbf{E}\left(W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})\cdot Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1}))

\displaystyle\leq c\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]^{R}}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{2(R-1)}\sum_{t=2}^{R}N^{(2R-t-1)(1-\beta)}\leq c(R-1)\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]^{R}}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{2(R-1)}N^{(2R-3)(1-\beta)}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow Q_{N,1}=O\left(N^{(2R-3)(1-\beta)}\right).

Next, using Assumptions
(A.2)
and
(A.3)
in the expression
Q_{N,2}
we get the following expression,

\displaystyle Q_{N,2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{1}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1}),W_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})\right)\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1}))\cdot\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{1}}\left\{\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{s_{1,1}}+(1-W_{s_{1,1}})\prod_{j=2}^{R}W_{s_{1,j}},W_{s_{1,1}}+(1-W_{s_{1,1}})\prod_{j=2}^{R}W_{s_{2,j}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}\left.\times\prod_{j=2}^{R}\pi_{N,\alpha_{s_{1,1}},\alpha_{s_{1,j}}}\pi_{N,\alpha_{s_{1,1}},\alpha_{s_{2,j}}}+\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{s_{1,1}}+(1-W_{s_{1,1}})\prod_{j=2}^{R}W_{s_{1,j}},W_{s_{2,1}}+(1-W_{s_{2,1}})W_{s_{1,1}}\prod_{j=3}^{R}W_{s_{2,j}}\right)\prod_{j=2}^{R}\pi_{N,\alpha_{s_{1,1}},\alpha_{s_{1,j}}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\pi_{\alpha_{s_{2,1}},\alpha_{s_{1,1}}}\prod_{j=3}^{R}\pi_{\alpha_{s_{2,1}},\alpha_{s_{2,j}}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{1}}\mathbf{cov}\left(W_{s_{1,1}}+(1-W_{s_{1,1}})\prod_{j=2}^{R}W_{s_{1,j}},W_{s_{1,1}}+(1-W_{s_{1,1}})\prod_{j=2}^{R}W_{s_{2,j}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\prod_{j=2}^{R}\pi_{N,\alpha_{s_{1,1}},\alpha_{s_{1,j}}}\pi_{N,\alpha_{s_{1,1}},\alpha_{s_{2,j}}}+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)

\displaystyle=c\cdot N^{2(R-1)(1-\beta)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{R}\\
(v_{2},\ldots,v_{R})\in[K]^{R-1}\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=2}^{R}N^{2\beta}\pi_{u_{1},u_{j}}\pi_{u_{1},v_{j}}\prod_{j=1}^{R}\lambda_{N,u_{j}}\prod_{j=2}^{R}\lambda_{N,v_{j}}\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right).

Therefore, combining the term
Q_{N,1}
and
Q_{N,2}
we get,

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1})

\displaystyle=N^{2(R-1)(1-\beta)}\left\{c\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u}\in[K]^{R}\\
(v_{2},\ldots,v_{R})\in[K]^{R-1}\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=2}^{R}N^{2\beta}\pi_{u_{1},u_{j}}\pi_{u_{1},v_{j}}\prod_{j=1}^{R}\lambda_{N,u_{j}}\prod_{j=2}^{R}\lambda_{N,v_{j}}\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right\}.

This completes the proof of part (c).
∎

Lemma D.15
.

Suppose
\{Z_{N}:N\leq 1\}
is a sequence of random variables, where
Z_{N}\sim\text{Poisson}(\lambda_{N})
and
Z
is another random variable such that
Z\sim\text{Poisson}(\lambda)
. Assume that
Z_{N}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}Z
, as
N\to\infty
. Then

d_{TV}(Z_{N},Z)\to 0,~\text{as $N\rightarrow\infty$}.

Proof of Lemma
D.15
.

Fix
N\geq 1
. Let
\hat{Z}\overset{d}{=}{Z}
and
\hat{Z}_{N}\overset{d}{=}{Z}_{N}
. Define two random variables
X_{N}^{+}=\hat{Z}_{N}-\hat{Z}
if
\lambda_{N}>\lambda
, and
X_{N}^{-}=\hat{Z}-\hat{Z}_{N}
if
\lambda_{N}<\lambda
. Here
X^{+}_{N}
and
X_{N}^{-}
are independent of
\hat{Z}
and
X^{+}_{N}\sim\text{Poisson}(\lambda_{N}-\lambda)
and
X^{-}_{N}\sim\text{Poisson}(\lambda-\lambda_{N})
. Then
(\hat{Z},\hat{Z}_{N})
is a coupling of two random variable
Z
and
Z_{N}
. Let
A
be an event
[\hat{Z}_{N}=\hat{Z}]
. Then for all
B\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R})
, we can write,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(Z_{N}\in B\right)-\mathbf{P}\left(Z\in B\right)=\mathbf{P}\left(\hat{Z}_{N}\in B\right)-\mathbf{P}(\hat{Z}\in B)=\mathbf{P}\left(\hat{Z}_{N}\in B,A^{c}\right)-\mathbf{P}\left(\hat{Z}\in B,A^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{P}(A^{c})=\mathbf{P}(\hat{Z}_{N}\neq\hat{Z})=\mathbf{P}\left(\left|\hat{Z}_{N}-\hat{Z}\right|>0\right).

(E.134)

Next, when
\lambda_{N}>\lambda
, we can write,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(\left|\hat{Z}_{N}-\hat{Z}\right|>0\right)=\mathbf{P}\left(X^{+}_{N}>0\right)=1-\mathbf{P}\left(X^{+}_{N}=0\right)=1-e^{-(\lambda_{N}-\lambda)},

(E.135)

and when
\lambda_{N}<\lambda
, we can write,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(\left|\hat{Z}_{N}-\hat{Z}\right|>0\right)=\mathbf{P}\left(X^{-}_{N}>0\right)=1-\mathbf{P}\left(X^{-}_{N}=0\right)=1-e^{-(\lambda-\lambda_{N})}.

(E.136)

Therefore, combining (
E.135
) and (
E.136
), from (
E.134
), for all
B\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R})
, we can write,

\displaystyle\left|\mathbf{P}\left(Z_{N}\in B\right)-\mathbf{P}\left(Z\in B\right)\right|\leq\mathbf{P}(|\hat{Z}_{N}-\hat{Z}|>0)=1-e^{-|\lambda_{N}-\lambda|}.

Hence, taking supremum over
B\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R})
, from (
E.6
), we can say that for any
N\geq 1
,

\displaystyle d_{TV}(Z_{N},Z)=\sup_{B\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R})}\bigg|\mathbf{P}\left(Z_{N}\in B\right)-\mathbf{P}(Z\in B)\bigg|\leq 1-e^{-|\lambda_{N}-\lambda|}.

Since,
Z_{N}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}Z
, as
N\rightarrow\infty
, thus
\lambda_{N}=\mathbf{E}(Z_{N})\to E(Z)=\lambda
. Hence, using the previous inequality, and letting
N\rightarrow\infty
, we can claim that
d_{TV}(Z_{N},Z)\to 0
. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma D.16
.

Let
H
be a simple, connected and undirected graph with vertex set

V(H)=\{1,\dots,R\}
. Then the size of minimum vertex cover of
H
,
\tau(H)=1
(cf. (
3.4
)) if and only if
H=\mathbb{K}_{1,R-1}
or
R
-star graph.

Proof.

Assume that
\tau(H)=1
and
\{v\}
is the minimum vertex cover of
H
. Then, every edge of
H
must have
v
as an endpoint. Thus, no edge connects two vertices in
V(H)\setminus\{v\}
. Since
H
is connected and has at least one edge, each vertex in
V(H)\setminus\{v\}
must be adjacent to
v
. Therefore,
H
consists of the center vertex
v
with all other vertices attached only to
v
,
i.e.
,
H
is a star graph.

Conversely, assume that
H
is a star graph with the center vertex
v
. Every edge of
H
is incident to
v
, so
\{v\}
is a vertex cover. Since the graph has at least one edge, the empty set cannot be a vertex cover. Thus, the minimum vertex cover set has size
1
. This completes the proof.
∎

Proposition D.17

(CLT for linear combination of estimated wedge and triangle density)
.

Suppose that Assumptions
(A.1)
,
(A.2)
,
(A.3)
and
(A.4)
hold. Let
Z\sim N(0,1)
. Fix a
(t_{1},t_{2})^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, define

\displaystyle T_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})

\displaystyle=\frac{t_{1}}{N^{3-2\beta}f_{l}(p_{N}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})+f_{l}(p_{N},\mathbb{K}_{1,2})S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{t_{2}}{N^{3-3\beta}f_{l}(p_{N}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{3})+f_{l}(p_{N},\mathbb{K}_{3})S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{3})\right),\hskip 8.50012pt\text{and}

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{Var}(T_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})),\hskip 8.50012pt\text{for $l=1,2$.}

(E.137)

Then

(a)

In the induced case (
l=1
), for
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{1,1}\cup R_{1,2}
, (see (
3.26
))

\displaystyle d_{1}\left(\frac{T_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})}{\sigma_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})},~Z\right)\to 0,~\text{as $N\to\infty$.}

(b)

In the ego-centric case (
l=2
), for
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,1}\cup R_{2,2}
(see (
3.26
)),

\displaystyle d_{1}\left(\frac{T_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})}{\sigma_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})},~Z\right)\to 0,~\text{as $N\to\infty$.}

Proof of Proposition
D.17
.

Fix a
(t_{1},t_{2})^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
.
For
l=1
, from (
E.137
), we can write,

\displaystyle T_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})=\frac{t_{1}}{N^{3-2\beta}p_{N}^{3}}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})+p_{N}^{3}S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{t_{2}}{N^{3-3\beta}p_{N}^{3}}\left(\widehat{S}_{N,l}(\mathbb{K}_{3})+p_{N}^{3}S_{N}(\mathbb{K}_{3})\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in[N]_{3}}\widetilde{W}_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}:\mathbb{K}_{3})\left(\frac{t_{1}}{N^{3(1-\alpha)-2\beta}}\mathbf{Y}_{N}(\mathbf{s}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})+\frac{t_{2}}{N^{3(1-\alpha)-3\beta}}\mathbf{Y}_{N}(\mathbf{s}:\mathbb{K}_{3})\right),

where
\widetilde{W}_{N,l}(\mathbf{s}:H)
for

and

are defined in (

In the above expression
\mathbf{E}(T_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3}))=0
.
Now, when

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\left(\sigma_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})\right)^{3}}\sum_{\mathbf{s}_{1}\in[N]_{3}}\sum_{\mathbf{s}_{2}\in N(\mathbf{s}_{1})}\sum_{\mathbf{s}_{3}\in N(\mathbf{s}_{1})}\left(\mathbf{E}\left|\prod_{i=1}^{3}\widetilde{W}_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{i}:\mathbb{K}_{3})\right|\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\mathbf{E}\left|\prod_{i=1}^{3}\left\{\frac{t_{1}}{N^{3(1-\alpha)-2\beta}}\mathbf{Y}_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{i}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})+\frac{t_{2}}{N^{3(1-\alpha)-3\beta}}\mathbf{Y}_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{i}:\mathbb{K}_{3})\right\}\right|+\mathbf{E}\left|\prod_{i=1}^{2}\widetilde{W}_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{i}:\mathbb{K}_{3})\right|\mathbf{E}\left|\widetilde{W}_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{3}:\mathbb{K}_{3})\right|\right.

\displaystyle\left.\times\mathbf{E}\left|\prod_{i=1}^{2}\left\{\frac{t_{1}}{N^{3(1-\alpha)-2\beta}}\mathbf{Y}_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{i}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})+\frac{t_{2}}{N^{3(1-\alpha)-3\beta}}\mathbf{Y}_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{i}:\mathbb{K}_{3})\right\}\right|\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\mathbf{E}\left|\left\{\frac{t_{1}}{N^{3(1-\alpha)-2\beta}}\mathbf{Y}_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{3}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})+\frac{t_{2}}{N^{3(1-\alpha)-3\beta}}\mathbf{Y}_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{3}:\mathbb{K}_{3})\right\}\right|\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\left(\sigma_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})\right)^{3}}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{3}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2},\mathbf{s}_{3})\in\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}}\left(\mathbf{E}\left|\prod_{i=1}^{3}\widetilde{W}_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{i}:\mathbb{K}_{3})\right|\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 17.00024pt{}\times\mathbf{E}\left|\prod_{i=1}^{3}\left\{\frac{t_{1}}{N^{3(1-\alpha)-2\beta}}\mathbf{Y}_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{i}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})+\frac{t_{2}}{N^{3(1-\alpha)-3\beta}}\mathbf{Y}_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{i}:\mathbb{K}_{3})\right\}\right|+\mathbf{E}\left|\prod_{i=1}^{2}\widetilde{W}_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{i}:\mathbb{K}_{3})\right|\mathbf{E}\left|\widetilde{W}_{N,1}(\mathbf{s}_{3}:\mathbb{K}_{3})\right|\right.

\displaystyle J_{N,2,1}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{r_{1}|t_{1}|^{3}}{N^{9-3\alpha-6\beta}}L_{N,2,1}+\frac{r_{2}t_{1}^{2}|t_{2}|}{N^{9-4\alpha-7\beta}}L_{N,2,2}+\frac{r_{3}t_{1}^{2}|t_{2}|}{N^{9-4\alpha-7\beta}}L_{N,2,3}+\frac{r_{4}t_{2}^{2}|t_{1}|}{N^{9-5\alpha-8\beta}}L_{N,2,4}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}\hskip 17.00024pt{}+\frac{r_{5}t_{2}^{2}|t_{1}|}{N^{9-5\alpha-8\beta}}L_{N,2,5}+\frac{r_{6}|t_{2}|^{3}}{N^{9-6\alpha-9\beta}}L_{N,2,6}\right),~\text{(say)}

(E.150)

where,
L_{N,2,i},i=1,\ldots,6
has the following expression,

\displaystyle L_{N,2,1}=\sum_{\star}\mathbf{E}\left|\prod_{i=1}^{3}\widetilde{W}_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{i}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\right|\cdot\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{6}Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{i}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\right),

\displaystyle L_{N,2,2}=\sum_{\star}\mathbf{E}\left|\widetilde{W}_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\widetilde{W}_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\widetilde{W}_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{3})\right|\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{3}:\mathbb{K}_{3})),

\displaystyle L_{N,2,3}=\sum_{\star}\mathbf{E}\left|\widetilde{W}_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{3})\widetilde{W}_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\widetilde{W}_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\right|\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{3})Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{3}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2}))

\displaystyle L_{N,2,4}=\sum_{\star}\mathbf{E}\left|\widetilde{W}_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{3})\widetilde{W}_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:\mathbb{K}_{3})\widetilde{W}_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\right|\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{3})Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:\mathbb{K}_{3})Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{3}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})),

\displaystyle L_{N,2,5}=\sum_{\star}\mathbf{E}\left|\widetilde{W}_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})\widetilde{W}_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:\mathbb{K}_{3})\widetilde{W}_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{3})\right|\mathbf{E}(Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{2}:\mathbb{K}_{3})Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{3}:\mathbb{K}_{3})),

\displaystyle L_{N,2,6}=\sum_{\star}\mathbf{E}\left|\prod_{i=1}^{3}\widetilde{W}_{N,2}(\mathbf{s}_{i}:\mathbb{K}_{3})\right|\cdot\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{6}Y_{N}(\mathbf{s}_{i}:\mathbb{K}_{3})\right).

(E.151)

In the above expression, we denote

\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{3}\sum_{(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2},\mathbf{s}_{3})\in\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}}\equiv\sum_{\star}.

In (
E.151
), the model components are the same as the induced case described in (
E.140
). From the proof of Theorem
3.1
and similar (
E.141
), there exists an
M_{6}
such that for all
N>M_{6}
, for
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,1}\cup R_{2,2}
, we can write,

\displaystyle L_{N,2,1}\leq c\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{6}N^{7-\alpha-6\beta},~\text{and}~L_{N,2,6}\leq c^{4}\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{9}N^{7-4\alpha-6\beta}.

(E.152)

In order to analyze the remaining terms in (
E.150
), recall, for any
k_{1},k_{2}\in\{1,2,3\}
, definition of the set
\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}
defined in (
E.9
). Consider any
(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2},\mathbf{s}_{3})\in\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}
. First, we define the following union graphs using graphs defined in (
E.142
), for a tuple of maps
\bm{\phi}=(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{2})^{T}
, that is,

\displaystyle Q(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3}:\bm{\phi})=H_{1}(\phi_{1})\cup H_{2}(\phi_{2})\cup G_{3}(\phi_{3}),\hskip 8.50012ptQ(\mathbb{K}_{3},\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{1,2}:\bm{\phi})=G_{1}(\phi_{1})\cup H_{2}(\phi_{2})\cup H_{3}(\phi_{3}),

\displaystyle Q(\mathbb{K}_{3},\mathbb{K}_{3},\mathbb{K}_{1,2}:\bm{\phi})=G_{1}(\phi_{1})\cup G_{2}(\phi_{2})\cup H_{3}(\phi_{3})~\text{and}~Q(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3},\mathbb{K}_{3}:\bm{\phi})=H_{1}(\phi_{1})\cup G_{2}(\phi_{2})\cup G_{3}(\phi_{3}).

(E.153)

From (
E.143
), we get a tuple of maps
\bm{\phi}^{\star}\equiv\bm{\phi}^{\star}_{k_{1},k_{2}}=(\phi^{\star}_{1},\phi^{\star}_{2},\phi^{\star}_{2})^{T}
for which the maximum value,
m^{\prime}(k_{1},\cdot,\cdot)+m^{\prime}(k_{2}:\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)
is obtained for a choice of
k_{1},k_{2}\in\{1,2,3\}
. Then define the size of the minimum vertex cover of the union graph as follows,

\displaystyle\tau\left(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3}\right)=\tau\left(Q(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3}:\bm{\phi}^{\star})\right),\hskip 8.50012pt\tau\left(\mathbb{K}_{3},\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{1,2}\right)=\tau\left(Q(\mathbb{K}_{3},\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{1,2}:\bm{\phi}^{\star})\right),

\displaystyle\tau\left(\mathbb{K}_{3},\mathbb{K}_{3},\mathbb{K}_{1,2}\right)=\tau\left(Q(\mathbb{K}_{3},\mathbb{K}_{3},\mathbb{K}_{1,2}:\bm{\phi}^{\star})\right),~\text{and}~\tau\left(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3},\mathbb{K}_{3}\right)=\tau\left(Q(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3},\mathbb{K}_{3}:\bm{\phi}^{\star})\right).

(E.154)

Then we define the following maps,

\displaystyle e_{2,1}(k_{1},k_{2}:\alpha,\beta)

\displaystyle=(9-k_{1}-k_{2})-\alpha\cdot\tau\left(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3}\right)-\beta\cdot(7-m(k_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})-m^{\prime}(k_{2}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})),

\displaystyle e_{2,3}(k_{1},k_{2}:\alpha,\beta)

\displaystyle=(9-k_{1}-k_{2})-\alpha\cdot\tau\left(\mathbb{K}_{3},\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{1,2}\right)-\beta\cdot(7-m^{\prime}(k_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})-m^{\prime}(k_{2}:\mathbb{K}_{3},\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{1,2})),

\displaystyle e_{2,4}(k_{1},k_{2}:\alpha,\beta)

\displaystyle=(9-k_{1}-k_{2})-\alpha\cdot\tau\left(\mathbb{K}_{3},\mathbb{K}_{3},\mathbb{K}_{1,2}\right)-\beta\cdot(7-m(k_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2})-m^{\prime}(k_{2}:\mathbb{K}_{3},\mathbb{K}_{3},\mathbb{K}_{1,2})),~\text{and}

\displaystyle e_{2,5}(k_{1},k_{2}:\alpha,\beta)

\displaystyle=(9-k_{1}-k_{2})-\alpha\cdot\tau\left(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3},\mathbb{K}_{3}\right)-\beta\cdot(7-m^{\prime}(k_{1}:\mathbb{K}_{3},\mathbb{K}_{1,2})-m^{\prime}(k_{2}:\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3},\mathbb{K}_{3})),

where
m(k_{1}:H)
is defined in (
3.2
). Now, there exists a
M_{7}
such that for all
N>M_{7}
, we can write

\displaystyle L_{N,2,2}\leq c^{2}\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{7}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{3}N^{e_{2,1}(k_{1},k_{2}:\alpha,\beta)},~~L_{N,1,3}\leq c^{2}\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{7}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{3}N^{e_{2,2}(k_{1},k_{2}:\alpha,\beta)},

\displaystyle L_{N,2,4}\leq c^{3}\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{8}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{3}N^{e_{2,3}(k_{1},k_{2}:\alpha,\beta)},\text{and}~L_{N,1,5}\leq c^{3}\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{8}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{3}N^{e_{2,4}(k_{1},k_{2}:\alpha,\beta)}.

(E.155)

The values of
e_{2,1}(k_{1},k_{2}:\alpha,\beta)
are listed in Table
Appendix E: Variance expressions for some common target subgraphs
, the values of
e_{2,2}(k_{1},k_{2}:\alpha,\beta)
are listed in Table
Appendix E: Variance expressions for some common target subgraphs
, the values of
e_{2,3}(k_{1},k_{2}:\alpha,\beta)
are listed in Table
Appendix E: Variance expressions for some common target subgraphs
and the values of
e_{2,4}(k_{1},k_{2}:\alpha,\beta)
listed in Table
Appendix E: Variance expressions for some common target subgraphs
for all possible choices of
k_{1},k_{2}\in\{1,2,3\}
. Using an argument similar to the one in the induced case, using the values of
e_{2,i}(k_{1},k_{2}:\alpha,\beta)
from the table mentioned above, we obtained a pair
(k_{1},k_{2})
from evaluating

\displaystyle(k_{1},k_{2})\in\argmaxA_{k_{1},k_{2}\in[3]}\{e_{2,i}(k_{1},k_{2}:\alpha,\beta)\},\hskip 8.50012pt\text{for $i=1,2,3,4$.}

From all tables, for
(\alpha,\beta)\in R_{2,1}\cup R_{2,2}
, we get
(k_{1},k_{2})=(1,1)
. Then, using the value
(k_{1},k_{2})=(1,1)
, from (

), we can write

\displaystyle L_{N,2,2}\leq c^{2}\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{7}N^{7-2\alpha-7\beta},\hskip 8.50012ptL_{N,2,3}\leq c^{2}\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{7}N^{7-2\alpha-7\beta}~\text{and,}~

(E.156)

\displaystyle L_{N,2,4}\leq c^{3}\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{8}N^{7-3\alpha-8\beta}~L_{N,2,5}\leq c^{3}\left(N^{\beta}\max_{i,j\in[K]}\pi_{N,i,j}\right)^{8}N^{7-3\alpha-8\beta}.

Therefore, using (
E.148
), (
E.152
) and (
E.156
), from (
E.150
), for all
N>\max\{M_{5},M_{6},M_{7}\}
, from (
E.150
), we can write

\displaystyle\frac{J_{N,2,1}}{\left(\sigma_{N,1}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})\right)^{3}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{N^{\frac{3}{2}(1-\alpha)}}{a_{2,t_{1},t_{2}}}\cdot O\left(\frac{1}{N^{2(1-\alpha)}}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{2}(1-\alpha)}}\right).

Using a similar argument, it can be shown that there exists an
M_{8}
such that for all
N>M_{8}
,
J_{N,2,2}/\left(\sigma_{N,2}(\mathbb{K}_{1,2},\mathbb{K}_{3})\right)^{3}\leq O(N^{-\frac{1}{2}(1-\alpha)})
(cf. (

)). Hence, for

, for all
